# Changelog

layerling started over at 1.0.0 when it was forked from SketchForge-3D 1.0.9.
Everything from 1.0.9 downwards is SketchForge's history, kept here because the
code still carries it - so a lower number further down is older, not newer.

## Unreleased

## 1.117.0

- **The first visit downloads about 10 MB less:** The offline copy left out the guide's pictures and its page for printing only for the German, English and Dutch guides. The 16 other languages' print pages (about 400 KB each) and the pictures of the Arabic guide went into the cache of every new visitor. `precachePaths` in `generate-service-worker.mjs` now leaves out `img/` and `print-all.html` in every language's folder.
- **Import, Export and Attachments can be moved:** Like the tool panels, the window under these three buttons is dragged by its title bar and docks again with a double click or when dropped near its place. Moved low down, its content scrolls, so the export button stays reachable. The question mark of the attachments jumps to their section of the guide.
- **The edge tool's preview is about a third quicker:** For a body of one solid, the CAD worker meshed it, wrote its BREP and collected its edges a second time for the component list, and the editor built the shape twice; it now reuses them. `collectEdges` works out face areas only when small treatment faces are left out, finds a flat face's normal once and each face's surface type once, and skips matching edges to solids when there is only one. Moving the pointer onto another edge swaps only the two hover lines instead of rebuilding every edge line (`setModifierEdgeHover`), and the tangent chain of the selected edges is one search over a grid of edge ends (`lib/cadEdgeChain.ts`). The flat-face check of fillets and the mesh-to-solid rebuild release the kernel handles they take, so many slider moves no longer pile them up. Importing a 3MF or OBJ of several parts no longer builds the whole file as one body just for its name. Measured with `tests/perf/cad-edge-tool.perf.ts`: a mesh dome of 1,150 edges previews in about 0.55 s instead of 0.9 s.
- **A STEP export no longer holds the editor either:** Writing a STEP file read every part back into the kernel on the page and wrote the file there, the editor standing for up to 1.7 s at a time at 390 parts. It is now the geometry worker's task `stepExport` (`exportShapesToStepOffThread`), which hands back the text; the editor's longest pause for the same design is 0.1 s and the whole export takes 3.7 s instead of 5.3 s. "Nothing STEP can carry" still comes back as `StepExportEmptyError`. `stepAssemblyImport.e2e.ts` reads the file written the worker's way and the one written on the page back in and compares their parts.
- **Large filleted bodies are quick to point at:** A body with edge treatments never got a picking tree, so every ray of the tape measure, of placing a shape on it or of choosing a face went through all its triangles: 0.4 ms at 10,000, 7 ms at 200,000, and a treated mesh may hold 400,000. From 20,000 triangles such a body now builds its tree the first time a ray is cast against it (`enableLazyMeshPicking` in `viewportShapeObjects.ts`), then a ray costs about 0.01 ms; smaller ones and bodies nobody points at pay nothing. `treatedBodyPicking.test.ts` checks the rays hit the same points.
- **A STEP file is read without holding the editor:** The CAD kernel read, meshed and wrote every part of a STEP file on the page, in one go: a file of 390 parts held the editor for 8.7 s in the browser, the kernel's loading included. It is now a task of the geometry worker (`importedShapesFromStepFile` in `stepFileImport.ts`, task `step`), which also prepares the parts' display; the page does not load the kernel for an import any more and stays usable, its longest pause 0.3 s for the same file. Where the worker cannot run, the page reads the file as before. `stepAssemblyImport.e2e.ts` compares the parts that come across from the worker with the ones read on the page.
- **Wrapping around a cylinder is several times quicker on large bodies:** The body was cut by up to 180 planes across the arc, each going over every triangle the cuts before had made. Each triangle is now cut on its own and only by the planes that lie across it, which gives the same triangles in the same order (`cutAcrossArc` in `cylinderWrap.ts`, compared with the old cut in `cylinderWrap.test.ts`). A plate of 120,000 triangles wraps in 0.28 s instead of 2.1 s.
- **The start page and the README name the newer tools:** Offset and Cut out, Surface texture and the Vase have their own entries in "What else layerling does" (German, English, Czech, Dutch, French, Russian, Slovenian) and in the README, which also names the paraboloid, the STEP import with every body in its colour, the position measured from the edges and the stencil bridges.

## 1.116.1

- **Opening a design from the server shows on its card (PR #976):** A large design takes a few seconds to download, and nothing on the start page showed the click had landed, so people clicked again. While it opens, the card dims its picture under a turning circle and its line reads "Opening server design …"; further clicks, its menu and dragging wait until it is open, and an error frees the card again. Contributed by @foofoodog.
- **Groups and bundles tell themselves apart in the Objects panel (PR #974):** A group or bundle shows the mark of the toolbar button that made it in front of its name, and the line under it reads "Group · 3 parts" or "Bundle · 3 parts", also after a rename (read from `groupOperation`). A bundle's open button says "Edit bundle". Contributed by @AbhiRoutray21.
- **A server folder for each user behind a proxy with sign-in, and downloads without filling the memory (PR #972):** Behind an authenticating reverse proxy (Authelia, oauth2-proxy, Cloudflare Access, Basic Auth and the like), setting `LAYERLING_USER_HEADER` to the header the proxy sends with the signed-in name gives every user their own store under `.users/<user>/`: designs, folders, exports and settings, in the Node route and in `store.php` alike. It is off unless set and reads only that one header; a request without it gets the common store. Only safe when layerling is reached through the proxy alone, which the README and the Docker guide say. Opening a design from the server now streams the file instead of reading it whole into memory (`createReadStream` in the Node route, `fpassthru` in `store.php`). Tests in `sharedProjectsAuthIsolation.test.ts`. Contributed by @foofoodog.
- **Position from the middle or from the edges (#973):** The position card has a switch **Measured from: Centre / Edge**, as the angle ruler measures from its corner or its middle. At **Edge**, X and Y show the body's left and front edges, taken from its real outline (also when it is turned), and a number typed there moves the body so that edge lands on it; Z stays its underside. The choice is kept in this browser with the cards (`useInspectorCard("positionAtEdge")` in `ShapeInspector.tsx`). A formula in X or Y is written for the middle, so at the edges the fields take plain numbers. Checked in the editor: a 40 x 20 mm box at X 10 shows -10 / -10 at its edges, typing 0 puts its middle at 20, turned by 90 degrees the edges follow its 20 mm side. Asked for by @willi1955.
- **Hole pattern: narrow rectangles and slots close together, the rows apart (#975):** The pattern had one spacing for both directions, so a hole 2 mm wide and 14 mm high with a 2 mm wall stood 16 mm from its neighbour in the row as well. A rectangle or slot now has its own **Rows centre to centre** (`rowSpacing` in `holePattern.ts`): the spacing counts along the row and only has to clear the hole's width, the row spacing clears its height, and **Wall between the holes** sets both (`holePatternMinRowSpacing`). A staggered pattern also keeps two rows up clear. A pattern saved without a row spacing keeps its rows where its spacing put them. `layerling_perforate_face` takes `rowSpacing` too. Checked in the editor: 2 x 14 mm with a 2 mm wall gives 4 mm and 16 mm, 19 holes on a 46 mm face. Reported by @willi1955.

- **STEP files with several solids: every body and its colour (#830):** A file can hold several bodies under one entry of its tree, the screws of a part say, and HOOPS Exchange writes them as surface models; such an entry came in as one part, so a stand of 390 bodies showed 85. Each solid, or each shell where an entry has no solid, is now a part of its own, numbered after the entry (`bodiesOf` in `stepAssemblyImport.ts`). The colours the file gives its bodies (a `STYLED_ITEM` on the body) come along too: the kernel's XCAF document leaves them out, so `stepBodyColors` in `stepColors.ts` follows them in the file's text from the product through its representations to the bodies, in their order. Colours on single faces are still not read. Tried with @ValSimDim's aquarium stand (390 parts in 5 s, each in its colour) and in `stepColors.test.ts` and `stepAssemblyImport.e2e.ts`.
- **A STEP export carries the colours as they show, no longer paler:** The kernel takes a colour as linear light and writes it to the file as sRGB, so a part in #d12a2a came out as #ea7171 in another CAD program. `exportShapesToStep` now hands each colour over as linear light, unrounded, through the kernel's own XCAF document (`stepColorForKernel`; brepjs's `exportAssemblySTEP` takes only whole hex steps, which would shift dark colours by up to 6 steps). A part exported and read back keeps its colour exactly (`stepAssemblyImport.e2e.ts`).
- **A design restored from a backup into the server store keeps its picture:** With new designs on the server, as in the Docker version, a restored design was uploaded before its picture from the backup reached it, so the server's card stayed blank until the design was next saved from the editor. The picture now goes up with the design. Reported by Jürgen Keller by mail.

## 1.116.0

- **Tape measure, clearer labels and better rendering (PR #961):** Each label starts with the axes the measurement runs along (`X`, `Y`, `Z`, `XY`, `XZ`, `YZ` or `3D`). Hovering a label shows the reach along each axis (ΔX, ΔY, ΔZ), hovering a point shows its X, Y and Z, and while the second point is placed a tip beside the pointer shows axes, length and reach. Labels sit beside their line instead of on it and step aside for other lines, points and labels. Every line has an outline, light orange in the dark and graphite themes. A measurement mostly behind another body fades, and hovering it brings it back; the body both ends sit on, holes, see-through bodies and what the section view cuts away do not count. The midpoint mark is the size of a point, where the reference point will go. The guide's chapter 10 is updated in all languages. Contributed by @gogades.
- **Japanese: the latest 42 sentences, and "Bring into design" reads as what it does (PR #970, PR #971):** The sentences of the recent releases are translated, and the history view's button now says 今のデザインに追加, "add to the current design", instead of a wording that read like rolling the design back. Contributed by @yokobond.
- **Danish and Brazilian Portuguese (PR #960, PR #966):** Danish gets the last new sentence of 1.115.0, two added sentences and an order in chapter 12 that had fallen out of step; chapter 15 of the guide (installing as an app, offline, self-hosting) is now in Brazilian Portuguese. Contributed by @Henrikth12 and @lmagalhaes.
- **A bent tube's opening is centred again when it cuts as a hole (#892):** With **Bore cuts as a hole when grouped**, the opening of a bent tube sat one wall too low after grouping and cut the bottom of the pipe open, so a T-junction leaked at the bottom. `splitBentTubeBore` built the opening with `bentTubeParameterPatch`, which keeps the underside where it was (right when the size is changed in the panel); the opening now sits in the middle of the pipe's box, lying, standing or tipped. E2e test in `tubeBoreHole.e2e.ts`: the opening's box shares its middle with the pipe's, and the bottom of the wall is still there after grouping. Reported by @WimSnor.
- **A field turned with the mouse wheel stays under the pointer:** Turning **Rotation X** or **Y** away from 0 brings up the hint that the body stands tilted, above the rotation fields. The field moved away from under the pointer, and the next turn of the wheel scrolled the panel instead of changing the value. While the wheel turns, the panel now scrolls along by as much as the field moved, and until the wheel rests its turns stay with the field that was clicked. Reported by StonXX in the drucktipps3d forum.
- **Saving to the server store no longer reads the whole design into memory (#588):** `store.php` read every upload into a string, wrote it to a probe file to check it was a .lyl and then wrote it again into the store. With a surface texture a design runs to hundreds of MB: slow on a small server, and past PHP's `memory_limit` the save failed. The upload is now streamed to a temporary file, checked there and moved into place; saved exports the same way. Tested with a 200 MB design and a 100 MB STL at `memory_limit=64M`. Reported by @foofoodog.
- **The backup notice on the start page reads more plainly (#968):** "These designs are stored only in this browser. A crash or browser update can erase them. Use “Back up all” above to save them all in one file." Suggested by @rondlh.

## 1.115.0

- **A STEP file with several solids comes in as one body each, under its own name (#830):** A STEP file from another CAD program, an assembly of posts, rails and panels say, was read as one body: the solids were welded into one mesh, their names were gone, and "Separate parts" then found only the pieces that do not touch (8 instead of 23 for a stand whose parts all touch). The kernel now reads the file as an XCAF document, which carries the solids, their names, the tree of groups and the placement of each instance (`importedShapesFromStepAssembly` in `stepAssemblyImport.ts`, `importXCAFFromSTEP` of occt-wasm). Every solid comes in as a body of its own under the name the file gives it, in its place to the others, selected together, with its exact body kept so a STEP export hands it back exactly; a message says how many came in. A file with one solid, with more than 400 solids, or one the kernel cannot take apart comes in as one body as before. Not yet: the colours of the file (the kernel's reader does not hand them over; the parts take layerling's alternating colours) and the groups of its tree (the parts lie side by side). Tried with a 23-solid stand (23 bodies in 3 s, together 2400 x 1000 x 650 mm as the file) and in `stepAssemblyImport.e2e.ts` against the real kernel. Asked for by @ValSimDim.
- **A bent tube's opening can cut as a hole (#892):** The switch **Bore cuts as a hole when grouped**, which tube and ring got in 1.113.0, is now also in the properties of a bent tube that has an opening. Grouped, such a pipe is cut as the pipe without its opening and the opening as a bent tube of its own that is a hole - same path, same start, the opening's profile as its outside (`splitBentTubeBore` in `editorBooleans.ts`, built with `bentTubeParameterPatch`, so a pipe stretched by its handles keeps the stretch in both parts) - so the opening also cuts whatever the pipe is placed against or through. Without the switch nothing changes, and a bent tube without an opening has none to cut. E2e test in `tubeBoreHole.e2e.ts` against the real kernel: a wall across a straight bent tube stays in its opening without the switch and is opened with it, and the hole's outside equals the opening for every pairing of round, square, hexagon and octagon. The guide (English and German) and the AI bridge's description say so. Asked for by @WimSnor.
- **"Keep proportions" for a round tube too:** The switch was missing in the properties of a round tube and appeared only once "Taper" had made it oval, because the round tube shows its outer diameter instead of width and length. It is there now, and typing the outer diameter or the height takes the other measures along.
- **Start page: clearer layout, the same controls for browser and server storage (PR #957):** The welcome guide comes first and can be closed with an X, which the browser remembers; a "Welcome to layerling" link in the footer brings it back. The three big buttons are always shown, and "Continue workplane" carries a thumbnail of the latest design. The designs sit under one heading with **Server** and **Browser** tabs, each with its count, the one with more designs first; with a single storage there are no tabs. Both tabs have the same toolbar (New folder, Select, Back up all, Restore backup, sort and view), and on the server tab Create and Restore go into the open server folder. **Back up all** on the server saves every design in its folders, the empty folders and the server's custom shapes as one ZIP, and Restore reads it back: the Node route and `store.php` each answer a new `?all=1`, and a server that cannot list everything stops the backup with an error instead of saving only part. The orange storage notice became a framed orange card over the browser's designs, shown only when it holds some, since designs kept only in the browser are lost with a crash or an update and a beginner may not know that. The "Custom shapes" server folder has its own icon, always comes first and cannot be renamed or deleted, which both servers refuse too. The dashboard styles moved out of `globals.css` into `dashboard.css`. Contributed by @gogades.
- **Danish: the 1.113.0 texts, the new font and hole pattern sentences, and 19 corrections (PR #947, PR #948, PR #949, PR #951, PR #956):** The 546 keys of 1.113.0 and the new guide passages are translated, chapter 8 gains the font list and the eight built-in fonts, and the three keys of 1.114.0 (the dialog that allows more holes) are in. "Mesh" replaces "net" in the Danish text as the rest of the app and the guide say it, and a review of the guide and the catalogue mends 19 passages (holes, "mesh", "own shape"). Contributed by @Henrikth12.
- **Brazilian Portuguese: the 1.112.0 and 1.113.0 sentences and chapter 5 of the guide (PR #958, PR #959):** The 41 sentences of the selection on the start page, the hull, attachments, languages, Cut out, the paraboloid and the stencil bridges are translated, six sentences whose English was reworded are brought up to date, and the new chapter 5 paragraphs (grouping in the background, the bundle's cards, Hull, closing openings in imported meshes) follow. The Cut out section now uses "sólido" and "grupo de união" like the rest, so its heading and the anchor behind the `?` buttons changed to `recortando-um-solido-de-outro`; an old link to the previous anchor opens the chapter at its top. The language stays marked as incomplete until two people have read it. Contributed by @lmagalhaes.
- **The sentence for a STEP file with several solids is in French, Slovenian and Russian too (#830):** `status.importStepParts` was missing in the three languages marked as complete, which failed the catalogue tests.

## 1.114.0

- **Surface texture: a relief on any body (PR #930):** A new card in a shape's settings, and a button of that name in the **Modify** group, lays a relief onto any body, for grip or for looks. Twelve patterns: grooves, knurling, honeycomb, bumps, cells (Voronoi), knitting, rope, basket weave, grain, an image used as a height map, cracks (kintsugi, with gold) and text stuck on. It is laid around the axis of round bodies, from all round for angular ones, or flat along an axis, and the mesh is cut along the pattern so that a pointed profile comes out exactly. Under **More** are the width and profile, slant, which walls, how far bottom and top stay smooth, a band of the height and the fade-in; it can be changed or removed later. Contributed by @marcus040473.
- **Vase: a shape of its own for vases, pots and lampshades (PR #932):** A new **Vase** in the shape library with its height and widest diameter as its own measures and everything else on cards that stay editable after saving: body form, cross-section (circle, polygon, wave or drawn) with an optional second one it blends into, twist and sway, solid for the slicer's vase mode or hollow, patterns (grooves, stripes, Voronoi cells, facets), drain holes, a hollow foot, an open wall, a lampshade, legs, a stand, a saucer, inserts and lids, and a watertight check. Contributed by @marcus040473.
- **Resize handles keep the box's own axes when the workplane stands on a side face (#933):** With the workplane on a side face of a box, dragging a handle wrote the new size to the wrong dimension while moving the part along the dragged side. A shape's handles write to its own width, depth and height, so a workplane that stands across its up axis no longer gives them its axes (`useWorkplane` in `viewportShapeObjects.ts`). Reported by @PingPongLee.
- **A mesh with many triangles and the hole pattern ask instead of refusing (#941):** The edge tool refused a mesh above the triangle limit (`CAD_MODIFIER_PREPARE_TRIANGLE_LIMIT`), and the hole pattern refused more than 400 holes. Both limits are where the work turns slow, not walls: whoever knows their machine can now go on after a clear warning that it may take many minutes, that the page can freeze and that unsaved work is lost, so the design should be saved first. For the edge tool a thread stays refused, since it has a better way. In the hole pattern the confirmed switch **Allow more holes** raises the limit to 3000 holes (`HOLE_PATTERN_MANY_HOLES`, `allowMany`). Texts in English, German, Czech, French, Dutch, Russian and Slovenian; unit test in `holePattern.test.ts`.
- **Tape measure: the crosshair replaces the hand while a point is dragged (#943):** The grabbing hand hid the very point the tape measure's end was being moved to. While a point is dragged the cursor is now the crosshair, which shows where it lands.
- **"Make default" is kept on the server too:** The settings saved with "Make default" lived only in the browser's storage, so after clearing it, or in another browser, a new design started with the built-in settings again, also in the Docker version with its projects folder. Where the installation has a server store (`LAYERLING_SHARED_PROJECTS_DIR` or the `store` folder of `store.php`), the default now also goes there, as `.settings/default.json` in a hidden folder the start page does not list (`?settings=default` on both, GET and POST, a JSON object of at most 256 KB, same-origin writes only). The start page fetches it when it loads and makes it the browser's default, so the last one saved in any browser wins; a browser whose default predates this sends its own up when the store has none yet. A new design waits for that answer as it waits for the server's other answers (`serverWorkspaceDefault.ts`, `serverAnswerGate` in `page.tsx`). Without a store nothing changes. Unit tests for the Node route and the sync; tried in the editor with a store: a cleared browser got the default back, a new design started with it, and "Make default" sent a change to the store. `store.php` tried with PHP's built-in server. Asked for by a Docker user by mail.
- **A block with a threaded hole can be filleted (#938):** A 40 x 20 x 20 block grouped with an M6 threaded hole through it could not be rounded or chamfered: "The grouped body could not be repaired into valid geometry". The cut itself was valid, but merging its faces afterwards (`simplify`, `unifySameDomain`) broke it when the hole's chamfer was as long as its pitch, which is what a new threaded hole has (1 mm chamfer, 1 mm pitch); a chamfer of 0.9 or 1.05 mm was fine. The merge now keeps the cut as it was when it would make the body invalid (`tidiedIfValid` in `cadFuzzyCut.ts`). A kernel test (`threadHoleInGroup.e2e.ts`) checks chamfers of 1, 0.9, 1.05 and 0.5 mm; with the user's own file the group lists its edges and takes a fillet. Reported by @kenehlert1959 on GitHub.
- **The "f" of Rounded has no spur on its crossbar (#916):** The outline of the "f" in the Rounded typeface (optimer) leaves the end of its crossbar for a point 4 units above it and comes back, so it touches itself; at 40 mm wide a triangle of about 0.1 mm hung there in the silhouette and the footprint DXF, and a CAM program may treat a self-touching outline differently. `withGlyphRepairs` in `textFonts.ts` mends that glyph where the typeface is loaded: the curve ends at the crossbar's own edge, a change of three thousandths of the letter height. Reported by @plazmabokor.
- **Japanese: 216 sentences that were missing since 1.97 (PR #936):** The Japanese interface gains attachments; Gridfinity (openings, compartments and dividers, label shelves, finger scoops, half-size grid, wall thickness, floor height, baseplate padding, corner radius, drawer size); chamfillet and the variable radius of the edge tool; the workspace switches; dashboard folders; DXF import and export; the key ring tab of name tags; a line from the middle and the sweep anchor in the sketch; and the history view's open group. The wording follows the rest of the translation (short sentences, plain words for children and their teachers); a key ring is now キーホルダー, which also changes two existing sentences. Japanese stands at 3092 of 3134 sentences. Contributed by @yokobond.
- **Japanese: the start page button says "continue the design" (PR #935):** The button that opens the last design said 作業平面を続ける, "continue the workplane", which read as if it carried on with the plate the parts stand on. It now says デザインを続ける, with デザイン as everywhere else in the Japanese interface. Contributed by @yokobond.
- **store.php names the real reason an upload fails (#588):** A project that PHP refused for its size was reported as "did not arrive completely", whatever the reason. It now says which limit it ran into and what the setting stands at (`upload_max_filesize`, `post_max_size`), and gives the other upload errors in words (partial upload, no temporary folder, disk not writable). A too large upload answers with 413, not 400. The README says how to raise the limits, also for the Docker image. Asked for by @foofoodog.
- **A corner radius for Gridfinity bins (#856):** A bin's outer corners were always rounded by 3.75 mm, as the specification draws them, so where four bins meet in a drawer they left a hole about 3.8 mm across. A bin now has **Corner radius** under **Magnet holes**, 3.75 mm to begin with so existing bins stay as they are, down to 0 for square corners, which leave only the 0.5 mm gaps between the bins. Only the outer wall takes it, from 0.5 mm over the feet up: the feet, the inside and the slopes of the lip a stacked bin rests on keep their rounding, so the bin sits in a baseplate and stacks as before; even on a one-unit bin, which sits down on the slopes of its socket, the square corners start 0.25 mm over the plate's top. Above a square corner the wall ends flat at the lip's top (the bin's top without one), or at the foot of the lip's chamfer, which keeps its rounding. A radius within 0.05 mm of 3.75 is built as 3.75; a baseplate and a tray keep the setting and ignore it, and a bin whose open sides leave every corner square has no row. The view, the exports and the exact body agree. `layerling_create_shape`, `layerling_update_object` and `layerling_read_scene` take and report `gridfinityBinCornerRadius`. Asked for by @TinkerBarn in #856. Contributed by @mlugo-apx.
- **Brazilian Portuguese: chapter 1 of the guide brought up to date (PR #931):** Two new sentences about pasting (and the setting that puts pasted parts in the middle of the workplane), the link text of the Tinkercad tip that was still in English, and "barra de opções" and "projetos" as in the rest of the translation. Contributed by @lmagalhaes.
- **Brazilian Portuguese: 43 sentences from 1.111.0 (PR #929):** The interface in Brazilian Portuguese (`pt-br`) gains the sentences it was missing since 1.111.0 - the attachments panel, "Colar no meio e baixar", "Luz suave", the calculation status lines, "Agrupar cópias" and the Alt+arrow shortcut - and five welcome sentences and two sketch shortcuts are brought up to date with the English. The language stays marked as incomplete until two people have read it. Contributed by @lmagalhaes.
- **STEP export keeps the planes and cylinders of edited bodies:** A body with a fillet, chamfer or hollow, or a joined group, goes into the STEP file as the exact body stored with it. That body was placed with brepjs' `applyMatrix`, whose occt-wasm adapter always takes the general transform, even for a body that was only moved or turned. That turned every face into a B-spline: CAD programs got no planes or cylinders to sketch on or measure, files grew (a 1550-face enclosure came to 5.4 MB), and the kernel's offset failed on such a body or ran for minutes. Stored bodies are now placed as the CAD worker places them (`applyCadTransform`, moved from `cadModifier.worker.ts` to `cadModifierRuntime.ts`): a move, a turn or an even scale stays rigid, and only a stretched body takes the general transform. Profile, thread, spring, gear and Gridfinity bodies use the same helper, so a rigid placement the kernel refuses now falls back as it does in the worker. E2e test in `stepRoundTrip.e2e.ts` (a moved and turned edited body comes back as planes and a cylinder; a stretched one still exports with the right volume). Found while testing a 3D offset on exported designs.

## 1.113.0

- **A paraboloid shape (#922):** A new shape **Paraboloid** next to the half sphere: a dome whose height falls with the square of the distance from the axis, with a rounded tip and an oval footprint when width and depth differ. One property, the number of steps. It is a mesh body, not an exact CAD profile; it is in the shape catalogue, the command search and the AI bridge like the other shapes.
- **A font preview in the font list:** The font of a text and of a name tag was a plain select that could only list names. It is now a list (`FontPicker.tsx`) that draws every font in its own letters, with the words of the text, so a font of one's own shows as it will come out, installed in the browser or not; the closed field shows the chosen font the same way. Arrow keys, Home, End, Enter, Escape and the first letter of a name work in it. The window of own fonts shows each font's name in it, and the fonts listed from the computer show theirs in their own letters through the browser. The outlines come from the typefaces the text is built from (`fontPreview.ts`, cached per font, text and size); the Japanese face is not fetched for a preview, it shows once a text has loaded it. The list of fonts from the computer showed only the first 150 and then stopped, whatever the search; it now grows as it is scrolled to its end. Not tried: the list of fonts from the computer (it needs the browser's permission).
- **Video list:** "I Tried Layerling After Tinkercad… Is It REALLY Better?" by Maker Mates Studio 3D Printing is new at the top: somebody coming from Tinkercad tries layerling and compares the two. In the start page list, the guide in every language, the READMEs and the first post of Discussion #296.
- **Cut one body out of another, with a clearance (#927):** A new tool **Cut out** in the Combine area (also in the right-click menu of two selected solids, in the command search and through the MCP as `layerling_cut_out`) subtracts a copy of a part from another body and leaves the part where it is: no more copying the part, making the copy a hole, aligning it and grouping. The smaller body is taken as the part, a button swaps them. The clearance (0 to 10 mm) makes the cavity larger on every side; it is built with the Offset tool and so needs an exact body. The result is a group, ungroup takes it apart. Asked for by @raradek.
- **A filleted hole flush with a wall cuts it cleanly (#928):** A grouped hole is widened by a thousandth of a millimetre at the sides (and 0.05 mm at the ends), so one aligned flush with a wall goes through it. A hole with a fillet or chamfer is a mesh, and meshes were left out of that widening since a strip set to hole lost a wall to it (#607); so a filleted hole flush with a side wall, a hair off it after float rounding, left a skin of no thickness there, which showed as a face and could not be cut. A hole mesh that bulges nowhere inwards, such as a box with a fillet or chamfer or a cylinder, is widened again, because scaled round its middle every wall moves outwards (`meshIsConvex` in `meshConvexity.ts`, up to 5,000 triangles); other meshes stay as they were. Tests in `meshConvexity.test.ts` and `flushConvexHole.e2e.ts`; tried in the editor with the reported file: the group made again has no skin. Reported by @MethylBromide.
- **The ruler carries millimetres and inches at once (#913):** The printed scale of the ruler followed the unit switch and showed one scale. A ruler 15 mm wide or more now has both: the scale of the unit you work in along the top edge and the other along the bottom edge, so a quick conversion needs no switch. A narrower ruler keeps its single scale. Only the marks on the top change, the body is the same box. Asked for by @maxwellsauber.
- **European Portuguese: 715 more sentences (PR #923):** The interface in "Português de Portugal" (`pt-pt`) gains 715 translations - tools, sketch help, keyboard shortcuts, status messages, history, patterns, import and export - and has 1401 of the 3115 sentences now. The language stays marked as incomplete, so what is missing shows in English. Contributed by @jcpcustodio.
- **Danish: the 1.112.0 texts and three corrections (PR #924, PR #925):** The 22 new keys of 1.112.0 are translated (the selection on the start page, the stencil bridges and the welcome cards), the guide passages on the selection, the hull and the files follow, and three passages of chapter 3 are corrected: Multicolor for a group or a bundle, Keep proportions with Shift+Enter, and one wrong sentence found in the code. The Danish catalogue is complete again, 3115 of 3115 sentences. Contributed by @Henrikth12.
- **Shift while dragging keeps its axis (#909):** Dragging a body with Shift held keeps the move on one axis of the workplane, but the axis was chosen anew with every mouse move, so close to the start of the drag, where the pointer had gone about as far along both, it jumped between the two and the part jerked diagonally. The axis is now chosen once, when the pointer has moved 10 pixels, and kept until Shift is let go (`latchedDragAxis` in `dragAxisLatch.ts`, used by the drag in `WorkplaneViewport.tsx`); until then the part waits. Unit test in `dragAxisLatch.test.ts`. Reported by @MartinShake.
- **Offset: every face of a body moved by one distance (#919):** A new tool **Offset** next to Hollow (toolbar, right-click menu, command search, MCP `layerling_offset_object`) grows or shrinks one exact body along its faces by 0.01 to 10 mm, with rounded or sharp outer corners (`offsetSolid` in `cadShell.ts`, the kernel's offset that Hollow already uses inward). That is the clearance of a press fit or a snap joint: scaling moves the features, Hollow only goes inward. With **Keep the original and add the offset body as a hole** the part stays as it is and a copy, grown by the distance, is added as a hole to cut the mating part with. Without it the body itself is offset and becomes a fixed exact body, like after a chamfer: the list in the panel takes the offset back and Edit opens the tool again with its distance (history entry kind `offset`, saved in the `.lyl`). Holes, bundles and bodies that are only a triangle mesh are refused with a message. E2e test in `cadOffset.e2e.ts` against the real kernel: a box grown by 0.2 mm is 40.4 x 30.4 x 20.4 with sharp corners and smaller with round ones, a shrink gives the inner box, a plate with a hole grows, and a shrink beyond the body is refused. Texts in all 18 languages; guide (Edges and hollowing, 15 languages) and the AI chapter's tool list say so. Asked for by @davidsillou.
- **A tube's bore can cut as a hole (#892):** A tube or ring has a new switch **Bore cuts as a hole when grouped** (`tubeBoreHole`, also an MCP property). Grouped, such a tube is cut as its outer cylinder and a bore hole (`splitBoreTube` in `editorBooleans.ts`), so the bore also opens the wall of whatever it is grouped with: two tubes in a T-junction open into each other, which the bore of a plain tube never does. The tube itself stays one shape, and without the switch nothing changes. E2e test in `tubeBoreHole.e2e.ts`: a thin rod through the bore of the lying tube meets the wall of the standing one without the switch and meets nothing with it. Reported by @WimSnor.
- **Text with a "#" no longer stops the silhouette export or loses counters (#916):** The font draws the "#" (in Multilanguage, Sans, Serif, Script, Monospace and Stencil) with outlines that touch or cross themselves, and the plain text mesh built from them was refused by the 3D kernel as "Not manifold": the silhouette export of a text such as "A#B" stopped with that message, and grouped as a hole with a plate the counters of the letters beside it were lost. A text the kernel refuses, or whose letters overlap, is now built from the union of its letters, extruded by the 2D kernel (`textJoinedGeometry`, `geometryIsSolid` in `textFill.ts`); a text that is fine stays as it was, and so do curved text and text with a bevel. E2e test in `textOverlapManifold.e2e.ts`: "A#B" in every font is a solid, it is exported as a silhouette, grouped as a hole in a plate it leaves the four counters of A, "#" and B as islands, and the footprint outlines are closed (it failed in eight places before). Not reproduced: the report's "f" of Rounded in the footprint export, which stays whole here with and without this change, and a bevelled "#", which the region cannot carry. Reported by @plazmabokor.
- **Align and Mirror no longer move a turned body by a hair (#917):** Align and Mirror rounded the new position of a body to four decimals. For a body that stands square to the axes that only cleans a sum like 0.1 + 0.2, but the exact place of a turned body is a long number, and rounding it moved the body by up to 0.00005 mm off the one it was aligned to. Two boxes aligned in a corner on a turned workplane then stood a hair apart, and the CAD kernel could not fillet, chamfer or hollow the group they made ("The selected edges cannot be filleted together at this size"). A position is now rounded only when it already is a four-decimal number up to floating-point noise (`roundedAlignedValue` in `alignRounding.ts`, used by `alignedShapesForSelection` and `mirroredShapePatch`). Unit test in `alignRounding.test.ts`; tried in the editor with the steps of the report (two boxes in an L, grouped, turned by 10 degrees, workplane on a side, ungrouped, aligned again in the opposite corner, regrouped): before, the fillet of all edges failed; now it goes through with 18 edges. Reported by @PingPongLee.
- **Stencil bridges for parts that would fall out (DXF and SVG, PR #915):** When a plate with text is laser cut, the middle of letters like o, a and b falls out. The export window of the DXF, and of the SVG with the top view, has an optional box **Bridges for parts that would fall out** with a **Bridge width** (0.5 to 3 mm, 1 mm to begin with) and **Bridges per part** (1 to 4, 2 to begin with): small bridges, as on a stencil, hold such a part to the material round it (`stencilBridges.ts`, joined into the section by `stencilBridgeSection.ts`). A bridge never touches another outline or another bridge, never leaves a gap too narrow to cut, and reaches less deep into a thin wall so the hole behind it stays closed; parts that would only hold each other are tied to the plate too, and a part no bridge can reach at the chosen width is counted in the message after the export. Nothing changes unless the box is ticked. Texts in English, German, Czech, French, Hungarian, Dutch, Russian and Slovenian; the guide (Files and saving, English and German) says so. Unit tests in `stencilBridges.test.ts`; 20 designs (text in seven fonts, QR codes, gears, nested rings, thin walls) in 12 export settings, every file checked with an independent tool and every DXF imported back. Contributed by @plazmabokor.
- **Cone hinge: size of the cones, a thinner wall, a flush leaf, and the style as one choice (#907, #897):** Four changes after the first reports of 1.112.0. A new field **Cone diameter** sets how big the cones are where they grow from their knuckle (`hingeConeSize`; left out, the largest that fits, which is bigger than before: the recess keeps a wall of 0.6 mm round it in the knuckle, and the two recesses of a knuckle keep 0.4 mm between them instead of 1 mm). In the cone style the leaf may be as thick as the whole knuckle, flush with it, because there is no bore for it to stay below; a leaf thicker than the radius ends the clearance away from the knuckle's side, level with the axis (`leafReach` in `hingeGeometry.ts`), so a flush hinge opens about 90 degrees. The **Style** is now one choice of "With pin", "Without pin" and "With cones": the separate switch is gone, and a design saved with the old switch (`hingePinless`) is read as "Without pin" (`normalizeHingeStyle`). The MCP takes `hingeStyle` "pinless" and `hingeConeSize`. Texts in every language, the guide (Threads and mechanics, every language) says so. Unit tests in `hingeGeometry.test.ts` (thick leaves close, the clearance between the two parts in every section, the cone diameter, the style choice); not tried: a print, and the field in the editor. Asked for by @MethylBromide and @NorthHighlander101.
- **DXF: import and export fixes from a real laser job (#774, PR #914):** Found while cutting a test plate: a DXF with very short steps, such as the 0.0078 mm steps of a QR code, failed on import with "makeLineEdge: construction failed", and pieces that touch at a corner could be joined to the wrong one, which showed as "invalid sketch topology" when an exported DXF was imported back. Tiny pieces are now handled, pieces are joined at the nearest end, and a run of pieces that closes on itself with no size is dropped (`ringExtent`). On export, no arc is bent through tiny sharp zigzags any more, since it could cross the outline beside it (`sharpTurn`, 40 degrees at most), and no side shorter than 0.001 mm is written, as some cutting programs choke on them. In the footprint view of the DXF and SVG export, a loose hole in a body lying inside another body was left out: the colour separation now knows which bodies were cut. Tried with 20 designs in 12 export settings, every file checked with an independent tool and every DXF imported back. Unit tests in `dxfExport.test.ts` and `dxfImport.test.ts`. Contributed by @plazmabokor.

## 1.112.0

- **Hinge: a knuckle width instead of a count, and a hinge with cones (#907):** Two additions for the print-in-place hinge. **Knuckle width (max)**: the knuckles are at most that wide and their number follows from the length (the fewest odd count that is not wider, from 3 up to 31; `hingeKnuckleWidth`, `hingeKnucklesForWidth` in `hingeGeometry.ts`); typing a number of knuckles takes the width away again, and 0 leaves it to the count. **Style "With cones"** (`hingeStyle: "cone"`): every other knuckle ends in a cone that nests into a conical recess of the knuckle beside it, 45 degrees, with the clearance all round. There is no pin and no long bridge that could sag, so it prints lying flat; the pin fields are hidden for it. It is a mesh (no chamfers or fillets, and a mesh in STEP, `hingeHasExactParts`), and it makes fewer knuckles where they would be too short to carry a cone. Both are on the MCP (`hingeKnuckleWidth`, `hingeStyle` in `layerling_create_shape` and `layerling_update_object`), texts in every language, the guide (Threads and mechanics, every language) says so. Unit tests in `hingeGeometry.test.ts` (counts from a width, closed and consistently wound bodies in the cone style, the wall between the recesses, fewer knuckles when short); the axis section of the cone hinge looked right. Not tried: a print. Asked for by @MethylBromide.
- **Align handles close to the body, and a body's own name for its export:** The line of alignment handles (L) stood 16 % of the selection's largest side beside it, at least 8 mm, so 15 mm and more away from a larger body; it now stands 3 mm beside it (`ALIGN_HANDLE_OFFSET` in `viewportTransformOverlays.ts`), and handles that land on one spot are still pushed apart on the screen. The export window started with the design's name even when one body was exported; when exactly one body is exported and somebody gave it a name, that name is suggested now, while a design file (LYL) keeps the design's name and a name typed in the field stays (`hasOwnShapeName` in `shapeCatalog.ts`, which leaves out the names the editor hands out such as Box, Group or Hull). A hull now shows its name in the interface language, as a group does. Unit tests in `displayShapeName.test.ts`. Suggested in the drucktipps3d forum.
- **Choose several designs on the start page and delete them together (#886):** Every design had to be deleted by its own menu, one question each, which is slow when many old designs have piled up. The start page now has a **Select** button above the designs (in the server folder too, beside Refresh): with it on, a click on a card chooses it, shown by a tick and an orange frame, instead of opening it; the bar above the designs says how many are chosen and has **Select all** (every design the list shows), **Select none**, **Delete** and **Done**; Esc leaves the selection too. Delete asks once for the whole choice ("Do you actually want the 3 selected designs to be deleted?"; for one design it is the old question with its name) and then deletes them, the designs of this browser at once, those of the server folder one after the other, as each needs its own revision, and says how many went. Designs that are moved, deleted or filtered away leave the choice by themselves, and a change of folder or list ends it. Folders cannot be chosen; deleting a folder still moves its designs up. The pure rules are in `designSelection.ts` (unit tests in `designSelection.test.ts`), the deleting of several designs in `deleteProjects` of `page.tsx`, which the single deletion now uses too; the styles are in `dashboardSelect.css` and the Graphite theme follows. Texts in English, German, Czech, Dutch, French, Slovenian and Russian; the guide (Files and saving, English and German), the welcome text and the READMEs say so. Tried in the browser: three designs, two chosen and deleted, still gone after a reload. Asked for by @geoffis-coder.
- **Framing square: measures only for the selected bodies (#831):** With several bodies at the arms of the framing square, each shows its length and width automatically and the numbers can no longer be told apart. A new button under the square's handle (the arrow) switches that off: then only the selected bodies show measures, the green distances of the selection stay. It is kept with the design like the square's other switches (`selectedOnly` in `cornerRulerStore.ts` and the `.lyl` file), and a new square takes it over from the one before. Texts in every language, the guide (Measuring and notes, every language) says so; the Graphite theme was regenerated for the button. Unit test in `cornerRulerStore.test.ts`; tried in the editor: the button switches, and the choice is stored; not tried with bodies at the arms. Asked for by @willi1955.
- **A sweep that takes long (#884):** Every sketch build had 30 seconds in the CAD kernel. Sweeping a stroked SVG outline with very many points along a path ran past that and stopped with an untranslated "OpenCascade timed out"; the worker then kept working in the background, so the next try waited behind it and timed out as well. A sweep now gets two minutes; a time-out stops the worker and answers every build waiting on it, so the next one starts fresh (`requestSketchCad` in `editorSketchCad.ts`, used by extrusion, revolve and sweep); and the message, in every language, says that outlines with very many points take longest and that an SVG exported from its original drawing builds quickly. Tried in the editor: sketch bodies from an SVG build as before. Reported by @KimA998.
- **Bundle panel: the same cards as a group, Multicolor and Hole, and part colours that are no longer lost (PR #890):** Selecting a bundle gave a long note and little else, while a group showed the Solid and Hole card, Transparent and Multicolor. A bundle now has the same layout: **Solid** and **Hole** (Hole used to be reachable only from the right-click menu), **Transparent** (T) and **Multicolor** (Shift+M), then **Edit bundle** (E) and **Separate Parts**, which takes a bundle apart as Ctrl+Shift+G does; the note moved below the buttons, and the panel for the parts of a bundle being edited has Transparent too. Multicolor is on for a bundle to begin with, so each part keeps its colour; switched off, the bundle shows in one colour and a 3MF or OBJ export writes its parts in that colour (`groupColors.ts`, `expandBundles`). Two losses are fixed for groups and bundles: going from Hole back to Solid sent the group's colour along and painted it onto every part, so a red and yellow group came back all red; and picking a colour did the same, so ticking Multicolor again brought nothing back. `colorForParts` now keeps the colour on the group and leaves the parts' own; a hull, which is one body, still takes it into its parts. The guide (Shapes and Solids and holes, English and German) says so. Unit tests in `groupColors.test.ts`. Contributed by @AbhiRoutray21.
- **A widened group keeps its tapered parts' taper when cut (#893):** A group stretched one way is opened into its parts when it is cut with a hole, ungrouped, edited or hollowed. Each part's width and depth stretched with it, but a taper and a lean are kept in millimetres and stayed as they were, so a box tapered to 10 x 10 mm on top came back with its 10 mm top on a 60 mm wide body. The taper's top and bottom sizes and the lean now stretch with the part (`taperedPartPatch` in `restoreGroupedChildren`), the same way Scale by percent does it. Unit test in `groupTaperStretch.test.ts`; tried in the editor with the reported steps: the group widened to 60 mm and cut with a hole keeps a 30 mm top. Reported by @rileymake.
- **Sketch editor: reference image, rotate arrows, locks and the 3D editor's habits:** The reference image's rotate handle was a round dot on top of the width label and easy to miss; it is now the 3D editor's blue curved arrow, one just outside each corner, and while turning only the arrow you hold shows, the resize handles hide and the angle stands over the pointer. A selection in the sketch has the same corner arrows. A locked image shows a padlock badge that stays visible zoomed in and unlocks and selects the image with one click (Alt+click still works, but many Linux window managers take it); locked points are filled grey, and a selected locked outline keeps its handles, grey and inert with a "not allowed" pointer and a padlock badge that unlocks it, like a locked 3D body. The pointer is a crosshair over the image while calibrating. As in 3D, note mode shows the copy cursor everywhere on the plate, and a double click on the empty plate with nothing selected slides the view to that spot with the same 320 ms ease and the same **Double-click centres view** setting (`useSketchViewSlide.ts`). **Lock is now Ctrl+L instead of L** (L does nothing in the sketch now); Ctrl+A selects every point and line (images left out, as reference images are in 3D); Ctrl+I opens Add Image; Ctrl+H, Ctrl+G and Ctrl+E (and with Shift) no longer reach the browser, where they opened the history, find or the address bar; and the sketch's Ctrl shortcuts go by the letter typed, so they follow the keyboard layout (`sketchEditorKeys.ts`). The styles of the sketch image moved out of `globals.css` into `sketchImage.css` and `sketchLock.css`, and the Graphite theme follows. Texts in every language; the guide (Sketches, every language) says so. Unit tests in `sketchEditorKeys.test.ts` and `sketchHandleSpots.test.ts`. Contributed by @gogades.
- **The sketch of a mirrored body shows its neighbours mirrored (#896):** A body mirrored in the 3D view opens in the sketch editor as it was drawn, without the mirroring, while the other bodies shown beside it stood at their places in the design: a block next to one end of a mirrored lettering appeared next to the other end. The bodies around the sketch are now shown mirrored too, about the place of the body's middle in the sketch's own coordinates (`sketchReferenceShapeOnWorkplane` with `mirrorAbout` in `editorSketchCad.ts`; also their cut outlines and edges), for a body mirrored along x, z or both. Unit test in `sketchReferenceMirror.test.ts`; not tried by hand in the editor. Reported by @Elkniwt.
- **Hollow and the edge tools keep a hull whole (#908):** Hollow, fillet and chamfer open a group into its parts so the CAD kernel gets them exactly. A hull was opened that way too, so hollowing a hull of two rounds hollowed the two rounds one by one and the hull was gone; an intersection could not be hollowed at all. A hull or an intersection now goes to the kernel as the one body it is (`restoreGroupedChildrenForEdges`). Unit test in `hullEdgeParts.test.ts`; tried in the editor: a hull of a 20 and a 40 mm round hollowed with an open bottom stays one belt-cover shape. Reported by @RobbieKnobbie.
- **A body inside another body of a different colour is no longer lost in the 3MF and OBJ export (#906):** The colour-separated export keeps each colour as a body of its own and lets the later shape cut its place out of an earlier one where two colours overlap (Discussion #79), so a logo set into a plate gets its pocket in the plate. A body that lies entirely inside a later one of another colour was cut away to nothing: a text first in the list and wholly inside a group's body was missing from the file, with nothing said, and the slicer saw one object fewer (the mirroring and the font were not the cause). Such a body now stays whole and the later body takes its pocket, as if the text had come last. A body that only reaches into a later one is cut as before. E2e test in `colorExportSwallowedBody.e2e.ts`; with the file from the report the export writes four bodies, the text with its 1972 triangles and the group with the pocket. Reported by @MethylBromide.
- **Keep proportions also for the size typed on the body (#899):** Keep proportions in the properties panel made the other two measures follow a typed one, but the number at a body's edge, clicked and typed into, passed the one measure alone: it applied the held edge first, after which the proportions were no longer worked out. It now keeps the proportions before the held edge, as the panel does, for one body and for a selection's frame; Shift+Enter in that box does the same once without the tick, as Shift does when a corner is dragged. The guide (Shapes, German and English) says so. Tried in the editor on a 20 mm box: 30 with the tick gives 30 x 30 x 30, 40 without it changes the width alone, and 20 with Shift+Enter halves all three. Reported by @KevinGroninga.
- **Seen from below, see-through bodies show through the plate (#894):** The plate is drawn before the see-through bodies, so a body above it is not laid over by it. Turned below the plate, the plate stood in front of them instead, and drawn first it hid a see-through body completely, while a solid one showed. From the plate's back side it is now drawn after them (`orderPlatesForView` in `renderFrame.ts`, by the side of the plate the camera is on), so they show through it faded; this holds for a workplane on a face and in the second view too. Tried in the editor: a see-through and a solid box seen from below at a slant both show through the plate, and from above nothing changed. Reported by @Elkniwt.
- **European Portuguese, a start (PR #888):** "Português de Portugal" (`pt-pt`) is a language of its own beside Brazilian Portuguese: 686 of the interface's sentences and the guide's first chapter, "Getting started", with the guide's wording for the language (`guia-pt-pt`). It stays marked as incomplete, so what is missing shows in English, and a browser that asks for `pt-PT` now gets it instead of English. The tests that listed the guide's languages, the order of the names and the Brazilian fallback were changed to match; the language count in the welcome text and the READMEs is 17 more languages. Contributed by @jcpcustodio.
- **Back up all with large designs (#887):** Back up all held every design in memory and then the whole backup once more in one block. With many large designs Safari ran out of memory, reloaded the page and left neither a file nor a word. The backup is now written entry by entry (`createBackupWriter` in `projectBackup.ts`, a stored ZIP as before): each design is added and let go, and what is written so far is handed to a Blob the browser may keep outside the page. A design that cannot be read is left out and the message names it with the reason. Before each design the backup notes where it is; if the page is reloaded in the middle, the start page says afterwards at which design it stopped and suggests saving that one on its own or simplifying its largest meshes. Texts in every language; the guide (Files and saving, German and English) says so. Unit test in `projectBackup.test.ts` (the written ZIP reads back like packBackup's, folders, pictures and an empty folder included). Reported by @TinkerBarn.
- **Reference point in the middle of the face you click (#881):** The right-click menu of a body has a new entry, **Mark the centre of this face**: it puts a reference point in the middle of the flat face the pointer was on, a side or an end, not only on the top as "Mark the centre" does. The middle is that of the whole flat face (every triangle in that plane joined to the one hit, `pickFaceCentre` in `viewportPicking.ts`, the same as the rotation pivot takes); a curved surface offers nothing. The tape measure snaps to the point like to a corner, so the centres of two stud ends can be measured, and the framing square gives the horizontal distance apart. The guide (Measuring and notes, every language) says so. Tried in the editor: on the front of a box 40 x 40 x 20 mm at x 30, the point lands at x 30, depth 20, height 10. Asked for by @LarryRB.
- **The tape measure's measurements stay with the design, and can be hidden (#881):** The measurements of the tape measure were lost when the page was reloaded or the design opened again. They are now kept with the design like the framing square (`lib/tapeStore.ts`): in the browser beside it and in the `.lyl` file (`editor.tapeMeasurements`: the points, the lines between them and the body each point is fixed to; the format version stays, older readers pass over it). A new eye button among the tape measure's buttons hides all measurements and shows them again; whether they are hidden is kept too, and adding, moving or deleting a measurement shows them first (`TapeModel.hidden`, `syncTapeOverlay`). A measurement fixed to a body follows it when the design is opened, and one whose body is gone is dropped as before; a body whose drawing is not built yet no longer makes its measurement go while the design opens (`tapeObjectBuilt`). A copy of a design (Duplicate) takes the measurements along, and the MCP action `layerling_measurements` shows or hides them and lists them with their lengths (guide chapter on AI, skill, tool list). Texts in every language, the start page (English and German), the guide (Measuring and notes, every language) and the format description say it. Unit tests in `tapeStore.test.ts` (file round trip, hidden state, damaged entries); tried in the editor: a measurement comes back after reloading, the eye button hides it, and it stays hidden after another reload; a measurement fixed to a box follows the box when it was moved before the reload, and the MCP bridge answers with the length. Asked for by @LarryRB.
- **A hollowed or filleted body keeps its exact size:** After Hollow, a fillet, a chamfer or Delete face the body comes back from the CAD kernel as facets, and its sizes were measured on them: a round side falls a few hundredths short, so a cylinder of 38.7 mm showed 38.69 after hollowing. Typing 38.7 again then stretched the body before the hollowing by that error, turned the round cylinder into an ellipse of 38.715 x 38.7 mm and drifted further with every try. A size, centre or bottom within the facets' reach (twice the mesh deflection, 0.02 to 0.1 mm) of what an unturned body had stays the body's own (`shapeFromCadMesh`), and the mesh is drawn stretched by that hair onto the true surface. Unit test in `cadMeshExactSize.test.ts`; tried in the editor: a cylinder of 38.7 x 27 mm hollowed with a 3 mm wall stays 38.7 mm, typing 38.7 changes nothing and 50 mm gives exactly 50. Reported by Jürgen Keller.
- **Gridfinity trays of any size, for board game inserts (#837):** A Gridfinity bin is always whole 42 mm units on feet with a stacking lip, so a token tray for a game box, or any tray that need not stand in a baseplate, had to be built by hand from boxes. The Gridfinity shape now has a third type under **Type**, **Tray**: **Length**, **Width** and **Height** are free in mm, 10 to 252 mm across and deep and 3 to 140 mm high, and the bottom is flat, with no feet, lip or magnet holes. Everything inside a bin works in a tray as well: the wall thickness, the floor (1.2 mm thick until it is set, up to a solid block), compartments with their dividers and labels, finger scoops, label shelves, open sides and partly open ones. As nothing stacks on a tray, its dividers and label shelves run up to its top, and it takes as many compartments as leave each at least 6 mm wide, up to 24 along a side and 40 in all; label shelves need no height units, only room, as finger scoops on any part. A bin turned into a tray keeps its box, a baseplate turned into one is 20 mm high in the panel, a new tray starts 80 mm wide, 40 mm long and 20 mm high when Tray is the default type in the workspace settings or through the MCP, and a tray turned back into a bin or baseplate is rounded to grid units. Bins and baseplates are built exactly as before. The view, the exports and the exact body agree. `layerling_create_shape`, `layerling_update_object` and `layerling_read_scene` take and report `gridfinityType: "tray"`; an older version of layerling refuses a project that has a tray. Asked for by @superkrm in #837. Contributed by @mlugo-apx.
- **The Face tool and the hole pattern light a group's whole face, and the pivot goes to its middle:** Since 1.111.0 the Face tool and the hole pattern pick a group's whole face, but the pointer still lit one part's face, and a rotation pivot clicked on a group went to the middle of one part's face: on two 20 mm cubes grouped with their middles 7 and 8 mm apart, to the middle of one cube's top at (0, 0) rather than of the whole 644 mm² top at (3.5, 4). The Face tool and the hole pattern now join the parts shortly after they open, without the "Calculating" notice, and light the whole face once the join is there; a click before that waits for it. A pivot click on a group or bundle waits for its own join and goes to the middle of the whole face. On a group or bundle with text in it, or a bundle with a hole among its parts, the pointer lights and the pivot takes one part's face as before. Lay flat, Align faces and a new shape's snap to the middle of a face (#823) still take one part's face. The guide (Select and arrange, in English, German, Dutch, French, Russian, Czech and Slovenian) says so. Unit tests in `groupFacePointer.test.ts`, `rotationPivot.test.ts` and `geometryWorker.test.ts`; tried in the editor: the two cubes' whole top lit once joined without moving the pointer, and the pivot set at (3.5, 4) on the top. Contributed by @mlugo-apx.
- **The face tools no longer freeze the editor on a group:** In 1.111.0 the Face tool, the hole pattern, `layerling_push_face` and `layerling_perforate_face` started reading a group's face from its parts joined (#878), and the join ran on the page: pushing the top of four overlapping 512-sided cylinders kept a click waiting about half a second, now 0.04 s. The join runs in the geometry worker, with the "Calculating" notice until the face is read; a group with text in it stays on the page, and a read that takes too long says so. Tried in the editor with the worker and with its file blocked: the Face tool and `layerling_push_face` give the same top of two grouped cubes, 27 x 28 across. Contributed by @mlugo-apx.

## 1.111.0

- **A workplane up to 5000 x 5000 mm (#879):** The custom workplane size stopped at 2000 mm in the Workspace settings, a limit the very first version had and for which there is no technical reason (`MAX_WORKSPACE_SIZE` in `WorkspaceSettingsModal.tsx`; saved settings were never cut). It now goes to 5000 mm. A 5000 x 5000 mm plate with the 1 mm grid opens and draws in the editor; how fast it turns on a slow graphics card was not measured. Asked for by @LarryRB.
- **Paste in the middle and drop (#876):** A paste puts each part a little beside the place it came from (at most 110 mm from the middle), so parts copied from another program, which can sit far from the plate or in the air, land where they get lost in the view. The new switch **Paste in the middle and drop** in the settings (area Appearance, off by default, `pasteCentered`) sets the pasted parts together in the middle of the workplane and drops them onto it: the middle is measured along the workplane (the plate, or the point picked on a face), the lowest point goes onto the plane, and the parts keep their places to each other (`pasteCenteringShift` in `pasteCentering.ts`, used by `pasteShape`). Texts in English, German, Czech, Dutch, French, Russian and Slovenian; the guide (Getting started, German and English) says so. Unit tests in `pasteCentering.test.ts`. Asked for by @foofoodog.

- **Text: other words keep the baseline (#871):** The box of a straight text is laid round the shapes of its letters, so changing the words left it bottom aligned: a "p" ended on the bottom of its box, where the "T" of the old words had stood, instead of reaching below the line, and a word without a tail jumped back up. Typing other words in the Text field now moves the box so that the baseline stays where it was (`textBaselineKeepShift` in `nameTag.ts`, along the text's own depth, so also for a turned text), and the box grows downwards for a tail or upwards for a tall letter. Curved and stretched text and text tilted out of the flat keep their place as before. Unit tests in `nameTag.test.ts`. Reported by @MethylBromide.
- **The Face tool and the hole pattern take the whole face of a group:** Two 20 mm cubes grouped with their middles 7 and 8 mm apart have one top face of 644 mm², but the Face tool picked and pushed only one cube's top, 400 mm², and the hole pattern and the MCP actions `layerling_perforate_face` and `layerling_push_face` did the same. A group or bundle of solids without holes is its parts' surfaces side by side, and a face is grown over triangles that share corners, so it stopped at the edge of one part. The face is now read from the parts joined into one (`faceReadMesh`), as a plain group already is for the export and the volume, so the two cubes give their whole top rather than one cube's. Any other body, a group or bundle with a hole among its parts or in a bundle inside them, and a group whose parts cannot be joined are read as before. The highlight under the pointer still lights one part's face, as the parts are drawn side by side. E2e test in `faceReadGroup.e2e.ts`; tried in the editor: the two cubes' top picked whole with the Face tool, pushed out by 2 mm and taken back with one undo, the tool picking again after Cancel, and `layerling_push_face` giving the 27 x 28 top. Contributed by @mlugo-apx.
- **Attachments: pictures and PDF files (#820):** A design could only keep `.md` and `.txt` files, so a photo of the printed part, a wiring sketch or a datasheet had to live somewhere else. It can now also keep `.png`, `.jpg`/`.jpeg` and `.webp` pictures and `.pdf` documents, each a row of `ATTACHMENT_TYPES` with its own content check: a file must start with the first bytes of its type (the PNG signature, `FF D8 FF`, `RIFF`…`WEBP`, `%PDF-`), so a renamed file is refused with "is not a real PNG file" and the like. A picture is also decoded once when it is added (panel and MCP tools, `createImageBitmap`), so a real header with garbage, HTML or a script behind it, a cut-off file or a picture too large to decode is refused too; a picture the viewer still cannot decode (from a package made elsewhere) gets a sentence saying so instead of a broken image. A picture's row has the eye, which shows it in the attachment viewer, shrunk to fit: the browser draws it from a local Blob address of its own bytes (as the icon tag preview does), revoked when the window closes, so nothing is fetched. A PDF is downloaded only. SVG may not be attached, since it can carry scripts and links to other servers; GIF, BMP and HEIC are left out too. A design with a picture or a PDF is written as `.lyl` format version 4 (each type names the version that first carries it), so a layerling that knows version 3 refuses it with "requires a newer layerling" instead of failing on the file; designs with only text files stay version 3, and those without attachments version 2. A version 3 package with a picture is refused. Limits now go by the type (a `maxBytes` column): text stays at 2 MB, a picture may have 16 MB (a phone photo of the finished assembly) and a PDF 32 MB (a scanned technical document); a design holds up to 32 files and 64 MB together, up from 16 and 8 MB. A design past the old limits is written as version 4 even with text alone, since a layerling that knows version 3 would refuse it as broken. Pictures and PDF files are compressed already, so the package stores them as they are instead of deflating them again (`boundedProjectZip` takes a set of stored paths): a save with 50 MB of photos costs about a tenth of the time. The MCP tools take the new types through the same checks; their descriptions and the skill (all three copies) list them. Texts in English, German, Arabic, Czech, Danish, Dutch, French, Russian and Slovenian; the guide (Files and saving, the same languages) and `LYL_PROJECT_FORMAT.md` describe it. Unit tests in `projectAttachments.test.ts`, `lylAttachments.test.ts`, `projectAttachmentsMcp.test.ts` and `boundedProjectZip.test.ts` (signatures, renamed files, SVG refused, the limit of each type, format version 3 or 4 by content and by count, a version 3 package with a picture or too many files refused, pictures and PDF stored and text deflated); tried in the editor: a PNG, a JPEG, a WebP and a PDF attach, the three pictures open in the viewer and the PDF has no eye, a renamed text file is refused. Contributed by @jbuggie.
- **A corner radius for Gridfinity baseplates (#856):** A baseplate's four outer corners were always rounded by 4 mm, as the specification draws them, so a plate pushed into a drawer's square corner left a gap in it. A baseplate now has **Corner radius** under the padding rows, 4 mm to begin with so existing baseplates stay as they are, down to 0 for square corners, padded or not; the sockets keep their rounding, so bins fit as before. A radius within 0.05 mm of 4 is built as 4. A bin keeps the setting and ignores it. The view, the exports and the exact body agree. `layerling_create_shape`, `layerling_update_object` and `layerling_read_scene` take and report `gridfinityCornerRadius`. Asked for by @TinkerBarn in #856. Contributed by @mlugo-apx.

## 1.110.0

- **A bundle of text wrapped round a cylinder is no longer stretched (#667):** A body wrapped round a cylinder covers only an arc, but its box for aligning and centring is the whole cylinder (#106). Grouping or bundling took that box as the size of the group, so the group came out larger than the parts it held, and the view stretched them to fill it: four parts of a medal, one of them wrapped text 122 mm wide that covers 33 mm, made a bundle of 127 x 122 mm over parts that fill 100 x 110 mm, and the medal showed 27 % wider and oval until Undo. The group is now sized by the real vertices of its parts (`boundsForShapesOfVertices`, used by `groupedShape`), so it fits them and sits in their middle. Unit test in `bundleOfWrappedParts.test.ts`. Sizes changing after grouping and ungrouping, reported by @kokobandit; the bundle that showed it, with video and file, by @MiniCoop8.
- **Text: changing the words keeps the letter size (#871):** Typing another text into the Text field only set the box's width (at least 46 mm), and the depth stayed, so the letters were stretched to fill the old depth: a single "a" grew until it was 2 mm tall and the letter size jumped (2 to 2.52 mm), and the measure showed the width of the old box. The text field now uses `textLetterSizePatch`, as the Letter size field does, so the letter size stays and the box follows the letters; a text that would be wider than the allowed width is still fitted into it. Not changed: the letters sit on the bottom of their box instead of on the baseline, so a "p" does not reach below the line. Reported by @MethylBromide.
- **Alt and the lift arrow makes a copy (#865):** Dragging the black lift arrow above a shape with Alt held from the start now leaves the shape where it is and makes a copy where you let go, so copies can be stacked straight up, as Alt and dragging the shape already did sideways. While you drag, the shape shows the copy and a stand-in holds its place (`duplicate` in `TransformDragState`, `beginLiftDuplicate` and `finishTransform` in `WorkplaneViewport.tsx`; the copy is made by the same `onDuplicateShapesMoved`). The height handle has no copy. The guide (Shapes, every language that has the section) had the two handles the wrong way round and now says: the arrow above lifts, the white handle on the top face changes the height. Asked for by @raradek.
- **Attachments: read a file without downloading it (#820):** A `.md` or `.txt` attached to a design could only be downloaded and opened elsewhere. Its row in the Attachments panel now has an eye (only for the types that can be shown: a `view` column in `ATTACHMENT_TYPES`), which opens the file in the quick guide's window, moved by its title bar and remembered where it stood (`AttachmentViewer.tsx`). Markdown is shown formatted (headings, lists with tick boxes, quotes, code, tables with their alignment, links), a text file exactly as written; Escape, the cross or a click beside the window closes it, it closes by itself when the file is removed or another design opens, and it works in the History view, as Download does. Safe by default, since a file can come from anywhere: Markdown is read by a small parser of its own (`markdownView.ts`, no new dependency) that makes a tree and never HTML, and `MarkdownView.tsx` draws that tree as React elements, so nothing is ever set as HTML; raw HTML in a file stays text, only `http:`, `https:` and `mailto:` links work (in a new tab, with `noopener noreferrer`), a picture is never loaded (its description and address stand in its place, so opening a file tells no server about it), nesting stops at 12 levels, and a file made to be slow is still read in linear time (a 2 MB file of unclosed markers takes well under a second). Texts in English, German, Czech, Danish, Dutch, French, Russian and Slovenian; the guide (Files and saving, the same languages) describes it. Unit tests in `markdownView.test.ts` (raw HTML, refused link targets, pictures, depth, hostile files, every block and inline form); tried in the editor: Markdown and text files open and close by each way, in light and dark theme, with no request for the picture. Contributed by @jbuggie.
- **Hull: shapes wrapped in one convex body (#863):** Combine has a new tool, **Hull**: it wraps the selected shapes in their convex hull, the smallest body without dents round all of them, as if shrink-wrapped. A half sphere on a cylinder becomes a dome with no step, four corner posts a box with round corners, two spheres a capsule. Holes in the selection are cut out of the hull afterwards. It is a group of its own kind (`groupOperation: "hull"`, `buildHullShapeFromSelection` in `editorBooleans.ts`, through `Manifold.hull`): the parts stay in it, Edit group opens it and Done builds the hull anew, and Ungroup gives the parts back. The hull is a mesh, so round shapes show facets. A hull or an intersection inside a group with a box hole is no longer opened into its parts by the quick box cut, and a hull shows in one colour. `layerling_hull_objects` does the same over MCP. The toolbar thresholds moved for the new button (2180, 1850 and 1698 px, measured). Texts in every language; the guide (Solids and holes, German and English) describes it, and the AI chapter lists the tool in every language. E2e test in `hull.e2e.ts`; tried in the editor: the button on a half sphere and a cylinder, Edit group with the dome moved aside, and the MCP action on two spheres. Asked for by @MethylBromide, and earlier by @rondlh.
- **Curve through points:** With three or more points of an outline selected, the sketch's selection bar now has **Curve through points** (other programs call it "Fit curve" or "Fit point spline"): it lays one flowing curve from the first selected point to the last, through every point in between (selected or not), with the same handles the Smooth Curve tool gives (a sixth of the way between each point's neighbours). The sides beyond the two ends stay as they are, and so do the ends' other handles; on a closed outline the curve takes the short way round the selected points, and with every point selected the whole outline turns round. Pressing 3 still makes each selected point smooth on its own. The new code sits in `sketchCurveThroughPoints.ts`; the point-mode helper moved out of `LayerlingEditor.tsx` into `sketchSmoothHandles.ts`. Texts in every language; the guide (Sketches, English, German, Arabic, Czech, Danish, Spanish, French, Hungarian, Italian, Dutch, Polish, Russian, Slovenian and both Chinese) describes it. Unit tests in `sketchCurveThroughPoints.test.ts`. Contributed by @gogades.
- **Guide: soft light and the light height (#812):** With the light set low a bright reflection can still show on a body even with Soft light on; the guide (View and workplane, German and English) now says that lifting Light height to 90° removes it and looks like Tinkercad. Noticed by @VZ-studios.
- **On a Mac, Control and drag turns the view again (#860):** Control-click is the Mac's right click, and a right drag turns the view, so on a MacBook Control with a trackpad press and drag turned it. Since #654 (1.95.0) Ctrl or Cmd with the left button moves the view, and on a Mac that took the turn away: only the view cube was left. On a Mac, Control alone now turns the view again (`leftButtonCameraAction` asks OrbitControls for PAN there, which its modifier swap turns into a rotation; `isMacPointer`), in the editor, the second view and the Simplify comparison. Cmd and drag still moves the view on a Mac, and Ctrl and drag elsewhere. The mouse table of the guide (View and workplane, German and English) says so. Unit test in `cameraMouseButtons.test.ts`; tried in the editor with a Mac browser: Control and drag turns the view, Cmd and drag and, on Windows, Ctrl and drag move it. Reported by @jerryjaksha.
