- **New designs on the server: every way in goes there:** With `LAYERLING_NEW_DESIGNS_ON_SERVER` (or the tick New designs on the server) only the Create new 3D design tile started a design in the server folder. Opening a file, importing, restoring a backup, duplicating a browser design and taking a state from the history still made it in the browser, where Safari clears it after seven days without a visit, and New folder on the start page made a browser folder that stayed empty. Each of them now places the design in the server folder that is open (`placeNewDesign` in `page.tsx`: stored here first, then uploaded and bound; a name taken in that folder becomes a copy's name, and if the server refuses or cannot be reached the design stays in the browser and the message says why). A backup goes there with its folders, made below the open one, and New folder makes a server folder ("Folder … made on the server"; its button says so). A design made in the first moments waits for the server's answer (its defaults and the first look at its folder, at most 8 seconds) before it decides, so a click right after a container start no longer lands in the browser. With no designs left in this browser, the storage notice says "Your designs are kept on the server" in green instead of asking for a browser backup, and keeps Restore backup. Texts in every language; the guide (Files and saving, German and English) and the README say so. Tried in the editor with a server folder and `always`: the tile clicked at once, an STL import, New folder and a backup with two folder levels and an empty folder all landed on the server; without the folder everything stays in the browser as before. **Docker guide:** [docs/docker](docker/README.md) holds a complete guide in English and German for running the image with the server folder as the only storage, with its own compose file and an update script that checks the configuration against the new image, backs up the folder and rolls back a failed update; the README links it. Reported and written by Jürgen Keller.
- **Gridfinity sides opened partly (#455):** A side switched open lost its whole wall and lip, while a bin often needs only a slot to reach into or a lower front. Under the four open switches, now laid out as a cross seen from above, each open side has its own rows, named for the front here: **Depth at the front**, the opening's depth from the lip's top (from the bin's top without the lip), at least 2 mm; **Width at the front**, at least 5 mm; **Position at the front**, at one end or in the middle; and, at an end, **Distance at the front** from it. The whole side down to the floor, as it starts, is the open side as before, and so is anything within 0.5 mm of it or a side whose straight part is too short for the narrowest opening. Anything less keeps the side's wall, rounded corners and lip and cuts a notch with a flat bottom and square ends out of them: down to the floor a slot, with no ridge of the floor's rounding left across it, and across the whole side a lower wall. The notch stays on the straight part of the wall between the corners, or runs out to the edge beside a side open in full, where the wall has no rounded corner. The floor, the dividers and the lip beside the notch stay, so a bin stacked on it still sits on the lip; the side's shelf, scoop and compartment label shelves stay off as on any open side, and the wall thickness row stays. On a bin built solid by its wall, a side switched open is open in full. **Opening corners** and **Opening corner size** round or chamfer a notch's two top and two bottom corners seen from outside the wall, from 0 to 10 mm, the slider in steps of 0.5 and the size kept to 0.01 mm, square to begin with: each corner takes at most what the notch and the wall beside it leave, the top ones first, and under 0.5 mm it stays square; a notch across the whole side has no top corners, and one run out to the edge beside a side open in full has none at that end. The view, the exports and the exact body agree. `layerling_create_shape`, `layerling_update_object` and `layerling_read_scene` take and report `gridfinityOpenings`, `gridfinityOpeningCorner` and `gridfinityOpeningCornerSize`. A bin switched open on all four sides is never built solid; a side whose wall leaves no room for its opening is open in full. Asked for by @RudiVader in #455. Contributed by @mlugo-apx.

## 1.109.0

- **Touch: a tap beside a body no longer raises the keyboard by accident (#855):** The value boxes around a selected body (a length, a height, a move) are buttons that open a number field, and with it the on-screen keyboard. A finger aiming at the body or at a handle landed on such a box now and then and the keyboard covered the workplane. With a finger the first tap on a box now only marks it (a frame in the accent colour, for 2.5 seconds) and the second tap opens the field (`touchSecondTap.ts`); a mouse and a pen open it at once as before. Reported by @crazykay.
- **The Align dots are easier to hit with a finger (#854):** The dots of the Align tool are 14 px wide, and a tap that missed one did not start the tool and also ended the multi-selection that aligning several parts depends on. On a touch screen (`pointer: coarse`) the dots keep their look and now catch a tap from a finger's width round them, as the transform and rotate handles already did. Reported by @crazykay.
- **Alt+W puts the workplane back on the base plate (#845):** Going back to the base plate took Esc while placing, a click beside the plate or the arrow button below the eye; there was no key for it. The shortcuts list has its own row for it, and the guide (View and workplane, every guide language) and the in-app guide (every language) say so. Tried in the editor: with the workplane on the top of a box and the box selected, Alt+W put it back on the plate and kept the selection. Contributed by @gogades.
- **A group stretched one way keeps its round parts oval when it is hollowed (#844):** Two cylinders and a box grouped into a capsule, then made wider only, looked right, but Hollow turned the ends into circles of the new length, a "dog bone". Hollow, the edge tools, Ungroup and the boolean tools take a group apart into its shapes again, and a cylinder is always round, so it took the larger of its two stretched sizes. A cylinder the group stretches more one way than the other now comes back as an ellipse of both sizes (`restoreGroupedChildren`, through `cylinderToEllipsePatch`, so one still called "Cylinder" is called "Ellipse"); a group grown the same both ways keeps its cylinders. Stars, knurls and gears have no oval form and stay round. Unit test in `stretchedGroupCylinder.test.ts`; tried in the editor: the capsule widened from 70 to 100 mm hollows as a smooth capsule, and ungrouped gives two ellipses of 42.86 x 30 mm. Reported by @forrrge.
- **Danish: the texts and guide passages new in 1.108.0 (#851):** The 29 texts of Attachments and Group the copies, and the guide's passages on Attachments (Files and saving), Select other and grouping a pattern's copies (Select and arrange) and the framing square kept with the design and showing sizes only at its arms (Measuring and notes). By @Henrikth12.
- **Guide: the framing square is kept with the design (#851):** Measuring and notes still said, in every language that has the chapter, that the tape measure and the framing square go when the page is reloaded, against the paragraph above it. Since #829 that holds for the tape measure only; the sentence says so now (English, German, Arabic, Czech, Spanish, French, Hungarian, Italian, Dutch, Polish, Russian, Slovenian and both Chinese; Danish came corrected with #851). Spotted by @Henrikth12.
- **brepjs 20.10.3, without its webpack workarounds (#846):** brepjs, which the STEP export and import use, goes from 20.5.0 to 20.10.3. Its import of an optional kernel is now marked for webpack in brepjs itself, so `next.config.ts` no longer needs the aliases for `brepkit-wasm` and `brepjs-opencascade` or the filter for the "Critical dependency" warning (#819); the build compiles without warnings. The new version also names the edges a fillet or chamfer failed on and rejects shell and offset results that are not usable solids. Checked with all unit and e2e tests (the STEP round trip included) and a production build. Contributed by @andymai.
- **Use my default brings the printer along (#850):** Use my default put the saved colours and grid on an opened design but kept its plate and printer, so a Tinkercad export stayed on "No printer - free size". A printer in the saved default now comes along with its plate size (`workspaceWithSavedDefault`); a default without one still leaves the design its plate and printer, and the units stay with the design. The button's hint (every language) and the guide (View and workplane, German, English and Danish) say so. Unit tests in `useMyDefault.test.ts`. Reported by @TinkerBarn.
- **A start view of your own (#853):** New designs always opened looking from the front right, and Home went back there. Settings → Appearance now has **Start view** with **Use current view**, which keeps the angle, pan and zoom on screen, and **Standard**, which goes back to the usual view. New designs open in it and Home (and `layerling_capture_image` with face "home", and the second view's Home) goes back to it (`resetCamera`, `startViewOf`); a design opened again still opens the way it was left. It is kept per browser like "Start editor in perspective view" (`layerling.editor.startView`), and as the normal camera sees it: a flat view's zoom becomes the distance that shows as much, so it looks the same in either projection. `layerling_saved_view` takes `startView: "save"` or `"reset"` and answers whether one is set. Texts in every language; the guide (View and workplane, German, English and Danish; the MCP table in every guide language) and the start page describe it. Unit tests in `startView.test.ts`. Asked for by @TinkerBarn.
- **occt-wasm 5.8.0: a refused fillet names its edges (#849):** When a fillet or chamfer on several edges is refused, the kernel now names the edges whose contour failed (`OcctError.faultyEdges`). The worker tries those alone first (`cadFaultyEdgeIds`), and when each of them fails alone they are the answer, so the other edges are no longer tried one by one; when one of them works alone, or the error names none, every edge is tried as before. Both CAD workers start the kernel through `OcctKernel.init` instead of reaching its private constructor. E2e test in `cadFaultyEdges.e2e.ts`; checked with all unit and e2e tests and a production build. Contributed by @andymai.
- **Attachments for the AI bridge (#820, #852):** An AI client could build a design but not reach the files kept with it, so a parts list or assembly notes it wrote had to be uploaded by hand. Five MCP tools now do what the Attachments panel does: `layerling_list_attachments` (id, name, type, size and hash of each file, and the limits), `layerling_get_attachment` (one file's bytes as base64), `layerling_add_attachment` (a file given as base64), `layerling_rename_attachment` and `layerling_remove_attachment`; `layerling_read_scene` lists the files too, without their content. The tools move bytes and nothing else; decoding and choosing names is the agent's part. They run every file through the panel's own checks in `projectAttachments.ts` (types, the content check, sizes, number of files, clean names), so the panel and the bridge cannot disagree, and the commands sit in their own module, `projectAttachmentsMcp.ts`, next to it. Safe by default: attachments are not in the undo history, so a name already attached is only overwritten with `replace: true` (the file keeps its id, place and name, as the panel's Replace) and a file is only removed with `confirm: true`; there is no `path`, so an agent cannot copy a file from the computer into a design on its own; listing and reading work in the History view, changes wait until it is closed; the tool descriptions and the skill say that a file's text is the user's data, not instructions. The skill (all three copies) and the AI chapter of the guide in every language list the tools. Unit tests in `projectAttachmentsMcp.test.ts` (each command, a taken name with and without `replace`, the same content again, refused types, text, sizes and base64, the number of files, rename keeping id and content, remove only with `confirm`). Contributed by @jbuggie.

## 1.108.0

- **Danish: the texts and guide passages new in 1.107.0 (#827):** Soft light with its hint, and the guide's passages on soft light, the cut in the background, closing small holes and the Gridfinity lip chamfer. By @Henrikth12.
- **Danish: the guide passages that were never translated (#841):** Compared chapter by chapter with the English guide: the Japanese letters in Text (and eight typefaces, not seven), parking parts beside the plate in Printing, the standing ruler in Measuring and notes, the cut-off bundle entry and a mistranslated sentence on bundle holes in Solids and holes, and smaller passages in Shapes, Select and arrange and Offline. Eight paragraphs that stood twice in View and workplane are gone. By @Henrikth12.
- **A pattern can group its pieces (#828):** The Pattern tool made every copy a shape of its own, so a ring of 35 pieces had to be selected and grouped by hand. The Pattern window now has **Group the copies**: with it ticked, Create groups the original and the copies in the same step, through the same off-thread Group as the button (`buildGroupedShapeOffThread`), as an ordinary group that Ungroup and Edit group open again. Above 5 pieces the box ticks itself and unticks at 5 or fewer, until the person sets it (`groupCopies`, `arrayGroupsCopies` in `shapeArray.ts`); a ruler stays loose, and if grouping fails the pieces come in loose with the reason. One Undo takes it all back. `layerling_array_objects` takes `group: true` for the same (left out, the pieces stay loose). Text in English and the six complete languages; the guide (Select and arrange, German and English) describes it. Unit test in `shapeArray.test.ts`. Contributed by @AbhiRoutray21.
- **Select other: reach a shape behind another (#804):** A hole inside a body, or a part behind another, could only be clicked after turning the view, or picked in the object list. When more than one shape lies under the pointer, the right-click menu (and a long press on a tablet) now has **Select other…**: it lists every shape the ray under the pointer passes through, front to back, each once (`shapesUnderPointer` in `viewportPicking.ts`, with the same rules as a click: in front of the section plane, no locked reference image). The row the pointer or the focus is on outlines its shape through whatever stands in front, with the History view's marking in a layer of its own (`syncPickHighlight`, `highlightIds` on the viewport); a click selects it, Shift adds it. The menu scrolls when the list is long. Texts in every language; the guide (Select and arrange, German and English) describes it. Asked for by @raradek, the way Onshape and Fusion do it described by @ssmeier-gmail.
- **The framing square is kept with the design (#829):** The framing square lived only in the open view (`toolMemory`), so a reload or opening the design again took it away, and lining it up on the grid again shifted every measure taken from it. It is now kept like the saved views: per design in the browser (`layerling.cornerRulers.<id>`, `lib/cornerRulerStore.ts`) and in the `.lyl` file under `editor.cornerRulers`, which older readers pass over; a file opened or a backup restored brings it back. It comes back where it was, on its workplane, turned the same way, with its endpoint/midpoint modes and whether bodies hide it; the × removes it from the design too. It stays out of the undo history, as a tool. The guide (Measuring and notes, German and English) says so. Unit tests in `cornerRulerStore.test.ts`; tried in the editor: set, reloaded (the handle at the same pixel), saved as .lyl (it is in the file) and removed. Asked for by @Wes711 and @Gpic2030.
- **The framing square shows sizes only for bodies at its arms (#831):** A body that is not selected gets its sizes at the framing square when it stands at an arm. The test only looked at how far the body lies across the arm, not whether it lies beside it: a body that touched the corner from outside the square, or lay behind it on an arm's extension, kept its length and width on screen after it was deselected. Now a body counts only when it reaches along the arm's length and to its inner side, or across it (`cornerRulerDimensionMatchesFromCorner`, which takes the second arm's direction from the drawn arm); the straight ruler is unchanged. The guide (Measuring and notes, German and English) says so. Unit tests with all four sides of the corner in `rulerDimensions.test.ts`; tried in the editor: a 40 x 30 box at the corner from outside shows nothing, one inside at an arm its size. Reported by @willi1955.
- **Add Image works at once in a new sketch (#833):** A new sketch starts with the line tool, and Add Image was greyed out until Select was chosen, so a reference image could not go in before drawing without that detour. The button and its command are now always available: a click switches to Select itself, as a click on the arrow would (a line just begun is let go), and opens the picker. The guide (Sketches, German and English) says so. Tried in the editor: a new extrude sketch with the line tool and a first point set, Add Image switched to Select and put the picture in. Asked for by @blueeagle69.
- **A group under a sketch shows its parts' outlines also when they are fine (#834):** Under a sketch each body is drawn by its outline, but a body with more than 20,000 triangles only by its box. For a group that limit counted the whole group: a knurl and a bolt, each well under it, came to 27,320 triangles together, and their union showed as one square while each on its own showed round. The limit now counts per part (`groupMeshFootprint`), and a part over it shows as its own box, the others keep their outlines. Unit test in `sketchReferenceOutline.test.ts`; tried in the editor with a knurl and a bolt grouped: two round outlines. Reported by @willi1955.
- **Attachments: files kept with a design (#820):** Instructions, a parts list or a few notes had no place in a design except the notes, which are short labels (2000 characters, pinned to a body). A design can now carry files. **Attachments** (a paperclip in the Manage toolbar, also in the simple toolbar, with a small dot while there are any) opens a panel with one button, **Upload attachment**, and the list of the files with Download and Remove; the row asks once more before it removes. To begin with `.md` and `.txt`: UTF-8 text, 2 MB a file, 16 files and 8 MB together (`ATTACHMENT_TYPES` and `ATTACHMENT_LIMITS` in `projectAttachments.ts`; another type is another row of that table). A name that is already attached is neither added silently nor refused: the panel offers **Replace** (the attached file keeps its place, its id and its name), **Rename** (it suggests the first free name, `notes 2.md`, and checks what is typed) or leaving the file out; names count the same in upper and lower case, and the same file again changes nothing. The files are saved in the design's `.lyl` package as assets of a new kind, `attachment`, listed under `attachments` in `project.json`, and they travel the way `assets` do: through the editor, the start page's storage of a design, the server autosave, the export window, "Back up all designs", duplicating a design and a new design made from a history state. They are not part of the undo history, and "Insert into this design", custom shapes and the bug report leave them out. **Format version 3** is written only for a design that has attachments (`minimumReaderVersion` 3): an older layerling refuses it instead of opening it and dropping the files at its next save, and every other design is still a version 2 file, byte for byte. `docs/LYL_PROJECT_FORMAT.md` and the guide (Files and saving, in English, German, French, Dutch, Czech, Slovenian and Russian) describe it; texts in those languages. Unit tests in `projectAttachments.test.ts` and `lylAttachments.test.ts` (types and content checks, names, limits, round trip, version 2 unchanged without files, every refusal, rename on the start page); tried in the editor: uploading two files, the prompt for a name that is taken with Replace and Rename, an invalid name, the question before Remove, and the files still there after a reload and in the package stored in the browser. Not tried in the editor yet: a shared server folder, backup and restore, and duplicating a design. Contributed by @jbuggie.
- **A double click on the workplane centres the view there (#840):** A double click on empty workplane slides the view, eased like a turn of the view cube, until that spot is in the middle of the screen. The spot also becomes the pivot, so the view turns around it from then on; the angle and the distance stay. It counts on the workplane drawn now (on a face, inside its plate) or on the level of the base plate, also beside the plate, nearest first; the camera clamp lets the pivot stay on a spot beside the plate until the house button (`focusReach`, as Center view does). On a body, towards the horizon (more than 9000 mm away), or while a tool takes the clicks (tape measure, notes, picks), nothing moves. With reduced motion the view jumps there. A new setting **Double-click on the workplane centres the view there** (Appearance, on by default; `WorkplaneWorkspaceSettings.doubleClickCentersView`) sits beside the two other double-click switches. The slide is a `PivotSlide` in `viewCubeTransition.ts`, stepped by `stepCameraTurn`, so a drag cuts it short as it does a turn (`panCameraTo` in `cameraRig.ts`). The 3D view's double-click handling moved out of `WorkplaneViewport.tsx` into `useViewportDoubleClick.ts`. Texts in every language; the guide (View and workplane, every language: the mouse table, and the settings paragraph where a language has it) describes it. Unit tests in `doubleClickPan.test.ts`; tried in the editor: a double click on the plate slides the spot to the middle, on a body, after a selection frame and with the setting off nothing moves. Contributed by @gogades.
- **Align in the sketch (#836):** Two circles for a ring could only be centred on each other by hand: a typed width keeps the outline's top left corner, so a resized circle moved off the other's middle. With two or more outlines selected, the sketch's selection bar now has Align left, Centre left and right, Align right, Align top, Centre top and bottom and Align bottom (`alignSketchSelection` in `lib/sketchAlign.ts`). An outline is the selected points that lines join; each moves as a whole, handles with it, and they line up on the frame round all of them; a locked outline stays and the others line up on the locked ones. One undo step. Texts in every language; the guide (Sketches, German and English) describes it. Unit tests in `sketchAlign.test.ts`; tried in the editor: a rectangle moved off a circle and centred both ways lies on it again. Asked for by @lukyanenkomax.
- **A round button locks Select multiple on a touch screen (#843):** A tablet has no Shift key, and selecting several bodies took a long press and Select multiple from its menu. As in Tinkercad, a round button now shows in the lower left as soon as a finger picks a body (`TouchMultiSelectButton`, `showsTouchMultiSelect`). A tap on it locks Select multiple: every further tap on a body adds it to the selection or takes it out, as Shift does, and a tap on empty space keeps the mode and the selection (the finger may turn the view there). A second tap on the button ends the mode, clears the selection and puts the button away; a new body, a tool that takes the taps itself (workplane, tape, align, mirror, split, framing square, face picks ...) and a mouse or a pen end it too. The guide (Tablet and touch, German and English) says so. Unit test in `touchMultiSelect.test.ts`; tried with touch events on three boxes: lock, add two, take one out, a tap on empty space keeps two, a second tap on the button clears it all, and a mouse click shows no button. Contributed by @crazykay (it replaces #839, which held the button with one finger).

## 1.107.0

- **The browser is asked once to keep the designs (a mail from a user):** layerling never called `navigator.storage.persist()`, so every browser was free to clear the designs, thumbnails and fonts first when it ran short of room. With a design in the browser it now asks once (`persistentStorage.ts`, remembered in `layerling.persistentStorageAsked`): Chrome and Edge decide on their own, Firefox asks the person once, Safari grants it only to an installed web app. The designs work either way. The guide's chapter on installing now says why installing helps on Safari: Safari clears the data of a website that has not been visited for seven days, not that of an installed app. Found by a user who lost his designs that way.
- **Soft light (#812):** The view is lit by a strong main light from above at the front right plus a strong even light, and the renderer clips colour at white (no tone mapping), so a face of a dark colour that points up came out nearly white, while faces turned away stayed dark. A new setting **Soft light** (Appearance, off by default; `WorkplaneWorkspaceSettings.softLighting`) adds a light that sits in the camera and shines where it looks (`lights.head` in `viewportScene.ts`), and turns the fixed lights well down (`softSceneLightLevels` in `workplaneGrid.ts`): a face turned towards you is lit, none is burnt out, and the main light stays a little for the shadows. The contrast slider still works with it; Reset light and shadows and Classic look switch it off. Texts in the seven complete languages; the guide (chapter View and workplane, German and English) describes it. Unit tests in `shadingSettings.test.ts`; tried in the editor with a dark red box and a blue cylinder, with and without. Asked for by @VZ-studios.
- **Smooth turns a corner with two handles smooth at once (#347):** A point whose two handles had been broken apart (a corner with handles) stayed as it was when it was set to smooth, with the point's menu or the 3 key: only its name changed, and it took a detour through Corner. The two handles are now lined up through the point in the direction they share, each keeping its own length (`alignedSmoothHandles` in `sketchSmoothHandles.ts`); a point with fewer than two handles is smoothed as before. Unit test in `sketchSmoothHandles.test.ts`; not tried in the editor yet. Reported by @djelliott19.
- **A group keeps its pivot after Edit group (#807):** Closing a group opened with Edit group ([[E]]) built it anew and kept only its name, colour, hole setting, see-through and hidden state, so a rotation pivot set on the group was gone. It now keeps its pivot on the same spot, also when its parts moved while it was open (`carryShapePivot` in `finishOpenGroup`). Tried in the editor through the AI bridge: a group with its pivot at (25, 5, 3) opened, a part moved, closed, the pivot still at (25, 5, 3), and gone again without the fix. Reported by @raradek.
- **The Height field keeps the base of a turned body in place (#808):** A text or box laid on a sloped face and given a new height in the properties slid up or down the face: the Height field kept only the body's lowest point at the same height above the plate. A turned body now grows along its own up axis from the middle of its base, as the handle and the AI bridge already did (`patchWithResizeAnchor` in `viewportResize.ts` uses `heightPatchKeepingBase`); after a pull on the lower handle the middle of the top face stays instead. A body that is not turned keeps its bottom as before. Unit tests in `heightKeepsBase.test.ts` with the text and box from the report (63.43 degrees, 1 to 10 mm); tried in the editor through the Height field: the middle of the base stayed at the same spot for both. Reported by @MethylBromide.
- **Danish: the leave-site hint quotes Chromium's own wording (#809):** `workspace.confirmTabCloseHint` now quotes "Vil du forlade denne side?" instead of a literal translation, as every other language quotes its browser's real dialog. By @Henrikth12.
- **brepjs 20.5.0 (#810):** The CAD library moves from 18.164.3 to 20.5.0, and its `occt-wasm` override is gone, as brepjs now allows `occt-wasm` 5 itself. It brings fixes for freeing native results when a cast or placement fails, boolean options on occt-wasm, and a compound of separate solids kept as one boolean operand. Typecheck, unit tests and all 464 e2e tests pass on the current branch; not tried in the editor yet. By @andymai.
- **Shift steps by the snap grid in a size or position field (#749):** In a clicked size or position field Shift with Up, Down or the mouse wheel jumped ten millimetres, too far for a fine grid. It now steps by the snap grid (5 mm with a 5 mm grid), and by ten steps with the grid off; angles and other fields keep ten steps, Ctrl and Alt are unchanged (`shiftStep` in `wheelNextValue`). Unit test in `wheelValueStep.test.ts`; tried in the editor on a box's length: 20 to 25 with a 5 mm grid, ten more with the grid off. Asked for by @MartinShake.
- **Inserting a design keeps its notes and reference points (#814):** "Insert into this design" (and dropping `.lyl` files on the import window) brought in the bodies of the saved design but threw its notes and reference points away. They now come along the way a custom shape's do (`notesForInsertedShapes`): fresh ids, pinned to the inserted bodies, closed, and added after the notes already in the design, so they are numbered on from them; free notes and reference points come too, as the whole design does. Only as many as the design has room for, with the same message as a custom shape. The insert moved from the editor into `useCustomShapes` beside the custom shape's, both sharing `addInsertedBodies`. The guide (chapter Files and saving, every guide language that has it) says so. Unit test in `myShapes.test.ts`.
- **On a workplane set on a face the sketch shows the bodies as seen from it (#778):** The sketch showed of each body only the face lying on the plane. A plane set on a facet of a round body stands a little askew, so that face was a thin strip at one end; a part of a group behind the plane, such as a cylinder on a capsule, was left out; and a body with no face on the plane was drawn as one rough polygon round all its points, a tower of a box, a cylinder, a sphere and a cone as a single blob. Each body, and each part of a group, now shows its outline as seen from the plane (`sketchReferenceShapeOnWorkplane` with `seen`, `footprintOfPositions` with `asSeen`); the base plate keeps the footprint, and a body with no flat bottom there gets its outline from above instead of the hull too. Unit tests in `sketchReferenceOutline.test.ts`; checked with the design from the report: the capsule (152.5 x 9.5) and the cylinder on it in side view, where only a strip of 3 mm showed before. Reported by @NWoods56.
- **Wall thickness for Gridfinity bins (#455):** A bin's wall was fixed at 0.95 mm, not a whole number of lines for a 0.4 or 0.6 mm nozzle, and too thin for heavy tools or a block with one small pocket. A Gridfinity bin now has **Wall thickness**, from 0.8 mm (two lines of a 0.4 mm nozzle) up to where the walls meet, 0.95 mm to begin with so existing bins stay as they are. The wall is set in from the outside, which never changes, so the bin still fits the baseplate and stacks with any other. The inner corners are rounded by 3.75 mm less the wall and square from a wall of 3.75 mm, and a scooped or divided bin's floor edges are rounded as much. With the lip, a wall over 1.75 mm stops at a flat ledge 0.85 mm under 7 mm per height unit, where shelves and dividers stop, so a stacked bin's feet stay 0.5 mm clear; the floor goes no higher. Where the walls leave less than 1 mm inside, the bin is built solid, and the floor's row is hidden while it is. Finger scoops and label shelves without room are left out and fewer compartments are built and offered; an open side stays open at any wall that leaves an inside. The view, the exports and the exact body agree, so the edge tool and STEP work on it. `layerling_create_shape`, `layerling_update_object` and `layerling_read_scene` take and report `gridfinityWallThickness`. Contributed by @mlugo-apx.
- **Multi-colour SVG: one body per colour (#818):** An SVG drawn in several colours came in as a single body, its colours lost. It now comes in as one body per fill colour (an outline counts by its stroke), each in its colour and in its place in the drawing, a kept page included (`importedColourShapesFromSvg`, `svgColourGroups`); each body is a sketch of its own outlines (`sketchProfileFromSvgPaths`), or a mesh that carries itself, as the file holds every colour. One colour, or more than 12 as a traced picture has, stays one body. Guide (Files and saving, every language) and the welcome page say so. Unit tests in `svgImportColours.test.ts`; tried in the editor with a drawing of a red rectangle and circle and a blue square with a hole: two bodies, red and blue, in their places. Asked for by @microenh.
- **Small holes in an imported mesh are closed before a split, group or cut (#791):** A mesh with an opening anywhere, such as an OBJ figure whose eyes are open cups, was refused by the kernel as a whole, and Split said only "Split kernel error: Not manifold". When the kernel refuses a mesh, layerling now caps its openings and tries again (`closeSmallMeshHoles` in `meshHoleFill.ts`, through `manifoldFromMeshData` in `editorBooleans.ts`): corners are welded by position, the edges only one triangle uses are chained into rims, and a rim is capped by a fan from its centre, turned like the triangles beside it, when it is small (at most a tenth of the mesh's diagonal) or flat (off its plane by at most 5 % of its radius), and no fan triangle folds back. A mesh with an edge three triangles share is left alone. Split, Group, Cut and the AI bridge say how many holes were closed; a large opening whose rim bends stays open, and the message says so in plain words instead of "Not manifold" (`status.holesTooLarge`, in every language). Exports are unchanged. The guide (Solids and holes, German and English) describes it. Unit tests in `meshHoleFill.test.ts`; tried in the editor with the file from the report. Reported by @aloarjr810.
- **Scale by percent shows decimals (#817):** The New size field took a value such as 102.5 % and scaled by exactly that, but then showed it rounded to 103, as its slider moves in whole steps, so decimals looked ignored; and a value between 99.5 and 100.5 counted as 100 and could not be applied. The field now keeps and shows up to two decimals (`decimals` on `EdgeModifierSlider`), whole values still without; the slider keeps whole steps, and only exactly 100 % counts as no change (`isUnchangedPercent`). The status message gives the percentage with two decimals. Unit test in `scaleByPercent.test.ts`. Asked for by @bgrdtruk.
- **Tape measure: typing a length moves a body (#823):** A straight measurement with a body at one end at least now has a number that can be clicked (`editable` on the tape overlay). A typed length moves the body at the second point along the measured line until the distance is that length, as Tinkercad's ruler does; when only the body at the first point is selected, that one moves (`tapeMovableShapeId`). The measuring points stay on the body, so the measurement follows; a whole edge, or both ends on one body, stays a plain number. One undo step. Tried in the editor in a headless browser: two boxes measured top to top at 80.80, typed 100, the second box moved along the line and the measurement read 100.00. Asked for by @danyvanimpe-lab.
- **A new shape snaps to the middle of a face (#823):** A shape placed by click (Cruise) on a body's face now snaps to the middle of that face when the pointer is within a third of the face's reach from it, a corner or edge under the pointer winning; a shape dragged from the library onto a face lands on that face, in its middle (before, it went to the workplane, wherever the pointer was). Alt sets it down freely in both cases. The middle is the area centre of the connected flat face the pointer is on (`planarFaceCentre` in `faceCentre.ts`, cached per triangle), so two separate faces in one plane stay apart. Guide (Shapes and Measuring, every guide language that has them) and the welcome page say so. Unit tests in `faceCentre.test.ts`; tried in the editor in a headless browser: by click near the middle and by dragging, a cylinder on a 40 mm box landed at its centre, far from it and with Alt where the pointer was. Asked for by @danyvanimpe-lab.
- **A quieter start (#819):** `npm run dev` printed two Node warnings about the build scripts loading TypeScript files and webpack's "Critical dependency" warning from brepjs on every compile, which looked like errors. The scripts run with `--disable-warning=MODULE_TYPELESS_PACKAGE_JSON` and the brepjs warning is ignored (`ignoreWarnings` in `next.config.ts`). Reported by @plainpylut.

## 1.106.1

- **store.php checks every export it saves (GHSA-wj5x-5r5v-89r3):** 1.106.0 stopped Apache from serving the `store` folder with a `.htaccess`, but Apache reads that file only where `AllowOverride` allows it, and Debian's default for `/var/www` is `None`; nginx never reads it. So `store.php` now looks at the bytes too (`export_bytes_acceptable`): an SVG may hold only plain drawing elements (`svg`, `g`, `path`, `rect`, `circle`, `ellipse`, `line`, `polyline`, `polygon`, `text`, `tspan`, `title`, `desc`, `metadata`) and no event handler, link, `javascript:`, `url(` or DOCTYPE; PNG, PDF and 3MF must start with their signature and STEP with `ISO-10303-21`; STL, OBJ and DXF may not start with a tag, so a server that sends no type for them cannot have the browser take them for a web page. Anything else is refused with a message. Tried with a local `store.php`: a script, an `onload`, a `foreignObject` and HTML named `.stl`, `.step` or `.png` are refused, and real exports of every format from the editor (STL, 3MF, OBJ, SVG in all three views, the drawing as SVG and PDF, DXF, PNG; STEP's header from the CAD kernel) are saved. The READMEs say how to block the folder on Apache without `AllowOverride` and on nginx.
## 1.106.0

- **The store folder is no longer served directly (GHSA-wj5x-5r5v-89r3):** Since exports can be saved on the server (#779), `store.php` writes them into the `store` folder as they come, and Apache served that folder like any other: an SVG with a script in it, opened by its address, ran on layerling's own address and could reach what a visitor keeps in the browser. The app never opens a file there by its address - projects, preview pictures and exports all go through `store.php`, and a slicer reads the folder on disk - so the `.htaccess` that `store.php` writes into the folder now refuses every direct request (`Require all denied`, `Deny from all` on Apache 2.2); an existing store gets it on its next request. The READMEs say how to do the same on nginx and other servers that ignore `.htaccess`. The Node route and the Docker image keep the folder outside what they serve and are not affected. `store.php` also accepts DXF for "Save to the server", as the Node route already did. Reported privately by @mlugo-apx, with a reproduction and the fix.
- **Align faces puts holes on one axis (#794):** Align faces has a third way, **Same axis**: click a round face of the part that moves (the inside of a hole, the outside of a pin), then a round face of another part, and Align turns the part the shortest way until the two axes are parallel and slides it straight across onto the other axis, keeping its depth along it - a hole over a hole, a pin in a hole. The axis is read from the triangles alone (`roundFaceAxis.ts`: the face grows from the clicked facet over neighbours that turn only a little and stand square to the axis, so a sharp rim, a chamfer or a flat face ends it; the direction is the one all its normals stand square to, the centre a least-squares circle through its corners), so it works on imported meshes as well; a flat face, a cone or a curve that is not part of a cylinder is refused with a message. While pointing, the whole round face lights up; the gap slider is hidden in this mode, and a flat face picked before switching is asked for again. The AI bridge's `layerling_mate_faces` takes `mode: "concentric"` with `facePoint` and `targetFacePoint` (a point on or near each round face, a point on the hole's axis works too) and reports both axes with their diameters. Texts in every language, the guide (chapter Select and arrange, every guide language), the editor's quick guide in every language, the start page, the READMEs, the AI chapter and the three SKILL.md. Unit tests in `roundFaceAxis.test.ts` (a tilted hole far from the origin, a hole with a chamfer and a flat rim, an eight-sided hole, a flat face and a cone refused, a hole found from its axis through a thin plate and a point on the plate refused, the motion itself); tried in the editor: a pin tilted by 25 degrees clicked onto a bigger cylinder stood upright on its axis at its old height, and through the AI bridge a pin tilted by 30 degrees landed on the axis of a hole in a grouped plate. Asked for by @Grassmedia.
- **The arrow keys change a number (#749):** In a number field of the properties you have clicked, [Up] and [Down] now change the value by one step, with Shift a larger step and with Alt a finer one, like the mouse wheel already did (#226), and the value stays selected so you can go on typing. Until now the keys only moved the text cursor. Asked for by @MartinShake.
- **Shift+N shows or hides the notes and points:** The notes and reference points could only be shown or hidden with **Show notes and points** in the Visibility menu (the arrow beside the eye icon). [[Shift]]+[[N]] now does the same, in the 3D editor and in the sketch editor, with the same message at the bottom; [[N]] alone still places a note. [[Ctrl]]+[[N]] was not used because the browser keeps it for a new window. The key is in the shortcut list (Organise group in 3D, Sketch group in a sketch, with a new text `shortcuts.toggleNotes` in every language), beside the entry in the command search, where the sketch editor now has the entry too, and in the guide (chapters Sketches and Measuring and notes, every guide language that has them). Tried in the editor: in 3D and in an open sketch the key hides and shows the notes and points with the message, N alone still places a note, Ctrl+N stays with the browser, and the command search names Shift+N in both. Contributed by @gogades.
- **A dragged reference point snaps (#799):** A reference point you drag, one marked in the middle of a measurement for example, went wherever the pointer was and snapped to nothing, so it could not be put exactly on a corner or an edge. It now snaps to the corners of a body's box, the middles of its 12 edges and the centres of its 6 faces (turned with the body), to a real corner of its mesh and to the other points, the nearest within 10 pixels, and otherwise onto an edge line near the pointer (`snapDraggedPoint` in `WorkplaneViewport.tsx`, `boxSnapSpots` in `referencePoints.ts`); [[Alt]] held lets it go free. Shapes snapped to points before and still do. The guide (chapter Measuring and notes, German and English) says so. Unit test in `referencePoints.test.ts`; tried in the editor: a point dragged over a 20 mm cube landed on its corners and edge middles, with Alt where the pointer was. Reported by @makinglayerschannel.
- **A ruler stood up measures along its own direction (#799):** The ruler only knew its turn about the vertical axis. Stood up or tipped, it kept measuring along the plate in its old direction: upright beside a 40 x 20 x 20 box turned by 3 degrees it showed 40.99 instead of the height of 20. `rulerDimensionMatch` now takes the ruler's whole orientation and the height of both middles, so a ruler measures along its axis wherever it points; the number of a tipped ruler stands beside it in the view instead of above the plate, and can be typed when a body axis runs along it. The plus handle that places a copy along the ruler is only there while the ruler lies flat, since the copy lands on the plate. A flat ruler measures as before. Unit tests in `rulerDimensions.test.ts`; tried in the editor (flat 40.00, turned 3 degrees 40.99, stood up 20.00). Reported by @makinglayerschannel.
- **Video list:** Werkplaats Marc's video is replaced by his layerling playlist, which also holds his first tutorial on sketches (tracing a photo, revolve and sweep); Printology's video by his playlist "Layerling CAD Videos" (with a second video on what layerling does beyond Tinkercad), and Print N Repeat's two videos by his playlist "Print by Layer" (tutorials on hinges and threads). New videos in them arrive without a release. In the start page list, the guide in every language, the READMEs and the first post of Discussion #296.
- **Danish brought up to date (#798):** 58 new interface texts, and with #803 the four that came with 1.105.0 (`status.computing`, `status.computeTimedOut`, `workspace.confirmTabClose`, `workspace.confirmTabCloseHint`), so Danish covers every text, and the Danish guide follows the English one in every chapter, with the new chapter on tablet and touch. Contributed by @Henrikth12.
- **A chamfer on the Gridfinity lip (#780):** The top of a bin's stacking lip is a sharp edge, as the specification draws it, and taking it off meant picking its edges with the edge tool on every bin. A Gridfinity bin with the lip now has **Lip chamfer**, from 0 to 1 mm in steps of 0.05, 0 to begin with so existing bins stay sharp. It is the edge tool's chamfer of that size built in, that much off the outer wall and off the slope inside, in the view, the exports and the exact body. Beside an open side the lip's end stays square. A bin stacked on top rests on that slope right up to the top, so the chamfer takes away the top 0.25 mm of the 2.69 mm it rests on at a chamfer of 0.25 mm, and the top 1 mm at 1 mm; the bin still sits 0.35 mm down in the lip and stacks as high as before. The bin keeps its height setting, and its top comes down by 0.71 times the chamfer, 0.18 mm at 0.25 mm and 0.71 mm at 1 mm. The chamfer is built to 0.01 mm. `layerling_create_shape`, `layerling_update_object` and `layerling_read_scene` take and report `gridfinityLipChamfer`. Asked for by @PJMCL1618033 in #780. Contributed by @mlugo-apx.
- **Center on workplane works on a face (#805):** With the workplane laid on a face, "Center on workplane" still centred the selection on the base plate, so a half sphere set on the slope of a wedge slid off it. It now measures the selection along the workplane's own axes (`projectShapesExtent`) and moves it only along them, so its middle lands on the point picked with [[W]] and it keeps its distance from the face (`faceWorkplaneCenteringShift` in `workplaneCentering.ts`); on the base plate nothing changes. The guide (chapter Select and arrange, German and English) says so. Unit test in `workplaneCentering.test.ts`; tried in the editor: a half sphere pushed 9 mm along the ridge and 6 mm up the slope came back onto the origin, 5 mm off the face as before. Reported by @MethylBromide.
- **Security and library updates:** Next.js 15.5.28 closes two advisories in 15.5.27 (GHSA-2464-fhr4-w9xw for the image optimizer with SVG, GHSA-p3m5-59mp-jv5r for server actions); layerling turns image optimization off and has no server actions, so the published site and the Docker image were not open to them, but scanners no longer flag the image. vitest 4.1.12 and vite 8.3.5 for the same reason among the development tools (`npm audit` finds nothing now), brepjs 18.164.3 and three-mesh-bvh 0.9.16 within their ranges.
- **Docs caught up:** The German guide's AI chapter and the German and Dutch READMEs have the section on Gemini and Antigravity the English ones have, and the READMEs list DXF among the imports and exports (in every README language). Two interface texts no code used any more (`status.sketchChainCleared`, `notice.opened`) and three unused functions are gone.

## 1.105.0

- **Ask before closing the tab (a setting):** In the settings, under the same group as "Save STL as a download", "Ask before closing the tab" makes the browser bring up its own question "Leave site?" when a tab with an open design is closed or reloaded (`beforeunload`; the wording is the browser's and cannot be changed, and there is no "Save" in it). Off by default: a design is saved after every change anyway, so the question is only a reminder, as with Ctrl+W by mistake. A reload layerling starts itself, such as "Save and reload" for a new version, skips it (`leaveGuard.ts`). Texts in nine languages and a sentence in the first chapter of the guide in all of them. Asked for by a user in a mail.
- **Open DXF files (#774):** The import window, the start page and the AI bridge's `layerling_import_file` read DXF, the counterpart of the DXF export: a part outline from a laser cutting shop or a supplier comes in as parts to work on: every solid outline a sketch body 4 mm high (its points editable, its fill changeable), every hole in it a loose hole of its own beside it - a whole circle as a cylinder hole of its diameter, any other outline as a sketch hole - so a hole is moved and sized in the 3D editor like any other and not only in the sketch; loose holes cut the body as they always do (#203), in the view and in every export. A hole with an outline lying inside it (a part in a cut-out) stays in its body's sketch, as a loose hole would cut that part too. Such a hole is as high as its plate, so the ray meets both at once: a press there now picks the hole even with the plate selected (`viewportPicking.ts`; until now the selected body won such a tie, #484, and a flush hole could not be picked to move it). `dxfImport.ts` reads ASCII DXF of any version: LINE, ARC, CIRCLE, ELLIPSE, LWPOLYLINE and POLYLINE with their bulges, SPLINE (each knot span as a cubic piece through its ends with their tangents, exact for a non-rational spline of degree 3 or less, a rational one in four pieces per span; fit points alone as a smooth curve through them) and INSERT (blocks nested, moved, scaled, turned, mirrored and in arrays), in model space and on layers that are on and thawed; an entity drawn with its extrusion down (a mirrored arc) is turned the right way, one standing off the drawing is left out. Arcs become cubic pieces of a quarter at most, as the SVG import makes them, so the sketch builds them back into true arcs (`sketchCircularArc.ts`). Loose pieces are joined end to end within 0.01 mm, a piece drawn twice counts once, and what does not close is left out; text, dimensions, hatches and the like are left out too, and the message counts both. `$INSUNITS` gives the unit (inches, feet, cm, m ... converted to mm); a file without one, such as any R12, is read as millimetres and the message says so. A binary DXF is refused with a word on saving it as ASCII. The outline is centred, the drawing's y running to the back, so the DXF export's file comes back as it went out (tested). Texts in twelve languages, the guide's import list (also in Danish, Arabic and both Chinese guides), the AI chapter and the three SKILL.md. Unit tests on files written by ezdxf (loose lines and arcs in R12, a polyline with bulges and an ellipse, inches, blocks in arrays with a layer switched off, a mirrored arc and a spline) and on one from layerling's DXF export (`dxfImport.test.ts`, also how a drawing is taken apart into bodies, sketch holes and cylinders, and a hole with a part inside it kept in its sketch); all of them imported in the editor through the AI bridge. Asked by @plazmabokor on GitHub.
- **A design file dropped on the import window is inserted (#683):** The drop zone in the import window took a single `.lyl` file as a new design and refused several at once. Since a large Tinkercad import now comes as several `.lyl` files, a `.lyl` or `.skf` file (or several) dropped on the zone is inserted into the open design, as "Insert into this design" does, one after the other. Opening one as a new design stays with its own button above the zone; a drop that has other files among the `.lyl` ones goes the old way. Asked for by @HopGabor62.
- **DXF export finds circles and arcs on wobbly outlines (#774):** A sketch body's outline - a DXF read back in, a drawn curve - has corners a few thousandths of a millimetre off the curve and unevenly spaced, and the arc check gave up on them: a hole came out as many small arcs instead of one circle. `dxfExport.ts` now checks the direction round the fitted centre and lets steps back within 0.01 mm pass, looks past a short run that fails (three corners a few thousandths off give a poor circle where a longer run holds), and keeps a run whose corners lie on a straight line within 0.01 mm a line rather than an arc of a circle metres wide. A unit test with a wobbly circle and a wobbly rounded rectangle (`dxfExport.test.ts`). The guide's DXF row is now also in Danish, Arabic and both Chinese guides; the Brazilian Portuguese guide has no files chapter yet. Asked by @plazmabokor on GitHub.
- **Preview pictures show all bodies, however far you have zoomed:** The preview of a design in the overview, in the shared copy and on the server was a shrunken copy of the editor's screen, so it depended on the zoom the work was done in: a close-up showed a cut-off detail, zooming far out left tiny bodies in the corner of the plate. For the picture the view is now fitted to all bodies for a moment, from the side the view looks from, and put back at once; the screen does not change. A design without bodies keeps the screen as it is. Suggested by Jürgen Keller.
- **Cutting and splitting large meshes no longer freezes the editor:** Grouping with holes, merging imported meshes and Split now run in a worker (`workers/geometry.worker.ts`, `lib/geometryWorker.ts`); the shapes cross as transferred Float64Arrays (`lib/geometryTransfer.ts`), so the result is the same to the bit. The longest freeze while cutting a cylinder out of a 200,000-triangle sphere went from 1.9 s to about 0.4 s, splitting a 300,000-triangle sphere from 3.9 s to about 0.4 s; meanwhile the status line says "Calculating… you can keep working". If the worker cannot start (an old offline copy, another build, no worker support) or fails, the work runs on the page as before; text stays on the page. Measured with `scripts/measure-main-thread.mjs`, see `docs/PERFORMANCE_MAIN_THREAD.md`.
- **Less waiting after a cut:** The edge lines of a body are worked out with numbers instead of a text key per corner (`lib/fastEdgesGeometry.ts`, the same lines as three.js's EdgesGeometry, about five times quicker), the parts behind "Separate parts" are counted the same way (`lib/meshPartCount.ts`), and Split asks the worker whether the plane crosses an object. Splitting four imported 100,000-triangle spheres froze the editor for 1.2 s after step 1, now for 0.75 s.
- **The new mesh of a cut is drawn without a pause:** The geometry worker also builds what the view draws of the meshes it makes - the geometry and its raycasting index (`lib/importedMeshDisplay.ts`), the same as the viewport builds them - and the viewport takes them over; the normals of a mesh without stored normals are worked out once instead of twice. The longest freeze after splitting a 300,000-triangle sphere went from 0.46 s to 0.11 s, after cutting a cylinder out of a 200,000-triangle sphere from 0.39 s to 0.21 s.
- **Import, 3MF export and the print estimate block the editor less:** STL and OBJ files are read in the geometry worker (also a stored STL when a .lyl opens), the 3MF export is packed by fflate's workers (the same bytes), and the print estimate welds the mesh with numbers instead of a text key per corner (the same result). For 300,000 triangles the editor waits 0.8 instead of 1.1 s for the estimate and 1.1 instead of 2.3 s for the 3MF export; importing an OBJ through the file chooser blocks the main thread 3.7 instead of 5.7 s in all. What is left there is the new design the import starts: compiling the view's shaders and its preview picture. 3MF files are still read on the page (they need DOMParser).
- **Print estimate and Simplify no longer freeze the editor:** Both run in the geometry worker (text stays on the page), and Simplify calls off a value the slider has already left. The print estimate of a 300,000-triangle import kept a click waiting 0.78 s, now 0.04 s; simplifying it 0.42 s, now 0.08 s. The section view welds its mesh with numbers instead of a text key per corner (`lib/gridWeld.ts`, shared with the estimate and the part count, the same result): switching it on keeps a click waiting 0.47 instead of 0.73 s.

## 1.104.0

- **Opening a project no longer starts hundreds of compression workers (#786):** Opening a `.lyl` file saves the project once before the editor appears, at compression level 1, and the zip library started one worker for every entry of at least 160,000 bytes, so a project with many mesh assets could launch hundreds at once. A streaming zip writer now runs at most two compression jobs at a time; small entries stay synchronous, level 0 stays uncompressed, entry order and timestamps are kept, and a worker error stops the save and ends the remaining work. This is not a fix for the memory errors some very large imports show. Contributed by @taylormadearmy.
- **Deeply nested groups no longer run the browser out of memory (#683):** A design with groups nested many levels deep, such as a Tinkercad import (a 12 MB file with 667 bodies in 19 levels), crashed the tab a few seconds after opening. The editor builds a key for every group to know when its look or shape has changed, and each level of nesting put the key of the level below into its own as text, which doubles the escape characters every time: at 19 levels one key was 346 MB. The keys of the parts are now appended as they are, so the key stays small. Found with the files of @HopGabor62.
- **DXF for laser cutting shops (#774):** The export window and the AI bridge's `layerling_export_mesh` write DXF next to SVG, with the views Silhouette from above and Footprint on the workplane (the visible-edges view would turn inner edges into cuts, so DXF has none). It is built the way laser cutting shops ask for it, from the file rules shops publish, confirmed by a CO2 laser user: only LINE, ARC and CIRCLE entities - no polylines, splines or blocks, and no curves made of short straight pieces, which make a cutting head shake - everything on layer 0 in colour 7 (white), DXF 2000 (AC1015) with `$INSUNITS` = 4, so millimetres stand in the file (R12 has no unit, and some programs read it as inches), Z = 0, scale 1:1, seen from above with the front at the bottom, the lower left corner at 0,0. The bodies' outlines are joined into one area first (`CrossSection.union`), so bodies that touch or overlap give one contour and no line is cut twice; every contour is closed, its pieces meeting end to end. The editor's outlines are polygons, so `dxfExport.ts` finds the round parts again: a whole loop on one circle becomes a CIRCLE, a run of corners on one circle an ARC through its two end corners. For a hole or a fillet this is the true circle, since the polygon's corners sit on it (a 5.5 mm hole comes out as a circle of 5.502 mm: the editor cuts holes a thousandth of a millimetre wider each side, `CUTTER_LATERAL_PADDING`); a free curve such as a letter gets arcs within 0.01 mm. A side counts as part of a curve only when it strays no more than 0.05 mm from the arc (`EXACT_ROUND_TOLERANCE`, as for the edge tool), so a hexagon or a star stays straight lines. The message names the numbers of circles, arcs and lines; a footprint outline that does not close is left out and counted. "Save to the server" works for DXF as well, as for the other formats (#779). The guide's table of formats has a row for it, the AI chapter, the three SKILL.md copies and texts in twelve languages. Unit tests for circles, rounded rectangles, slots, polygons that stay straight, a free curve within the tolerance, closed chains, the file's version, units, layer, colour, handles and orientation (`dxfExport.test.ts`); tried in the editor through the AI bridge and the export window (filleted plate with round, hexagonal and square holes; a plate with holes, a slot and text), the files checked with ezdxf (audit without errors, no open ends, fillets as arcs of 6.000 mm) and against the SVG of the same design (every corner within 0.01 mm). Not yet tried on a laser cutter: a shop's cutting test follows, and the guide says so. Asked by @plazmabokor on GitHub.
- **A group shows its real outline under the sketch (#778):** A group of bodies, say a round plate and a gear, was drawn under the sketch as a plain box of its width and depth, so a plate and a gear showed up as a square. It now shows the outline of each of its parts, the round plate and the gear, and snaps to them. Reported by @NWoods56.
- **The revolve preview can be reset (#781):** Since the 3D preview of a revolve can be panned, the shape could be moved out of sight with no way back. A small button in the top right corner of the preview and a double click on it now put the camera back to the first view of the shape; the view you set is still kept while you edit the sketch, until you reset it. The button's tooltip is a new sentence (`sketch.revolvePreviewReset`), written in English, German, Dutch, French, Russian, Czech and Slovenian. Contributed by @AbhiRoutray21.
- **Alt and the arrow keys move in fine steps (#749):** Shift with an arrow key moves a body by five grid steps; Alt with an arrow key now does the opposite and moves it by a fifth of a grid step, so with a 1 mm grid it is 0.2 mm. It works on the workplane and in the sketch (points, lines, image and curve handles), and the keyboard shortcut list names it. With Shift and Alt held, Shift wins. Asked for by @KimA998.
- **Export files can be saved on the server (#779):** When the server folder is on, the export window has "Save to the server" for STL, 3MF, OBJ, STEP, SVG, PNG and the drawing as well, not only for the design file. The file is written into the folder of the open design (or the folder open on the start page), so a program that shares it, such as a slicer in Docker, can open it. It does not show in the list of designs; a file of the same name is replaced only by a second press on the button. Asked for by @nekoprog.
- **The framing square's numbers no longer sit on each other (#777):** With a body right at the corner of the framing square the three green distance fields (X, Y, Z) fell on the same spot and covered each other and the square's buttons. They now step aside where they would overlap, and stay at the middle of their arrows where there is room. Each number also starts with a small x, y or z, the axis it belongs to, so it can be read without hovering over the field. Reported by @willi1955.
- **Search the keyboard shortcuts:** The shortcuts window has a search box at the top. It keeps only the shortcuts whose sentence, group or keys contain every word typed, in the language of the interface and under the key names it shows (Cmd and Option on a Mac); capital letters and accents do not matter, "ctrl+z" finds the combination, and a single letter or digit finds that key and the words starting with it rather than every word that holds the letter. Groups left empty disappear, and a search that finds nothing says so. The X at the end of the box empties it, and with the box empty the whole list is back. The box has the focus when the window opens (not on a touch screen, where that would bring up the keyboard) and stays in place while the list scrolls; the Print button prints what the window shows. The window's styles moved from `globals.css` to `shortcuts.css`, which the Graphite theme reads too. Texts in every interface language; the guide (chapter Keyboard shortcuts, every guide language) describes it. Unit test in `shortcutsSearch.test.ts` (jsdom); not tried in a real browser yet.
- **Notes on sketches:** The sketch editor has a Manage section with the note tool; it and the N key place a note anywhere on the sketch, with the same pins and cards as in 3D (`SketchNotes.tsx`, reusing `NoteOverlay`). A sketch's notes are its body's: finishing pins them to the body (on the top of an extrusion or a sweep, on the drawn profile of a revolve, in the middle of its turn), and opening the sketch again brings the body's notes back onto it, those placed in 3D included; a note placed on a side in 3D keeps its height, a turned one its angle (`sketchNotes.ts`). While the sketch is open the notes travel in the sketch profile (`SketchProfile.notes`, never stored on a body), so the sketch's own undo and Cancel cover them. The N key is in the sketch shortcuts and the note tool in the sketch command search. The note callbacks moved out of `LayerlingEditor.tsx` into `useEditorNotes.ts` and the note styles out of `globals.css` into `notes.css` (added to `GRAPHITE_SOURCES`; the generated Graphite theme only changes order). The guide (chapters Sketches and Measuring and notes) describes it in every language that has those chapters, and the What's-new card is in all the program's languages. Unit tests in `sketchNotes.test.ts`.

## 1.103.1

- **Sketch: the keys 1 to 3 also change a selection (#347):** With the Select tool and lines or points selected, 1 makes the lines straight and the points corners, 2 turns the lines into curves and 3 makes the points smooth, all selected ones at once and as one step in the history, as in Tinkercad. With nothing selected the keys pick the drawing tool as before; the keys are named in the tooltips of the buttons. Asked for by @djelliott19.
- **Several objects turn rigidly, even past the edge (#763):** Turning several selected objects with the rotate handle rounded each centre to the grid and kept it inside the workplane, so objects near the edge stopped while the rest went on and the arrangement broke. The group now turns exactly around its pivot and keeps its spacing. Reported by @MethylBromide.
- **Fit a Gridfinity baseplate to a drawer (#455):** Filling a drawer meant working out the units and the paddings by hand. A baseplate now has **Drawer width** and **Drawer depth**, each showing the drawer the plate fills, 0.5 mm larger than the plate. Typing a drawer's inside size takes as many units as fit, up to 6, and splits the rest evenly into the two paddings of that axis, up to 41 mm a side, so the plate comes out 0.5 mm smaller than the drawer: 292.1 mm gives 6 units and 19.8 mm each side. Only the axis typed changes, in one undo step; a section of a larger drawer padded on one side only is set with the padding rows. Nothing new is stored: the rows are worked out from the size, like the units. A padded baseplate's size is now kept to 0.01 mm, as its padding is. Contributed by @mlugo-apx.

## 1.103.0

- **The sketch follows the unit setting (#757):** With Imperial (or any other unit) chosen in the workplane settings, the sketch editor still read and typed in millimetres. Its dimension labels, the measure tool, the line preview, the selection size, image sizes and the typed fields (line length, dimension, width and height, fillet and chamfer size, image size and calibration, image position) now use the workspace unit, inches as fractions or decimals as set; the sketch itself stays in millimetres. Reported by @radomnik.
- **Save STL as a download (#731):** In Chrome and Edge the STL export asks once where to save and then writes into the same file again, so an export never gets a number like a plain download does (`name (1).stl`, as from Tinkercad). A new setting `stlAsDownload` (Appearance, "Save STL as a download", off by default; `WorkplaneWorkspaceSettings`, `normalizeWorkspaceSettings`) makes the STL export skip the linked file (`LayerlingEditor.tsx`): it is handed over as a plain download, so Chrome numbers a name it has already used, and the export window no longer names a linked file. The texts are in the languages that are complete, the others show English. The guide (chapter Files and saving, German and English) names it. Unit test in `workplaneSettings.test.ts`. Asked by @KimA998 on GitHub.
- **Back to your own settings with one click (#754):** A file you open brings its own colours, grid and plate, so a design from the Tinkercad importer looked different from yours, and "Reset grid" goes back to the app's standard, not to yours. Next to "Make default" in the settings there is now **Use my default** (shown once a default is saved): it puts the colours, grid, shadows, camera and shape starts you saved on the open design and leaves the size of its plate, the printer and the units as they are. Suggested by @grmonsey.
- **Loft: segments of a circle (#755):** The circles and rounded corners of a Loft were always drawn with 96 straight pieces per full circle in the view and in STL, OBJ and 3MF, with no way to change that; on a large round end the facets showed. A new field, **Segments per circle** (16 to 360, 96 to begin with), sets it. It shows when an end is round or has a rounded corner, and it is saved with the shape, in the shape defaults and in the AI bridge (`loftSegments`). The exact model for the edge tool and STEP was a true circle already. Asked by @WimSnor.
- **A scaled star keeps its shape (#761):** Pulling a star's corner handle changed its outer size but not its inner size, so a smaller star came out less pointy and a larger one more pointy. The inner size and the roundings now stretch with the outer size when the star is scaled by a handle or its size mark, by a percentage, or as part of a group that is made larger and ungrouped. Typing a value into the properties still sets just that value. Reported by @MethylBromide.
- **Scale by percent from the keyboard (#760):** The percentage field now has the focus when the window opens, and [[Enter]] in it applies the scale, so [[Ctrl]]+[[K]], the percentage and [[Enter]] do it without the mouse. Before, Enter only took the value and the field had to be clicked first. The number field of the tool windows has the two as options (`autoFocus`, `onEnter`), so other windows can follow one at a time. Suggested by @foofoodog.
- **The material estimate is worked out on a click (#734):** The Material box in the export window calculated its numbers every time the window opened and every time you changed the format, which for a finely traced multicolour model took about as long as the export itself, and the Export button stayed out of reach meanwhile. The box now shows a **Calculate** button and works out volume, weight and filament only when you press it; a change to the design or the format asks again. `layerling_estimate_print` is unchanged. Reported by @steveeglon, with @gogades.
- **Ungrouping a scaled group keeps a cone's radius (#756):** A cone grouped with other parts and then made larger kept its new height after Ungroup, but its base radius went back to the old value, so the cone came out narrower than the group showed. The radii now stretch with the cone's width. Reported by @rileymake.
- **The revolve preview turns with the mouse (#743):** In the sketch with Revolve the small 3D preview was drawn once from a fixed camera. It now turns with the left or the right button, zooms with the wheel (also on a wheel that sends pages or lines) and moves with the wheel button, and the view you set stays while you keep drawing. Contributed by @AbhiRoutray21.
- **Face tool: the new part as a body of its own (#750):** The Face window has a switch, "As a body of its own (not grouped)". With it on, what is pushed out of the face (or the hole of a pocket) is created beside the body instead of in a group with it and comes up selected, ready to be resized, moved or used to cut; without it everything stays as before, grouped. `layerling_push_face` has the same as `separate`. The switch is `separate` in `FaceToolSettings`; the editor's Create and the MCP command both commit the body alone when it is on. Type-checked and tested; not tried in a browser yet. Asked for by @nnicolas50-max in #750.
- **Ctrl+arrows slide the sketch view:** In the sketch editor, with nothing selected (no point, line, image or clicked curve handle) and no drag under way, Ctrl or Cmd with an arrow key slides the view the way the arrow points, a tenth of the view a press and half of it with Shift as well, as they do in the 3D view; the view stops at the edge of the plate as it does when panning with the mouse. The steps come from `viewArrowStep` in `viewArrowKeys.ts`, which the 3D view uses too. The two rows for it are in the sketch group of the shortcut list, and the guide (chapter Sketches, every guide language) names it. Type-checked; not tried in a browser yet.
- **Padding for Gridfinity baseplates (#455):** Drawers are rarely a whole number of 42 mm units, and a baseplate of whole units slid about in the gap that was left. A baseplate now has **Padding at the back**, **Padding at the front**, **Padding on the left** and **Padding on the right**, from 0 to 41 mm each: a solid border at the plate's 5 mm height outside the grid, with the outer corners rounded as before. The grid stays whole units and moves away from the padded sides. The plate's size is the whole plate with its padding, so a change of padding changes the size with it and keeps the units, and dragging a side snaps the units and keeps the padding. A drawer larger than the print bed takes several baseplates, each padded on the sides that face the drawer's walls. The plate stays an exact body for the edge tool and the STEP export. `layerling_create_shape`, `layerling_update_object` and `layerling_read_scene` take and report `gridfinityPaddingBack`, `gridfinityPaddingFront`, `gridfinityPaddingLeft` and `gridfinityPaddingRight`. A bin keeps the settings and ignores them. Contributed by @mlugo-apx.

## 1.102.0

- **The 3MF and OBJ export weld points much faster (#734):** A design of four colours traced from a picture (139,000 triangles) kept the page busy for seconds while the file was written, and on a slow computer Chrome asked whether to wait. `weldMeshVertices` looked in 27 neighbouring cells of one tolerance each, with a string key per cell, for every point; it now looks in cells 16 tolerances wide and only in the next one when the point is that close to its edge, with a number as the key. The welding of that model went from about 1.1 s to 0.04 s, writing the 3MF from 1.75 s to 0.6 s on the test computer. The result is the same set of points (`tests/unit/meshWeld.test.ts` holds it against the old search). The colour separation and the size estimate still run in the page and take their time; moving them to a worker is not done.
- **A fillet no longer draws a strip over the bay of an imported part (#740):** The top edges of an imported disc with a thin horn were filleted at 0.8 mm; the kernel returned a body it calls valid whose flat top face is meshed across the bay, a thin strip outside the disc (at 0.79 mm and below it was clean). The face has one straight, simple outline of 250 corners, so its mesh has to cover exactly the area of that polygon. `cadFlatFaceMeshStraysFromOutline` (`cadFaceOutline.ts`) measures both for every flat face with a straight outline and no hole, and the worker refuses a fillet or chamfer after which they differ, with the message that the size is too large for the edge ("intersects adjacent faces"), as it does for a body the kernel calls invalid. Kernel test in `cadFlatFaceStrip.e2e.ts` with the reported disc as `tests/fixtures/disc-with-bay-740.stl`: sizes up to 0.7 mm leave it as it is, and for every size from 0.3 to 0.9 mm the check says "strays" exactly when the mesh has triangles outside the disc. Reported by @tracy216 on GitHub.
- **Sweep: put an edge of the section on the path (#732):** The Sweep sketch always centred the cross-section on the path, so a rail that had to run along the edge of a part needed the path moved by half the section's width. A menu next to the mode badge in the sketch now sets `sweepAnchor` on the sketch (`center`, the default and left out of the file, `left` or `right`, as seen looking along the path); `sweepAnchorX` in `cadSketchSweep.ts` picks the matching x of the section. It is saved in the `.lyl` (`sketchProfile.sweepAnchor`), carried over by every sketch edit like the stroke, and `layerling_sweep_sketch` takes `anchor`. Texts in English, German, Czech and Dutch; the other languages show the English until translated.
- **A bent tube keeps its stretch when a setting changes (#737):** A bent tube pulled larger or smaller with its handles went back to the size its settings give it as soon as you changed the inner profile, the wall or a segment. It now keeps the stretch. Reported by @PeterSpie in #737.
- **SVG visible edges: rounded edges get an outline and the outlines close (#591):** Two things went wrong in "Visible edges from above" with a laser (xTool Studio). A rounded top edge had no outline from above, because the fillet runs smoothly into the wall below it and no edge there is sharp: an edge between a face towards the view and one running along it now counts as a silhouette too (`drawingProjection`, so the 2D drawing's top view as well). And some outlines stayed open, so the program could not fill them: loose ends of a body's lines within 0.02 mm are joined and loose stubs shorter than that left out (`joinedLooseEnds`, `bodyLines`), and the overlap tolerance goes from 0.5 to 5 micrometres, so the top and bottom rims of a bore through a rounded top count as one line. Unit tests in `svgExport.test.ts` (an arch with a half-round top, a block with a narrow V notch). Contributed by @DevxDre.
- **A fillet or chamfer on a tapered body with a lean or a twist no longer shifts it (#733):** The edge tool's result is a finished mesh that already holds the taper, the twist and the lean, but `shapeFromCadMesh` (and the bake into a mesh in `LayerlingEditor.tsx`) cleared only the taper fields, so a box tapered and leaned to get one straight side came out of the fillet leaning a second time - the same went for a twist. `extrudeTwist`, `extrudeTopOffsetX` and `extrudeTopOffsetZ` are cleared with the taper now. Unit test in `cadResultKeepsNoLean.test.ts`; the exact loft itself was already checked against the display mesh for this case (width 60, bottom 50, offset 5). Reported by @Eggberry on GitHub.
- **Reference images to line parts up on (#680):** The sketch had a reference image, the 3D editor had none, so a photo of a circuit board could not be laid on the floor of its case to place the standoffs, holes and connector cut-outs on. The import window now has **Reference image**: a PNG, JPG, WebP, GIF or BMP picture lands on the workplane - the plate, or the face the workplane was set on - with its longer side 100 mm and half see-through, selected, and is moved, turned and resized like any shape. It is built on the old picture plate (`imagePlate`, a thin box with the picture on top, now 0.2 mm), so designs that hold one open as before. It is a guide, not a body: `isNonSolidShape` counts it with the ruler, so it is never part of an export (STL, 3MF, OBJ, STEP, SVG, also the section and the drawing: `visibleExportShapes` leaves it out), a group, bundle, cut or intersection, the print estimate or the build volume warning.
