A body in front of it is picked first; a locked one lets clicks through to the plate and is picked in the object list. It is mirrored left-right and front-back, never across the axis it faces along, which would turn the picture to the back of its plate (that handle is left out). Its card has **See-through (%)** (stored as `imagePlate.opacity`, 0.1 to 1; an old plate without one stays solid, a file with a bad one is refused) and **Calibrate**, as in the sketch: two clicks on the picture - through anything in front of it - and the real distance between them; the picture keeps its proportions and its middle. The picture is kept once in the .lyl file as a project asset (assets are stored by their hash, so copies and undo states share it), and the message when it is added names its size from 2 MB up. The MCP action `layerling_reference_image` adds one from base64, moves, turns, calibrates (two points in the picture's pixels and their distance), fades and locks it; the three SKILL.md copies, the AI chapter (all languages) and a new section of the chapter Measuring and notes (EN, DE, HU, NL, FR, CS, RU, SL, ES, PL, DA, AR, ZH-CN, ZH-TW, with the "?" of the card; the texts in those languages too) describe it, and the start page names it (EN/DE). Unit tests for the export exclusion, the .lyl round trip, calibration and the AI bridge's argument checks; tried in the editor (headless Chromium). Asked for by @plazmabokor on GitHub.
- **A hollowed body that was turned stretches the right way (#729):** A hollowed body is built again when it is resized, from the shape it was hollowed from. When the body had been turned by 90 degrees and baked, its width and depth had changed places while that shape kept its pose, so a change of the depth stretched the width. The stretch is now read in the axes of the shape it was hollowed from. Reported by @NWoods56 in #729.
- **Links in running text look like links:** The page resets every link to the colour and underline of its surroundings, so the "Export to layerling" link in the Tinkercad box of the start page read as plain text. The links inside sentences - there, in the welcome panel's video list, the what's-new note, the guide window's video list and the help window's further answers - are underlined now, in the accent colour (lighter on the dark and graphite grounds), with a small arrow for "opens in a new tab". The footer and the buttons are unchanged.
- **The SVG footprint is cut at the workplane (#727):** With View "Footprint on the workplane" the cut lay a hair above the lowest point of all bodies, so one part reaching below the workplane moved it down there and the parts standing on the workplane were missing from the file. It is now cut a hair above the workplane whenever a body reaches through it; with all bodies above or below it, it stays at the lowest point. Reported by @DevxDre in #727.
## 1.101.0

- **SVG export: the edges seen from above (#591, PR #726 by DevxDre):** Under View the SVG export has a third choice next to the silhouette and the footprint, "Visible edges from above": the edges you see from above as lines in the colour of their body, pockets, engraved and raised text and parts standing on others included, every area closed with an even-odd fill, for laser cutters and plotters. A design with too many edges gets the silhouette, and the message says so. The MCP tool `layerling_export_mesh` takes `view: "edges"` too. Texts for every language; the guide describes it in "Files and saving".
- **Take your Tinkercad designs with you (#683):** A member of the community made a free bookmark, Export to layerling, that turns a Tinkercad design into a `.lyl` file with its real shapes. It is a project of its own, not part of layerling, and we have not tested it ourselves yet. The start page ("Know Tinkercad?") and the guide (Files and saving, Importing, and the tip in Getting started) point to it in every language, and a card in "What's new" mentions it.
- **The mouse wheel zooms with "scroll one screen at a time" (Edge on Windows) and in Firefox:** A wheel that counts in pages or lines sent 1 or 3 instead of a notch's 100 pixels, so a notch zoomed a hundredth as far whatever the zoom speed. Pages and lines are turned into pixels now. The labels in the workspace settings that wrap to two lines no longer run over the next row.
- **A second view in its own window (#706):** A new button in the camera bar (picture-in-picture icon, after the flat-view switch) opens a popup that shows the same design through a camera of its own, so it can be watched from two sides at once. The popup draws the editor's own scene with a renderer of its own, so it holds no copy of the design and every change shows there on the next frame; editing stays in the main window, and the handles and guides are not drawn there. In the popup the mouse, the view cube and the view keys move its camera only: 1-6 (with Shift also framing the selection), F and Shift+F (framing what is selected in the editor), Home, O, + and -, the arrow keys to turn and Ctrl or Cmd with the arrows to slide. The design's four saved views work there too: 7, 8, 9 and 0 bring them back in the popup, and Shift with one of them saves the popup's view in the same slot, so a view saved in either window is there for both; a short message in the popup says the view was saved or the key is empty (`saveViewInSlot` and `showViewInSlot` in `cameraViews.ts`, which the 3D view uses too). Its own camera bar has Home, Center view on selection, zoom in and out, the flat/perspective switch and hide plate, which hides the plate in that window only; it folds away like the main one, follows the toolbar size, and takes two columns only in a low window. The view cube follows the View cube position setting at any width. The popup loads `/view.html` (in `public/`, so it is in the offline copy too), so its address bar names it instead of about:blank, and its title is "Layerling view". Its size and place are kept in this browser (`layerling.secondView.window`); the first time it opens at 960 × 720. It closes with the editor, and a second click on the button closes it. The button is not offered on phones and tablets, a tablet with a mouse or trackpad included: a coarse main pointer, a browser that says it is mobile (`navigator.userAgentData.mobile`) or names Android, iPhone, iPad or iPod, or an iPad that reports itself as a Mac all hide it; a laptop with a touch screen and a Chromebook keep it. The guide (chapter View and workplane, all guide languages) describes it, and the button's texts are in every language. Along the way the camera helpers moved out of `WorkplaneViewport.tsx` into `components/workplane/cameraRig.ts`, the frame and hole preview into `renderFrame.ts` (its buffers now kept per renderer), and the view cube into `ViewCube.tsx`, which both windows use. Unit tests for the window's place and size, where it is offered, the arrow-key steps, the cube's wiring and the saved views both windows share (`sharedSavedViews.test.ts`); tried in Chrome and Edge.

## 1.100.0

- **The shape library shows each shape in the colour it will be added in (#717, #483):** The pictures were grey, so the list said nothing about the colour that landed on the workplane. Every entry now wears the colour a new shape of it starts with - the catalogue's own, or the one saved for its kind under Shape defaults - so a red box, an orange cylinder and a blue sphere are recognisable before the click. The grey render keeps its shading: a colour layer, masked by the picture's outline, multiplies over it, in the list, in the icon-only size and in the light, dark and graphite themes. `shapeAssetColor` in `shapeCatalog.ts` is the one place that decides the colour, shared with `makeShapeFromAsset`; unit tests in `shapeCatalog.test.ts`. The setting **Shapes in colour** (`lib/shapeColors.ts`, `workspace.shapeColors`) switches the colour off again for anyone who prefers the grey pictures; on is the start, and the browser remembers the choice (a boot script sets `data-shape-colors` before the first paint). Unit tests in `shapeColors.test.ts`. Contributed by @crazykay; asked for by @bdeboer58 on GitHub.
- **Boolean Intersection and Show common part grey out on the dark grounds (#718):** With fewer than two parts selected the two buttons were disabled but looked live in the dark and graphite themes; both icons carry `toolbar-vector-art-icon` now, which dims them like the rest of the ribbon (`ToolbarIntersectPreviewIcon` wraps the lucide icon). Contributed by @gogades.
- **A click on the view cube turns the view instead of jumping (#715):** A face, edge or corner used to cut the camera there in one frame, which read as a blink and made it hard to follow where the view had gone. The camera now turns around the orbit pivot in about a third of a second: the distance stays, the azimuth takes the shorter way round, an ease-out cubic settles into the clicked view, and the cube itself follows frame by frame. A second click starts from wherever the first has got to; any camera input - a drag on the canvas or the cube, a wheel, a touch - takes over and ends the turn, and a system set to reduce motion still gets the jump (`prefersReducedMotion`). The AI bridge's `layerlingCaptureView` still sets the view before it captures, unchanged. `lib/viewCubeTransition.ts`, unit tests in `viewCubeTransition.test.ts`. Contributed by @crazykay.
- **A text's height stays out of "Keep proportions" (#724):** With **Keep proportions** on in the properties, typing a text's Height scaled its width and depth by the same factor, so the letter size changed along with the thickness. The height of a text is only the thickness of its letters, so `keepsProportionsOnAxis` in `keepProportions.ts` now leaves it out; the width and the depth of a text still keep the proportions, and every other shape is unchanged.
- **STEP export takes groups apart (#709):** A group, say a block with a tapped hole, was "a mesh without a CAD source" to the STEP export and was left out, so only the loose screw came out. `exportShapesToStep` now builds a plain group as the exact bodies it holds (`buildScope`, `isOpenableGroup`, `restoreGroupedChildren`): the holes of the group cut only its own solids, as in the editor, so a screw standing in the tapped hole stays whole; groups inside groups open the same way, and holes around a group still reach its bodies. A group that is itself one CAD body, an intersection, a bundle or one with edge treatments goes the way it did. A round-trip test in `stepRoundTrip.e2e.ts` checks the volume of the block, the thread and the screw.
- **The what's-new help opens the Brazilian Portuguese heading (#712):** With chapter 1 translated, the what's-new help button still used the English anchor and opened the top of the page; `GUIDE_SECTIONS.whatsNew` has its `pt-br` id now, and a test in `guideAllLanguages.test.ts` checks every question mark against the pt-BR chapters translated so far, so the next ones are covered as they come. Contributed by @lmagalhaes.
- **layerling speaks Simplified and Traditional Chinese (#710):** The whole interface (`messages.zh-cn.ts`, `messages.zh-tw.ts`, 2,861 sentences each), the 17 chapters of the guide in `docs/guide/zh-cn/` and `zh-tw/` and a README of its own for each (`README.zh-cn.md`, `README.zh-tw.md`), chosen in the language menu; browsers that ask for zh-Hans, zh-Hant, zh-TW, zh-HK or zh-MO get the matching one. Marked not complete, like Hungarian and Spanish: sentences added later show in English until the Chinese ones follow. Made with a large AI model and proof-read in part by the contributor, a native speaker; more readers are welcome. Contributed by @crazykay.
- **Open sides for Gridfinity bins (#682):** Bins meant to stand side by side as one long tray had two walls and two stacking lips between each pair. A Gridfinity bin now has **Open at the back**, **Open at the front**, **Open on the left** and **Open on the right**, in any combination, all four for a tray. An open side has no wall and no stacking lip above the floor; the walls beside it and their lip run straight out to the bin's outline and end square, so two open bins stand 0.5 mm apart as any two bins do, and the floor runs flat to the edge and ends square there, even in a bin with finger scoops, whose other floor edges are rounded. The feet and the magnet holes stay as they are. A label shelf or finger scoop on an open side is not built and its switch goes, and so do that side's scoops against the dividers; the setting is kept for when the side is closed again. Shelves and scoops on the walls beside it, and dividers that meet it, run out to the edge, and a compartment offers no label shelf on an open side. A bin stacked on an open one is held by the lip on its closed sides only. A bin without the lip whose floor is raised to the top is a solid block and has no sides to open. An open bin stays an exact body for the edge tool and STEP. Also through the MCP action `layerling_create_shape` with `gridfinityOpenBack`, `gridfinityOpenFront`, `gridfinityOpenLeft` and `gridfinityOpenRight`. Asked for by @Joepic9 in #682. Contributed by @mlugo-apx.

## 1.99.0

- **Rounded box radii to 0.01 mm (#705):** `normalizeCornerFillet` and `normalizeTopBottomFillet` rounded to a tenth of a millimetre, so a corner fillet of 0.25 mm came back as 0.3 mm. They round to a hundredth now, and the fields (the shape's properties and the defaults in the workspace settings) step by 0.05. The CAD solid reads the same functions, so it follows. Unit test in `roundedBoxGeometry.test.ts` (0.25 stays 0.25, 7.336 becomes 7.34). Reported by @Algotz on GitHub.
- **SVG saved as UTF-16 imports (#317):** CorelDRAW and some Windows tools save an SVG as UTF-16 with a byte order mark. layerling read every SVG as UTF-8, so such a file came out with null characters between the letters and failed with "SVG is not valid XML". `decodeSvgBytes` in `svgImport.ts` now reads a file that starts with the UTF-16 mark in either byte order as UTF-16, and everything else as UTF-8 as before.
- **Cmd and Option in the shortcut list on Apple keyboards (#704):** The list of keyboard shortcuts shows Cmd and Option instead of Ctrl and Alt on a Mac, an iPhone or an iPad (a browser that says Macintosh, iPhone or iPad), and the footer "On a Mac, use Cmd wherever Ctrl is listed" (`shortcuts.footer`) is gone from every catalogue, as nothing needs it any more. Contributed by @gogades.
- **Simple mode for children and workshops (#695):** A switch in the settings (`workspace.simpleMode`, `lib/simpleMode.ts`) cuts layerling down: the ribbon keeps copy, paste, duplicate, delete, undo and redo, group and ungroup, mirror, chamfer, fillet, hollow, align and export, the shape library fifteen simple entries (`SIMPLE_MODE_TOOLS`, `SIMPLE_MODE_SHAPES`), the buttons grow to the large toolbar size, and the start page shows the designs and nothing else - no news, welcome text, update notice, install hint or footer, and no question-mark help links, so nothing leads out of layerling. Hidden too: the command search, the help group, visibility, notes, import, My shapes and the sweep. Switching it on is a tap, switching it off a press held for two seconds. `?mode=simple` and `?mode=full` in a link set it on the device that opens it (the boot script stores the choice and drops the parameter); `LAYERLING_SIMPLE_MODE=true` starts everyone who has not chosen in it and `always` fixes it, through `/api/server-defaults`. The settings panel of the selected body is not cut down yet. Unit tests in `simpleMode.test.ts` and `serverDefaults.test.ts`. Asked for by @cyryllo on GitHub, with the list of what the children use.
- **Close a gap in a sketch by dragging (#698):** Erase one side of a hexagon or a circle and two open ends are left; dragging one onto the other only moved it, the two stayed separate points on top of each other and the outline never counted as closed. A dragged open end now snaps to the other open ends of the sketch, and dropped on one it becomes one point with it (`joinOpenEnds` in `sketchPointJoin.ts`, called by `moveSketchPoint`; `openEndPoints` feeds the snap in `SketchWorkspace.tsx`). The curve handle of the dragged end goes across to the joined point when the two sides meet end to start; two curved sides that would both start (or both end) there are left as they are, as are a locked outline, the two ends of one single side, and a point with more than one side. Unit tests in `sketchPointJoin.test.ts`; not tried by hand in a browser. The guide (chapter Sketches, German and English) and the welcome text name it. Asked by @lukyanenkomax on GitHub.
- **Hinge opening angle (#699):** A new slider, **Opening angle**, folds the hinge's second leaf up about the middle of the pin, from 0 degrees (lying flat as it prints) to 180 (closed), so a hinge can be set as it stands in a design. The new `hingeAngle` turns the second part in the display mesh (`hingeTriangles`) and its exact boxes (`hingeLocalParts`, placed by `hingeWorldParts`, which also follows a mirrored hinge), so the edge tool and the STEP export see the folded hinge; the MCP shape settings and the project file take it too. The guide (German and English) and the MCP skill name it.
- **Imported parts thinner than 1 mm keep their size (#700):** The STL and OBJ import rounded every extent up to 1 mm in the base size it stores, while the points kept their real size, so a part 0.76 mm high showed "1.00" and every height typed in came out 0.76 times too small (5 mm became 3.81). `importedShapeFromTriangleSoup` and `importedShapeFromObj` now report the real extent (`reportedMeshExtent`; a sheet with no thickness still reports 1), and `canonicalizeShape` repairs saved designs and saved shapes that carry the old 1 (`repairClampedMeshBase` in `importedMeshBase.ts`): the base is set to the real extent and the shape's own size is scaled with it, so the part keeps its size in the world. Unit tests in `thinMeshSize.test.ts`. Reported by @Moosli-moo on GitHub.
- **layerling speaks Spanish (#697):** The whole interface (`messages.es.ts`, 2,858 sentences) and the 17 chapters of the guide (`docs/guide/es/`) in Spanish as written in Spain, informal ("tú"), registered as "Español" in `languages.ts` with the quotation marks and key labels of a Spanish keyboard; any Spanish browser (es-ES, es-MX, es-419) gets it. Not marked complete, like Hungarian: sentences added later show in English until the Spanish ones follow. Three tests that used Spanish as the example of an unsupported language use Swedish now. Typecheck, the unit tests and the guide build checked here; no native speaker has read it yet. Contributed by @litio2001.
- **Gears up to 500 teeth (#679):** `MAX_GEAR_TEETH` went from 150 to 500, so spur gears, ring gears and racks take up to 500 teeth, for example a module 0.5 wheel of 250 mm. The exact CAD solids of a 500-tooth spur gear and ring gear are checked in `cadGearBody.e2e.ts` and `cadGearRingRack.e2e.ts`. The guide names the new range.
- **Name tags and icon tags on a side face (#696):** Built on a standing or sloped face, or changed there, a tag stacked its layers up the world instead of out of the face: the plate came out in front, the rim and the letters shifted upward behind it. `textLayerShapes` and `layeredIconShape` now stack the layers along the tag's own up, from the middle of its underside, and `textLayersOf` and `iconLayersOf` read a stack back in that order, with its place on the bottom layer's underside; a tag lying flat is built exactly as before. Unit tests in `nameTagOnSideFace.test.ts` (a tag carried onto a face facing +x is built again layer for layer, each on the one below along +x) and `iconTag.test.ts` (a turned stack read back in order, its place on the face); the icon tag's layers built on a side face by the CAD worker not tried. Reported by @cyryllo on GitHub.

## 1.98.0

- **The guide says where the notes switch is and how a note folds away (#693):** The notes section named "Show notes and points" but not where it is, and said a click on a note opens it for editing. It now says the switch is in Visibility options, the arrow beside the eye icon, and that a click on the pin folds the card away to its number and opens it again, while a click into the text edits it (chapter Measuring and notes, all languages). Asked by @gquiring on GitHub.
- **SVG with only the footprint (#692):** Export → SVG wrote the silhouette from above, everything seen from the top, so a part wider above its base gave a bigger outline than the one standing on the plate. The SVG export now has a choice under View: Silhouette from above (default, as before) or Footprint on the workplane, which cuts the bodies 0.01 mm above their lowest point with the section view's slicer and writes those outlines as lines in their colours. `layerling_export_mesh` takes `format: "svg"` with `view: "silhouette"` or `"footprint"`.
- **Closed in one click when hollowing (#658):** A body hollowed with no side open keeps its cavity sealed inside, but the panel only said so in the small line under the six side buttons, and Top had to be switched off first. Below the side buttons there is now Closed (cavity inside): it is pressed while no side is open, a click switches every side off, and switching a side on lets it go (`ShellPanel.tsx`, `shell.side.closed` in all languages). The guide (chapter Edges and hollowing, all languages) names it. Unit test in `shellClosedOption.test.ts` (pressed with no side open, let go with Top open); not tried in a browser. Asked by @tpkeller on GitHub.
- **Thin floors under a hole stay (#691):** A hole stuck out 0.05 mm at its top and bottom, so a hole that ends flush with a face leaves no skin. That also took the same 0.05 mm off any floor or roof the hole did not reach: a hole box raised 0.01 to 0.05 mm above the bottom of a box took the whole box away ("hole consumed solid"), and one raised 0.06 mm left 0.01 mm. `CUTTER_PADDING` is now 0.001 mm, as the sides already were since #254, so a floor loses a thousandth at most; `CUTTER_RESIDUAL_INSET` keeps its 0.02 mm. Kernel test in `thinSkinUnderHole.e2e.ts` (floors of 0.01, 0.05, 0.06 and 1 mm, a hole from the bottom still takes the box away, a hole flush with the top leaves no skin). Reported by @KimA998 on GitHub.
- **Through the history with the mouse wheel (#688):** In the history view the mouse wheel over the slider row goes one state per notch, up to an earlier one; a touchpad's small deltas add up to a step every 50 px. Page Up and Page Down jump ten states, Home goes to the oldest state and End to the one the design is at (`lib/historyStepping.ts`, `historyStepping.test.ts`, wired in `HistoryViewOverlay.tsx`; the wheel listener is not passive, so the page does not scroll). The shortcuts list (new rows and a "Mouse wheel" key, all languages) and the guide (chapter Select and arrange, all languages) say so. Tried in the dev server on 15 steps: the wheel went 15, 14, 13 and back to 14, Page Up to 4, Home to 0, Page Down to 10, End to 15. Asked by @ucito on GitHub.
- **A key ring hole anywhere on the tag (#598):** The name tag and the icon tag could only put the key ring hole on the left, the right or the top, and on a picture like a dog's head none of the three looked right. Beside them there is now Free: it takes the hole off the tag and puts a key ring tab of its own beside it, an ordinary group of a capsule and the hole in the bottom layer's colour and height, with the measures of the side tab (the hole and a 1.75 mm wall either side, a round end round the hole), moved and turned with its handles like any shape. Fix to the tag in the tab's panel then makes it part of the nearest name tag or icon tag that is neither locked nor hidden, just where it lies: the tag is built again with the hole there, the ear (the disc round the hole and the strip to the tab's straight end) added to the bottom layer and the hole cut through every layer, so the plate no longer fills the hole when the tab lies over it, and nothing is grouped. The hole is kept to the corner of the letters' or the icon's box it is nearest to, so it stays there when the words, the letter size or the icon's width change; the box grows by what the ear reaches beyond it. Free on such a tag brings the tab out again where it was. Saved as `free` (the corner, the distance from it, the ear's angle and length) beside the key ring's side and size; a file with a bad one is refused. Unit tests for the tab's measures, reading its place back at several turns, the way into the tag's frame and back on a turned tag, the corner it keeps to and how far the box grows; tried in the editor (headless Chromium) on a name tag and an icon tag. Asked on GitHub by @KBFlyer.
- **Deleted designs stay deleted (#656):** A backup restored after 1.94.0 lost the list gives every design a new id, and the old copies stayed in IndexedDB without a card. The rescue of 1.95.0 looked for such designs at every start and skipped one only while a design of the same name and creation time was listed, so deleting the restored design brought the old copy back at the next start, without picture or folder; with several restores, the next one each time. The rescue now runs once per browser (`layerling.lostDesignsRecovered` in localStorage), and deleting a design also deletes its hidden copies (same name and creation time, no card; `hiddenCopiesOf` in `designListStorage.ts`). Unit test in `designListStorage.test.ts`; not tried in a browser with such copies. Reported by @kwjaarsveld-star on GitHub.
- **The browser may translate layerling again (#681):** Since 1.82.0 the whole page said it was not to be translated, so somebody who read layerling in Spanish through Chrome's translator lost that. The cause of the old trouble was the page language: the page said "en" until the chosen language was in, so a German browser translated a German page and made "Hell" into "Hölle". Now a script in the head (`LANGUAGE_BOOT_SCRIPT` in `i18n.ts`) sets `<html lang>` before anything is drawn, from the stored choice or the browser's languages as `detectLanguage` picks it, and `translate="no"` and the `notranslate` hint are gone from the page. A language layerling does not speak stays "en", and the translator may turn it into that language. The names people give stay as they are (`translate="no"` on the design and folder names of the start page and on the names in My Shapes). Unit test runs the head script for stored choices, browser languages and a language we do not have (`noBrowserTranslation.test.ts`); the page language checked in the dev server, the translator itself not tried. Asked by @Salleri on GitHub.
- **A renamed design keeps its new name in a backup (#689):** Renaming a design on the start page changed its card only; the saved package kept the old name until the design was changed again. A backup put that package into the ZIP as it was, under the new file name, so restoring it brought the old name back, and the check for designs that are already there compared the wrong name. The package now gets the card's name before it is handed on (`withLylProjectName` in `lylProject.ts`, used by `projectPackageBytes`), for a backup and for moving a design to the server folder; nothing else in the package changes. Unit test in `lylProjectName.test.ts`. Reported by @Woxon1 on GitHub.
- **A minus key for negative values on a phone or tablet (#686):** The number fields asked for the decimal pad (`inputMode="decimal"`), which on an iPhone or iPad has no minus key, so a position left of the middle, a negative angle or a move back could not be typed there. The fields that take negative values now ask for the keyboard with signs (`inputMode="text"`): position and pivot, every property field whose range goes below zero such as the rotations, the move distance typed beside a shape, the angle of a typed sketch line, and the position and rotation of a sketch's reference image. Fields that only take positive sizes keep the decimal pad. Checked by type check; not tried on a real iPhone or iPad. Reported by @Joepic9 on GitHub.
- **Gears up to 150 teeth (#679):** A gear, ring gear or rack stopped at 64 teeth, too few for a large wheel of a small module such as 0.5. `MAX_GEAR_TEETH` is 150 now, and the slider, the shape defaults in the settings, the check when a file is opened and the AI bridge's description follow it; the guide names the range (en/de/hu). Unit tests for round teeth up to 150 and the clamp; the exact bodies of an involute spur gear and a ring gear of 150 teeth at module 0.5 match their meshes in the CAD kernel (e2e). Asked by @IacobiniMarioEduard on GitHub.
- **A fillet with a radius that changes along the edge (#687):** The fillet had one radius for its whole edge, so a corner where a straight wall meets a slanted one could not be rounded small at one end and wide at the other. The fillet window now has Variable radius: it shows Radius at the start and Radius at the end, and the kernel's `filletVariable` rounds the one picked edge from the one to the other, evenly along it. Which end is the start follows from the edge's own direction; Swap ends turns the two values round. It takes one edge at a time; with more picked the window says so (`VARIABLE_FILLET_ONE_EDGE_MESSAGE`). The treatment is saved with `endAmount` beside `amount`, Edit brings both back, the list shows "2–1 mm", and the AI bridge's `layerling_apply_edge_treatment` takes `endAmount` for one edge id (all three SKILL.md). Guide (en/de/hu), start page (en/de), texts in nine languages. Kernel test on an 80° prism corner (removes more than the even 1 mm and less than the even 8 mm fillet; swapping the values swaps the ends) and a unit test for the message; tried in the editor on a leaning box through the AI bridge and through the window (variable on, Swap ends, Apply, the list entry). Asked by @NorthHighlander101 on GitHub.

## 1.97.0

- **Trackpad pinch zooms in full steps in Chrome:** Chrome (on a Mac and on Linux) sends a pinch as Ctrl wheel steps far smaller than a notch. OrbitControls counts those ten times over, but the notch count of #331 replaces the step it reads, so a pinch zoomed a tenth as far. `wheelZoomDelta` now multiplies a small Ctrl step by `PINCH_WHEEL_FACTOR` itself; a mouse notch with Ctrl held stays as it is, and Safari's pinch (`pinchWheelDelta`) is made that much smaller, so it zooms as before. Unit tests in `wheelZoom.test.ts`; not tried with a real trackpad. Reported by mail.
- **The properties panel follows the toolbar size:** Small, Medium and Large in the settings now scale the panel on the right as well (`zoom: var(--toolbar-scale)` on `.shape-inspector`), as they already did the ribbon, the shape library and the camera controls. Measured in the dev server: 240, 320 and 400 px wide, docked to the full height, and moved by its header it follows the pointer one to one. The guide (chapter View, all guide languages) says so. Reported by mail.
- **Take a sketch apart by its outlines (#669):** Separate Parts on an extruded sketch body now splits it into one exact body per outer outline, overlapping outlines included, each with a sketch of its own (`lib/sketchOutlineParts.ts`, `sketchOutlineParts.test.ts`); a status message says how many outlines came apart. The MCP action `separate_parts` does the same, and its description in the MCP tools and the three SKILL.md files, the guide's MCP table and the sketch chapter (all languages) say so. In French the button is now "Séparer les pièces".
- **French wording fixes (#675):** Y was labelled "Oui", the rotation axes read "À propos de X", the Position section "Poste", Taper "Cône" and Twist & lean "Tordez et penchez-vous". Only `messages.fr.ts` changed.
- **Esc puts the tool down in a sketch (#664):** As in the 3D editor, Esc first ends the drawing tool in hand and switches back to Select; only with Select in hand does it clear the selection (`lib/sketchEscape.ts`, `sketchEscape.test.ts`). The shortcuts list (all languages) and the guide's sketch chapter say so.
- **Big parts follow the mouse (#673):** A body was always dragged on the workplane itself, so a press high up on a tall or tilted body met the plate far behind it. In a design bigger than the plate that point lay past the drag limit (one plate width beyond the edge, #80), and the body stopped on one side or did not move at all; the arrow keys still worked. Now the drag runs on a plane lifted to the point where the pointer took hold (`dragWorkplaneAtGrab` in `WorkplaneViewport.tsx`), so that point stays under the pointer, and the limit reaches one plate width past everything in the design, not only past the plate (`lib/dragReach.ts`, `dragReach.test.ts`). Snapping and its guide lines work as before, the guides stay on the workplane. Tried in the dev server with the reporter's tube and a 50° tilted box seen from the front: before, the box did not move either way; now it follows the pointer both ways. Reported by @Geronimo700 on GitHub.
- **layerling speaks Brazilian Portuguese (#672):** The editor's texts in Brazilian Portuguese (`messages.pt-br.ts`), chosen in the language menu; whatever is not translated yet falls back to English, and the guide stays English for now. Contributed by @lmagalhaes.
- **The key ring tab can be moved along its side (#671):** The tab of a name tag's or an icon tag's key ring hole always stood in the middle of its side, so on a name like "Jess" it met the gap beside the J's stem and left a notch between tab and letter. A new slider **Position along side** under the hole moves it up or down (along the top: left or right), within the side, so it can sit where the letter reaches out; 0 is the middle, and changing the side takes it back there. The band the tab's flat end is fitted to moves with it, so the hole's edge stays 2 mm beyond the plate. Stored as `along` in the text's and the sketch's key ring, read back by the card, kept in .lyl files; the MCP actions `layerling_layer_text` and `layerling_layer_icon` take `keyring.along`. The guide (chapter Text, all languages) and the MCP skill say so. Tried in unit tests (`keyringAlong.test.ts`), not yet seen on screen. Asked by @madgolfermatt-sys on GitHub.
- **Four saved views per design (#657):** Shift+7, Shift+8, Shift+9 or Shift+0 saves the view exactly as it is (angle, pan, zoom, and flat or perspective) under that key, and 7, 8, 9 or 0 alone brings it back. A message confirms the save, and a key with nothing saved under it says how to fill it. The key's position counts too, so it works where Shift turns the digit into another sign (and on AZERTY, where the digit itself needs Shift); the number pad works as well. The views belong to the design: they are kept in this browser beside the view the design was left in (#220) and travel in a `.lyl` file as `editor.savedViews`, which older readers pass over. They are not part of the undo history. The shortcuts list and the guide (all guide languages) name the keys. Unit tests for the keys, the four slots and the `.lyl` round trip; not yet tried on screen. Along the way the camera-view code moved out of `WorkplaneViewport.tsx` into `components/workplane/cameraViews.ts`, and the status line's timers out of `LayerlingEditor.tsx` into `components/workplane/useEditorNotice.ts`. Also through the MCP action `layerling_saved_view` (`save`/`show` with the key, or neither to read which keys hold a view). The Gridfinity divider tests got 30 s, since one ran over the 5 s limit on the CI runner. Contributed by @gogades.

## 1.96.0

- **Metric threads up to M24 (#659):** The size list of the thread stopped at M12, while the inch sizes go up to 1 inch. It now goes on with M14, M16, M18, M20, M22 and M24, each with its standard coarse pitch (2, 2, 2.5, 2.5, 2.5 and 3 mm), the socket head of ISO 4762 and the nut of ISO 4032, so they fit bought screws and nuts; the countersunk head is twice the diameter, as for the smaller sizes. The MCP server and the guide (every language) name them too. A free diameter takes the pitch of the nearest size in the list, so one of 21 mm now gets the 2.5 mm of M20 and M22 instead of the 2.54 mm of 3/4". Asked for by @i8ahorse in #659.
- **"Set down on a face" no longer puts the body back on a tablet (#619):** After a long press, choosing "Set down on a face" and tapping a face showed "1 part(s) set down on the face", but the body stayed where it was, and only Undo showed it on the face. The long press had also begun moving the body under the finger, and on the iPad the finger's end did not reach the 3D view, so that move stayed open; the tap that set the body down ended it, and it wrote the body's old place over the new one as a step of its own. The long press now drops the move it began when the menu opens, and the end of a move made by another finger no longer counts (both in WorkplaneViewport.tsx). Tried in a headless Edge with touch events and the finger's end held back as on the iPad: before, the body stayed; now it lands on the face. Reported with a screen recording by @grandmaofdragonz on GitHub.
- **A copy of an imported part set to hole takes the whole part away (#607):** Every hole was made a thousandth of a millimetre wider on each side, so that one touching a wall along a line does not cut a slab out of it (#254). It did that by scaling the hole round its middle, which widens a box or a cylinder all round, but on a long imported SVG it pushed both walls of a letter far from the middle the same way: one wall of the copy missed the original by that thousandth, and a thin strip of wall stayed standing after grouping, even with the hole made taller. Imported meshes, sketch bodies, texts and groups used as holes now keep their width and depth exactly and are padded only at the top and bottom; simple shapes keep the thousandth. Tried with the reported SVG: the part and its copy cancel out, the same size or taller. Reported by @stainlessdmc12 in #607.
- **A sketch can become another kind of body, an imported SVG a revolve (#655):** The kind of body - extruded, revolved or swept - was chosen when a sketch was started and could not change, so an SVG, which comes in extruded, could only become a round body by copying its outline into a new revolve sketch. The badge at the top of the sketch is now a choice of the three, also while the sketch of an existing body is edited. Switched to another kind, finishing builds a new body of that kind from the same outline in the old one's place, with its id, name and colour, standing where it stood with its foot at the same height (`sketchBodyAsOperation` in editorSketchCad.ts); switched back, the body is edited as before. A revolve is drawn on the plate from the front, and an outline reaching across the axis - an SVG is centred on itself - moves left until its rightmost point lies on the axis (`sketchProfileLeftOfAxis`, undone with Ctrl+Z), so the half cross-section of an SVG turns about its straight side into an exact body. Also through the MCP action `layerling_update_object` with `sketchOperation`. The sketch chapter of the guide says so in every language. Tried in a headless Edge through the badge and through the MCP bridge. Asked for by @Megamudpie on GitHub.
- **Hollowing closed all round says where the cavity went (#658):** In the Hollow panel Top is switched on when it opens, so a click on it to get an open top switches it off, and the body is hollowed with no side open. From outside it looked as if nothing had happened. The message after hollowing now says, when no side is open, that the cavity is inside and shows only in the section view. Reported by @tpkeller on GitHub.
- **Holes can be shown more clearly (#662):** A hole is drawn in a pale grey at about half strength, and among see-through bodies, which are pale too, an unselected hole was hard to find. **Show holes more clearly** under Settings, Appearance draws holes in a darker grey (`#5b6670`), less see-through (72 %, 60 % for an imported mesh) and with a dark line round them, also in fast mode, since it costs no more than the hole itself. The setting is handed to the shape builder like the edge lines and is part of the material signature, so holes already on screen change at once. Off by default; the guide's view chapter says so in every language. Asked for by @grmonsey on GitHub.

## 1.95.0

- **A backup brings the card pictures back with the designs:** "Back up all" put only the .lyl files into the ZIP, so after a restore every card showed "No preview yet" until its design was opened, in the browser as in Docker. Each card picture now lies next to its design in the ZIP, under the same name as a `.png`, and a restore puts it back on the card at once (through the same path a duplicate takes, so on the server it is saved there again). A backup made before has no pictures; such a design still gets one when it is opened. Reported by a user by mail.
- **Sharper text on a Mac:** The interface sets `-webkit-font-smoothing: antialiased` (and `-moz-osx-font-smoothing: grayscale`), so the heavy weights of Avenir Next no longer look thick and soft on Retina screens. Not seen on a Mac here. Reported by a user by mail.
- **The list of designs is no longer lost when the browser's storage is full, and lost designs come back (#656):** The start page keeps its list of designs in localStorage, which holds only a few megabytes, and on layerling.com each card picture was written into that list. With many designs the list stopped fitting, and the way round a full storage took the list out before writing it again: when the second write failed too, the list was gone. The start page showed the folders, all empty, and opening the designs again to get pictures filled the storage once more. The card pictures now live in a database of their own, so the list stays small, and a list that does not fit leaves the one before it in place with a note. The shapes of the designs were never touched: they lie in IndexedDB, and the start page now brings back every design whose shapes are there but whose card was lost, with its name, at the top level; a design that a backup already brought back, or that is there twice, comes back once, the newest.
- **Sketch outlines can be locked, and the frame can keep its proportions (#653):** In the sketch only the template image could be locked, and a selection kept its proportions only while Shift was held on a corner. The bar of a selection now has **Lock** (also the key L, which still locks the template image when that is selected): the lock sits on the outline's points, and the editor refuses any change that would move a locked point, change its handles or kind, or remove, cut up or change a side between two locked points - so every tool, the arrow keys and Delete respect it without a rule of their own, and a drag shows nothing moving. A locked outline draws paler and its frame has no handles to stretch or turn it; lines may still start at its corners. **Keep proportions** beside it makes the corners and the sides stretch both ways alike and a typed width or height change the other one too; the switch is remembered in the browser. The logic is in `sketchLock.ts` and `sketchSelectionResize.ts` with unit tests; `locked` on a sketch point is checked when a design is loaded; the sketch chapter of the guide (EN/DE) and the shortcut list say so. Asked for by @structurednewstore-rgb on GitHub.
- **Compartments for Gridfinity bins (#554):** Dividing a bin meant grouping thin boxes into it and keeping them under the stacking lip by hand. A bin of 2 height units or more now has **Compartments** below **Properties**: **Columns** and **Rows**, up to 4 per grid unit each way and 40 in all, with dividers 1.2 mm thick (0.8 to 3) that stop where the label shelves do, clear of the feet of a bin stacked on the lip. They start evenly spaced and move one by one, every compartment at least 6 mm wide. Corners and floor edges are rounded by 2.8 mm, and finger scoops repeat in every compartment, made smaller in a narrow compartment, and left out where even 3.5 mm does not fit. From 3 height units each compartment can have a label shelf of its own on any side, 10 to 100 % of that side's length, at either end or in the middle, never closing it off; in a divided bin these take the place of the shelves along the walls, which come back with one compartment. It stays an exact body for the edge tool and STEP. Also through the MCP action `layerling_create_shape` with `gridfinityCompartmentsX`/`Z`, `gridfinityDividerThickness`, `gridfinityDividerPositionsX`/`Z` and `gridfinityCompartmentLabels`. Asked for by @DannyDesignStudio. Contributed by @mlugo-apx.
- **Ctrl or Cmd with the left button moves the view again (#654):** The guide promised that holding Ctrl and dragging with the left button moves the view, but it turned it: layerling asked OrbitControls for a pan on that button, and OrbitControls swaps pan and rotate by itself while Ctrl, Cmd or Shift is held. On a MacBook without a middle button there was no way left to move the view. The left button now asks for rotate with Ctrl or Cmd, which the swap turns into a pan, in the editor and in the Simplify comparison alike (`leftButtonCameraAction` in cameraMouseButtons.ts, with a unit test that also pins the swap in three.js). The mouse table of the guide names Cmd in every language, and in English and German says how it goes on a MacBook trackpad. Tried in a headless Edge with Ctrl and with Cmd. Asked on GitHub by @swingstate.
- **A fillet no longer leaves a body with gaps in its mesh (#651):** A third 1 mm fillet round a pocket in a block gave a valid exact body, but its mesh had 30 open edges at one corner: the two faces along one fillet edge were each meshed with points of their own. The body showed hollow in the section view, and the next cut (a Round Roof set as a hole) or Split stopped at "Not manifold". After an edge treatment the mesh is now checked, and one with gaps is made again from the body's faces sewn together anew at 0.000001 mm, which gives the same body, the same volume and a closed mesh; a body whose mesh is closed is left as it was. The check and the sewing are in `cadMeshAccuracy.ts` (`cadMeshHasOpenEdges`, `closedMeshBody`), with a kernel test on the body from the report (`cadMeshClosedAfterFillet.e2e.ts`). A design that already holds such a body keeps its mesh until that fillet is undone and made again. Reported by @danyvanimpe-lab on GitHub.

## 1.94.0

- **A Bezier point placed by a click has handles that can be seen and taken (#636):** A click with the Bezier tool, without dragging, gave the point handles of no length: both lay on the point itself, hidden under its circle, so the point looked as if it had none, and nothing could be pulled out of it when the curve was edited. Such a point now gets handles along the side that leads to it, a third of its length each way, so the side is visibly bent and the handles are there at once. The first point of a chain has no side yet and stays without; its outgoing handle comes when the next point is placed. In sketches that already hold handles of no length, a selected point or side draws them a third of the way to the neighbour, where they can be grabbed; the curve stays as it is until one is dragged. Tried in the unit tests and the whole suite (2634), not seen on screen. Reported by @raradek on GitHub.

- **The Bezier tool starts and ends on an existing point with handles (#635):** With the Bezier tool a press on a point that was already there only connected it: no handles could be pulled out of it, neither when a curve started there nor when it ended there. And a side bends only when both of its ends have the handle that faces it, so a curve that started on a point without handles stayed straight, and the handle of the next point was drawn but not applied. A press on an existing point now starts the drag as on the empty plane: it gets the mirrored handles, and a plain click gives the points of the new side the handle they lack, a third of the way along the side, so the side is a curve in both cases. Handles a point has already stay. The point the chain is on is left as it was, so a double click on it still ends the chain (#520). The logic is in `sketchBezierHandles.ts` with its unit tests; tried with the whole suite, but not seen on screen (the video of the report could not be played here). Reported by @raradek on GitHub.

- **Set a body down on a face without a keyboard (#619):** The key C picks the selection up so that it follows the pointer and lies down on the face under it, but there was no way to start that on an iPad. The menu of a long press on a body now has **Set down on a face**, and the command search finds it too ("cruise", "set down"); it does what C does, and a tap sets the body down on the face tapped, even a sloped one. The tablet chapter of the guide (EN/DE) and the chapter on selecting and arranging say so. Asked for by @grandmaofdragonz on GitHub.
- **The zoom buttons and keys no longer follow the zoom speed:** The zoom speed in the settings set the wheel and the trackpad, but also the step of the zoom buttons in the toolbar and of the keys + and −, up to 10. A trackpad wants the slider high, around 10 on a MacBook, and there one press jumped from too far to too close in three steps; with the slider at its slowest the keys were right but the trackpad crawled. The buttons and keys now always step as they did at the default position of the slider (5), in the 3D editor and in the sketch, so nothing changes for anyone who never moved it. The toolbar buttons now take the same step as the keys (they stepped ×0.7/×1.35 in the 3D view and ×0.8/×1.25 in the sketch, the keys ×0.72/×1.28); the slider sets the wheel, the trackpad and the pinch only. Guide (EN/DE) updated. Not tried on screen. Contributed by @gogades.
- **A design with an emptied object name opens again:** Clearing an object's name in the object list or the properties shows its default name, and the design saves it as an empty name - but loading refused that name, so the design, even the copy kept in the browser, opened empty. Loading now takes an empty name, and designs that were saved that way open again with all their objects.
- **The AI bridge refuses what would break a design:** `layerling_create_shape` and `layerling_update_object` refused nothing below a size of 0, and a design holding a body of width 0 or a negative height could not be opened again; they now answer with an error, and the tool list says sizes must be above 0. An empty colour is refused too. `layerling_delete_objects`, `layerling_ungroup_objects` and `layerling_apply_edge_treatment` now leave locked objects alone, as the editor does.
- **Placing on a large import no longer fails:** `layerling_place_on_face`, `layerling_perforate_face` and `layerling_push_face` stopped with a "Maximum call stack size exceeded" error when the target was a large imported mesh (from some tens of thousands of triangles, depending on the browser); they now work on meshes of any size.
- **The circle of a pattern follows the pivot (#638):** The centre of a circle pattern was taken from the pivot once, when the pattern tool opened. A pivot dragged to a new place afterwards left the centre, and the preview of the copies, where they were. The centre now follows the pivot as long as the pattern is open; a centre typed in the panel stays until the pivot moves again. Not tried on screen. Reported by @raradek on GitHub.
- **The build volume warning can be clicked away, body by body (#640):** Parts parked beside the plate kept the warning on screen all the time, and it lay over windows such as the workspace settings. The message now has a small cross: it hides the warning for exactly the bodies it named, for this session. A body that fits again is forgotten, so moving it out later warns again, and any other body that sticks out brings the message back. The warning now sits below the windows. The logic is in `keepDismissedOverhangs` (printBed.ts) with unit tests; the guide chapter on printing (EN/DE) says so. Asked for by @robstern on GitHub.
- **Two gears can be fitted to the distance they stand at (#645):** With two gears selected, the properties panel gave the centre distance they mesh at and how far apart their centres are, so meshing them meant moving one. It now also names the module at which they would mesh where they stand - centre distance x 2 / (teeth + teeth), inside a ring gear / (ring teeth - pinion teeth) - and the line can be selected and copied. **Fit module of both** resizes both gears to that module in one undo step; each keeps its teeth, place, height and centre hole (a gear without a hole size of its own keeps the hole it has now; the hole only shrinks where the smaller gear has no room for it). It works when the two modules differ too. The logic is `fitModule` in `involuteGearPair` and `gearPairFitPatches` (gearGeometry.ts) with unit tests; the gear section of the guide (EN/DE) says so. Asked for by @Elkniwt on GitHub.
- **A copy pasted into another design keeps all its settings (#642):** Within one design the tab keeps a copy as it is, but another design reads it back from the browser's storage, and that reading took only a fixed list of fields. Everything else was lost on the way: see-through, rounded and chamfered edges, mirroring, a twisted or leaning extrusion, a text's stroke, spacing and letter edge, the QR code settings, the pivot, formulas and the exact CAD body. The copy's own fields now go on top of the defaults (`clipboardShape` in shapeCatalog.ts, with unit tests). Tried in the unit tests, not seen on screen. Reported by @Elkniwt on GitHub.
- **Deleting a crossing point in a sketch keeps the circle (#641):** A point between two sides was always bridged, but a point where two lines cross - a circle cut by a straight line, as when a rounded corner is built from a circle - has four sides, and deleting it took all four away, so the rest of the circle fell apart. The sides that run straight on through the point are now joined in pairs, arc with arc and line with line; a side that turns away goes as before. Two arcs of one circle are joined with handles for the whole arc, so the circle stays round instead of flattening, also when a point between just two arcs is deleted. Removing only a piece of arc was already possible with the eraser on that piece; the sketch chapter of the guide (EN/DE) now says both. The logic is in `sketchPointRemoval.ts` with unit tests; not seen on screen. Asked for by @structurednewstore-rgb on GitHub.
- **The preview pictures of the start page survive a Docker update, and come back by themselves:** The Docker image keeps the pictures in the container, so a rebuild or update left every card blank, and so did bringing designs back from a backup; a picture only came again once something in the design changed. `docker/compose.yml` now keeps them in a named volume, `layerling-thumbnails` (Docker fills it from the image, so it needs no chown), and the `docker run` commands in the READMEs and the guide (every language) carry the same `-v` option. And a design whose card has no picture now gets one as soon as it is opened, without any change. Tried on the development server: a deleted picture came back a few seconds after opening the design; the compose file itself was not tried with Docker here. Reported by a user by mail.
- **Three-point arc in the sketch (#643):** A new drawing tool, **Arc**, next to Line: click the two ends, then a third point the arc runs through, and a true circular arc is laid between them, with a live preview after the second click and the radius in the message. An end on an existing point joins it, so an arc can close an outline; [[Esc]] drops the points clicked so far; with construction lines on it is a construction arc. The arc is built from Bezier pieces of at most a quarter circle, within a few ten-thousandths of the radius, and is ordinary geometry afterwards (it is not a parametric arc: radius and centre cannot be typed in later). Logic in `sketchArc.ts` with unit tests, a new icon `sketch-tool-arc.png`, texts in all 14 languages, the command search finds it ("arc", "Bogen", "radius"), and the sketch chapter of the guide (EN/DE) describes it. Tried in the browser: three clicks gave the arc, a line closed it and it became an exact body. Asked for by @structurednewstore-rgb on GitHub.
- **A bundle with a hole cuts the parts it is grouped with, but not its own solids (#646):** A bundle of a solid and a hole, grouped with another part, opened into its parts for the cut, so its hole cut away the bundle's own solid as well as the other part. Now it works as in Tinkercad: the hole of a bundle cuts every part outside the bundle, the bundle's solids stay whole and are merged with the rest, and a hole outside the bundle still cuts them. A bundle in a bundle counts as part of it. A group that already holds such a bundle keeps its shape until it is opened and closed again. The cut is `manifoldBundleCutShape` in editorBooleans.ts, with a kernel test (`groupBundleHole.e2e.ts`) that checks the volumes; the chapter on solids and holes of the guide (EN/DE), the AI bridge's `layerling_bundle_objects` and the skill say so. Not tried on screen. Reported by @TinkerBarn on GitHub.
- **The section view and the split follow the workplane, and the cut can face the view (#644):** The section view cut only across the design's own X, Y and Z, and the split plane started level and could be tilted only with its two sliders. The section panel now has **Axes from: Design | Workplane | View**. On the workplane, X, Y and Z are the workplane's own, Z cuts parallel to it, the position counts from its origin, and the cut moves along when the workplane moves; on a workplane laid on a sloped face that cuts parallel to the face. View turns the plane to face the screen and takes away what lies in front, so the slider pushes the cut deeper along the line of sight to reach a buried part; it takes the view at the click, so it is clicked again after turning the view. The filled cut face, measuring on the cut, the cut as SVG and putting the workplane on the cut all work on the turned plane (the bodies are moved into the plane's frame and sliced there, `SectionFrame` in sectionView.ts). The split plane now starts parallel to the workplane (level on the base plate, as before), and **Align to workplane: Parallel | Across X | Across Y** lays it on the workplane or square to it, through the workplane's origin where that crosses the parts. The AI bridge has `orientation` on `layerling_set_section_view` and `alongWorkplane` on `layerling_split_objects`; `layerling_measure_section` and `layerling_export_section_svg` follow a turned section view; both skills say so. Texts in all 14 languages, the guide (chapters on the view and on solids, every language) describes both. Unit tests in `sectionFrame.test.ts`; tried in the browser on a cube tilted by 30°: the cut parallel to its sloped face was a 40 × 40 mm square, measuring on it gave 40 mm, the view cut showed the hexagon a cube makes across that view, the split plane started on the slope and Across X split the cube through the workplane's origin. A modifier key with the mouse wheel to push the cut was left out, as the wheel already zooms. Asked for by @Elkniwt on GitHub.
- **The snap grid menu now really lies over the Shapes panel (#616):** The fix in 1.93.0 raised the corner of the 3D view, but that corner lies inside the editor's own layer, and the Shapes panel belongs to the toolbar, which lies above the whole editor; so beside a pinned Shapes panel the upper entries were still hidden behind it. The menu is now put onto the page itself while it is open (a portal to the page, fixed above the snap grid button), above the toolbar and its panels, and on a low window it still scrolls. Measured in a headless Edge with the Shapes panel laid over the menu: all entries lie on top and can be clicked, and a click picks the grid. Reported by @grmonsey on GitHub.

## 1.93.1

- **More traced pictures come in whole (#584):** Three more pictures of the same kind (a drumming dance, children with a football, a homestead) still came in as a fifth of their area after 1.93.0, and the larger closing it tried did not help. The cause was in the check: the rounded outline had points a few millionths of a unit apart, the check merges points that close and then saw edges of four triangles, and a body that the kernel had built right was thrown away for the plain build, which leaves out any outline it cannot close. The closed outline is now cleaned of such points (0.002) before it is extruded, and all four pictures of the report come in whole with every outline. Reported by @stefaanl on GitHub.

- **The cards of the shape settings stay open or shut (#632):** Properties, Position, Rotation, Taper and Twist (and the cards of the thread and the gear) folded back to their start each time the settings were closed and opened again, so a card you use all the time, Rotation or Taper, had to be opened again for every shape. Layerling now remembers in this browser which cards you left open or shut, for the next shape and the next visit. What nobody has touched starts as before. Guide (EN/DE) updated. Reported by @prmod3d on GitHub.
- **Guide fixes in several languages:** The French shortcuts page showed the word "{{raccourcis}}" instead of the table of shortcuts, two French headings showed as plain text with "##" in front, two French links into a chapter went nowhere, and the French chapter on threads called them "fils" and "sujets" instead of "filetages". Arabic headings got no anchor, so the table of contents and links into a chapter of the Arabic guide went nowhere; they now take the anchor of the English heading in their place. The Danish AI chapter lacked two tools, the skill named a section-view tool that does not exist, and the printing chapter in every language spoke of fifty printers where there are over 220. A new test checks every language folder for these mistakes.
- **Node.js 22.18 or newer, said and checked:** The README asked for Node.js 20, but the guide and font scripts load TypeScript files directly, which needs 22.18; with an older Node.js the build stopped with a puzzling error. `package.json` now names the version, `npm run dev` and `npm run build` check it first with a clear message, and the Windows quickstart checks an already installed Node.js too.
- **Smoother turning, resizing and edge picking:** Every change to a selected body threw away the materials of its footprint on the plate and of the dashed guides and made new ones, and three.js compiled their shaders again each time; the Fillet and Chamfer tools did the same for every edge line whenever the pointer moved onto another edge, and drew all edge lines again at every render of the editor. These materials are now made once and shared. Turning a large import also counted its loose parts again at every step; the count is now kept per mesh. Measured in the browser: turning a 135,000-triangle import ten times blocked the page for 4.9 s, now 0.6 s; moving the pointer over the 722 edges of a gear with Fillet open blocked it for 11.7 s, now not at all.

## 1.93.0

- **A traced picture with many touching outlines comes in whole (#584):** A black-and-white drawing traced from a picture (the guitarist of a second file) came in as a handful of pieces, about a fifth of its area. The kernel built the body well, but at a few corners where outlines meet it was left with an edge of four triangles, so the check threw the body away and the plain build, which leaves out any outline it cannot close, put in only fragments. When the first closing (0.05) leaves such an edge, the kernel now tries 0.1, 0.2 and 0.35 and takes the first body that is clean; the drawing keeps its 345 x 277 mm and all its outlines. Reported by @stefaanl on GitHub.

- **Chamfer + Round on a cone with a pointed tip rounds the right edge (forum, DrJones):** On a cone that ends in a point, the foot came out as a round that ran straight down with a kink where it met the wall, and no chamfer on the plate. The tip of the wall is an edge of no length, and the check that tells the old wall from a new face failed on it, so the wall was taken for a new face and the edge between the plate and the chamfer was rounded instead of the one between the chamfer and the wall. That edge is now left out of the check; a cone with a tip gets the chamfer on the plate and the round into the wall, like a cylinder or a truncated cone. A very flat cone (a wall flatter than the 45 degree chamfer) still cannot take a chamfer + round, as the chamfer would stand steeper than the wall.

- **The knurl takes taper, lean and twist (#630):** Taper, Twist & Lean were switched off for the knurl although its outline is pushed up like a star's or a capsule's. They now work on all three patterns. A straight or round knurl stays an exact CAD body: a taper and a lean are a loft of its outline, and its 45 degree chamfers are cut by a turned bound that narrows and leans with the body; a twist is a smooth loft through sections that follow the fineness of the grooves (7.5 degrees for few grooves, 90 / number of grooves degrees for more), because sections 7.5 degrees apart let the surface swing out 1.2 mm beyond the ridges at 16 grooves and 45 degrees of twist. A taper that differs across and along keeps the chamfered knurl on its display mesh, as the crossed (diamond) pattern always does. Tried with the kernel in the tapered-bodies e2e test: tapered, leaning and twisted knurls with and without chamfer, turned and mirrored, and twists up to 180 degrees with 30 and 60 grooves stay within 0.05 mm of their own radius; not looked at on screen. Asked on GitHub.

- **A chamfer along the straight side of a tapered rounded box is straight (#612):** A rounded box tapered towards the top (a wider base than top) was built as one loft through its whole outline, so every side came out as a B-spline surface, flat sides included, and a chamfer or fillet along a bottom edge followed it: on a 100 mm box tapered to 80 mm the chamfer faces along the straight sides were up to 1.4 mm off a flat surface, visibly curved. A tapered outline of straight sides and arcs is now built piece by piece: each straight side a plane (checked to be flat), each rounded corner the ruled surface between its two arcs, and where the pieces do not close into one body the old loft is still the fallback. The box then has six plane faces, and a chamfer along it leaves flat strips. Tried with the kernel on the report's shape and in the whole e2e suite (375 tests, a new one for this); not looked at on screen. Reported by @Ynaught-42 on GitHub.

- **A stencil sheet with many fine holes imports again (#626):** An SVG that is one sheet with a dozen fine holes (a paint stencil with bridges, drawn as one even-odd path) was refused with "SVG has no filled contours that can be converted into a solid". The step that parts outlines meeting in a corner (#584) left edges of four triangles on it, and the import took the body for broken. When that step does not come out clean, the outlines are now joined as they are; where that is no cleaner either, nothing changes. The sheet of the report imports with all its holes. Reported by @Rghezzi69 on GitHub.

- **Sketch points snap to the middle of a line and the centre of a circle, and a line can be drawn from its middle (#624):** A new or dragged point in the sketch now snaps (blue ring) to the middle of every straight line, construction lines included, to the centre of every circle and rounded corner (the four arcs of a circle give one centre), and to the middles of the sides of a rectangular body shown underneath; before, only corners of bodies underneath and crossings of construction lines were taken. A point that is being dragged ignores the middle of its own lines. New: **Line from the middle** in the Draw group (also in the command search): switched on, the first click is the middle and the second the end, and the line goes on the same length to the other side, as two lines meeting in the middle point. Unit tests for the snap points; the guide has a new section in English and German; Czech and Dutch have the new texts, the other languages show them in English. Not tried on screen. Suggested by @structurednewstore-rgb on GitHub.

- **One entry for the countersink and the double countersink (#621):** The shape library listed the countersink and the double countersink as two shapes, which are one hole with a cone at one end or at both. The library now lists only the countersink, and its properties have a switch **Both sides** that makes it a double countersink and back, with every measure kept. Bodies that already are a double countersink, in saved designs, the history, custom shapes and over the MCP, are unchanged and show the switch on; the command search still finds "double countersink". Guide (EN/DE) updated.

- **Interactive Help in the properties of a shape leads to the right part of the guide (#424):** In the Properties, Rotation, Taper and Twist cards, a click on a field, a slider, the button that takes a value back to its default, the checkbox Keep proportions or Save as default and Reset defaults asked the guide for a word like "Length" or "Width" and landed in some section that merely used it. The properties, the taper and the twist of a plain shape now lead to the guide's section "The shape's settings", and the rotation to "Rotating and setting the pivot". The special shapes (thread, gear, knurl and so on) keep the answers of their own. Reported by @prmod3d on GitHub.

- **The snap grid menu lies over the other panels and scrolls on a low window (#616):** The list of snap grids (Off, 0.1 mm ... Brick, "Snap to shapes") opens upwards from the corner of the Properties panel. Beside a pinned Shapes panel the upper entries lay behind that panel and could not be clicked, so the user had to shrink the Shapes panel to reach them. While the menu is open, the corner it belongs to now stands above the floating panels, and on a window too low for the whole list the menu scrolls (`max-height`, `overflow-y: auto`). Tried on screen with the Properties panel and the Shapes panel open: the menu is on top; not tried with the Shapes panel pinned next to the Properties panel as in the report. Reported by @grmonsey on GitHub.

- **The split tool draws a white line where the cut will go (#615):** The translucent plane of the split tool showed the cut only as a surface, and where it really meets the body was hard to see. A white line now runs over the bodies to be split, wherever the plane meets them, as in a slicer; it follows the plane while you move or turn it. Bodies of more than 600,000 triangles get the plane only. Guide (EN/DE) updated. Reported by @jasonleshuk on GitHub.

## 1.92.2

- **The number field of the corner ruler closes when its bodies are deselected (#614):** A click on a green number of the corner ruler opens a field to type the distance. It closed on Enter, Esc or when it lost the focus, but not when the selection changed: after a click on empty space that left the field its focus, it stayed in the view with no body selected, as an active field that belonged to nothing. It now goes (without applying what is typed) as soon as the bodies it was opened for are no longer all selected. Not tried on screen. Reported by @willi1955 on GitHub.
- **"Delete all reference points" is in the menu of an empty spot too (#603):** The entry only stood in the right-click menu of a selected body, so someone who had marked a point with the tape measure and then right-clicked next to it found only Paste, Select all and Select multiple, and could not tell how to get rid of the point. The menu of an empty spot of the 3D view now has it as well, whenever the design holds a reference point. The guide (chapter 10, English and German) names both places. Reported by @Cowski4444 on GitHub.
- **An OBJ from Tinkercad comes in lying flat, not on its side (#599):** Tinkercad writes the OBJ with Z up and says so in the first line ("Object Export From Tinkercad"), but layerling read every foreign OBJ with Y up, so such a file stood on its edge: a part that is 435 mm wide and 120 mm high came in 244 mm high. The Tinkercad header is now recognised and the file is turned upright, like layerling's own Z-up files. Such a body carries its mesh itself, so a saved project never rebuilds it with the old axes; an OBJ without that header is read as before. Reported by @kwjaarsveld-star.
- **A .lyl file opens with a double click (#605):** The web app manifest now declares `file_handlers` for `.lyl`, and the start page reads `window.launchQueue`, so an installed layerling (Chrome or Edge on a computer) registers as a program for .lyl files and opens the design through the usual import, with the file handle kept so Ctrl+S saves back into it. It works on a computer only: Android has no file handlers for installed web apps, so there a design is opened with "Open a design or import geometry" (#605). Guide text (EN/DE) and a What's new card added.
- **Docker: the preview pictures are saved again:** The image runs the server as the user `nextjs`, but `/app` belongs to root, so `/app/.codex/project-thumbnails` could not be created: every POST to `/api/project-thumbnail` failed with 500 and the cards stayed on "No preview yet" (reported by mail from a Mac with OrbStack). The Dockerfile now creates the folder and gives it to `nextjs`.
- **Saving to the server works behind a reverse proxy that closes HTTPS (#608):** The shared-project and thumbnail routes compared the browser's `Origin` strictly with the host and protocol the app saw. A proxy that ends TLS but sends no `X-Forwarded-Proto` made the app see plain http, so every save failed with "Shared projects only accept requests from this site." (while the IP address worked). The check now takes an `https` Origin on the very same host when the proxy does not name the protocol; it stays strict when it does. New `LAYERLING_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` (full origins or bare hosts, comma-separated, read at run time) for a proxy that rewrites the host, in `store.php` as well. A refusal now writes the reason to the server log. Shared in `lib/sameOrigin.ts`, tests in `sameOrigin.test.ts`.

## 1.92.1

- **The selection frame that takes what it touches is green (#572):** Dragging a frame over the workplane from right to left (or the other way round with Alt) takes every part the frame touches, but the frame only differed from the enclosing one by its dashed edge. It now has its own colour, green and dashed, the way CAD programs mark it; the frame that must enclose the parts stays orange.
- **The snap grid stays reachable with several parts selected (#601):** The panel for a selection of several parts reached down to the bottom of the screen and covered the snap grid control in the lower right corner, so on a laptop the grid could not be changed without first deselecting. The panel now carries the snap grid (and the snap-to-shapes switch) at its foot, the way the panel of a single part does; once the panel is dragged away, the control is back in the corner.
- **Narrower: an outline moved in by a width (#558):** The fill of a sketch body could keep its area and widen it ("Wider"), or build a wall inside, outside or centred, but not move the edge of a filled area inwards. A new choice, **Narrower**, under Fill in the properties of a sketch body and under Align in the sketch's line panel, keeps the area filled and takes the line width off all round; the holes grow by the same. One sketch then gives the fit hole and the part that passes through it 0.5 mm smaller, or a wall inside a shape. It is built by the exact kernel where it can (an arc stays a cylinder), a width the outline cannot give leaves nothing, and the MCP bridge takes `shrink` for `align` too. Asked for by @jbeardon (#558), with @RobbieKnobbie.
- **The corner ruler names its numbers by the axis they count along (#597):** The three green numbers of the corner ruler carried the tooltips "X position to ruler" and "Y position to ruler" by the arm they stand on, but the arm that is called x in the code runs to the back at first, so the number called X changed when you moved a body to the back, and the one called Y when you moved it sideways. The numbers are now named by where their arm points: X to the right, Y to the back, Z up, also after you turn the corner by a quarter. Reported by @willi1955 on GitHub.
- **The space between the letters can be set (#594):** A text and a name tag have a new property, the letter spacing, in per cent of the letter size on top of the font's own advance: 0 leaves it as the font made it, above it the letters stand further apart (up to 300), below it tighter (down to -50). Straight and curved text have it; the letters keep their size and the box follows the line, so a name tag stays as tall as it was. It is built into a copy of the typeface with the advances changed, so the layout, the fit into the box, the fills and the edge tool all read the same spacing. Arabic and Hebrew take none, their letters join up. In the properties (Letter spacing (%)), in the MCP action layerling_update_object (`spacing`), in the saved file (`textSpacing`) and in the guide, chapter 8. Tried with a straight text at -20, 0, 60 and 150 per cent and a curved one; the translations of the label are in English, German, Czech, Dutch, French, Slovenian and Russian, the others fall back to English. Suggested by @Moosli-moo on GitHub.
- **Bodies under a sketch show their real outline (#596):** In the sketch view every body on the workplane that was neither an imported mesh nor a round shape was drawn as the rectangle of its width and depth, so a polygon, a star, a heart and a wedge all looked like the same square. Such a body is now drawn with the outline of its own mesh (the triangles of its underside), standing where it stands and turned as it is turned, and the points of the sketch snap to that outline. Boxes and round shapes keep their cheaper drawing, and a body of more than 20,000 triangles stays a rectangle. Tried in the sketch view with a polygon, a rotated polygon, a star, a heart and a wedge: their outlines agree with the 3D view; the snapping to the new outline and groups of bodies (still a rectangle) were not tried. Reported by @cadbent on GitHub.
- **The shortcuts print on one sheet (#490):** The printed list of shortcuts (1.87.0) was in large type and ran over three pages in the editor and in sketch mode. It now fits one side of an A4 or a Letter sheet: black on white, three columns, without a heading, a long group running over into the next column, a hairline between the rows, the keys on the right of each name or below it when they do not fit. Measured by printing to PDF in headless Edge: one page for the editor and for sketch mode, on A4 and on Letter, in German. Not looked at in other languages, where longer names may need more room. Suggested by @WimSnor on GitHub.
- **The tape measure snaps at a right angle and deletes on Shift or Ctrl click (#589):** Measuring the shortest distance between two inner walls of a hole or between two bodies needed eyeballing the second point, and removing a measurement meant switching to Delete measurement part first. When the first point of a measurement has been placed, the second point now catches at the foot of the perpendicular on an opposing edge or face (and across a cylindrical bore along the first face's normal, or radially on a circular edge) within 16 pixels of the pointer, or anywhere on the hovered edge or face while Shift is held; on the same face or on the workplane, Shift locks the measurement to the main axis. Moving an endpoint of a measurement does the same.
