While Add measurement is active, holding Shift or Ctrl (Cmd on a Mac) highlights a measurement point or line under the pointer and a click removes it right away. Described in chapter 10 of the guide in English and German. Suggested by @lukyanenkomax on GitHub.

## 1.92.0

- **A revolve can wind up as one continuous strand:** A revolved sketch always turned its outline into closed rings, at most once round, so a filament spool or a coil came out as a stack of separate rings. A revolved body now has **Pitch** in its properties: how far it climbs per turn, in mm. Above 0 the outline winds up as a helix, one continuous strand, and **Sweep** becomes **Turns**, up to 200; a negative number winds left-handed. Every closed outline in the sketch becomes a strand of its own. A pitch of 0 gives rings again, and a revolve without a pitch is saved as before. A helix is built as a mesh, so Fillet, Chamfer and Hollow do not take it. Turns set closer than the outline is high melt into one body: the overlap is united in the plane before the outline is wound, so a solid spool core with a 2 mm pitch can be cut and bored and still exports closed. Besides that, French named Pitch "Emplacement" and Turns "Virages" (on threads and springs too); they are "Pas" and "Tours" now. Contributed by @rmpel.
- **Interactive Help leads to the setting you click, not to a long section (#424):** A click on a setting in the Workspace settings led to "Grid and snapping", which described almost all the settings in one long paragraph, or to nothing: three settings were not in the guide at all (the angles of turned bodies, the overhang mark, saving a colour), and a field whose label holds its hint or its value (the Fast mode switch, the sliders, the colour buttons, the drop-down lists) got no answer. The guide now has a section for each group of settings under Appearance, Measurement, Workplane, Shape defaults and Saved history (in English and German), and "Choosing colours" for the colour card of a body, with the eyedropper and the HEX, RGB and HSL values of the browser's picker, the recently used colours and the pin that keeps a colour. The fields, sliders, colour buttons, the colour pickers and the list entries in the settings name themselves for the help. Reported by @prmod3d on GitHub.
- **Alt turns the selection frame around (#572):** Holding Alt while a selection frame is dragged makes it work the other way round for that one drag. With "The direction of the frame decides" (#430) on, a frame dragged to the right then takes everything it touches and one dragged to the left only what lies completely inside; with it off, Alt takes only what lies completely inside, in either direction. The frame is drawn dashed while it takes what it touches and a direction or Alt makes that a choice. Described in the chapter Select and arrange (English and German; the other languages show the English text). Suggested by @prmod3d on GitHub.
- **An engraving wrapped around a cylinder cuts all the way through (#578):** A logo wrapped inward ("Engraving") and taken out of a cylinder as a hole left a hair-thin skin of the wall standing over parts of the letters, and tiny loose pieces (the export reported 25 and 49 of them). The outside of the wrapped body lay exactly on the cylinder's wall; the cylinder is made of 96 flat facets, the wrapped body is cut every 2 degrees, so in places the hole ended up to 0.015 mm under the wall. The outside of an inward-wrapped body now reaches 0.05 mm beyond the given diameter (the inside stays where it was, the body is 0.05 mm thicker), and a solid copy stands 0.05 mm proud of the wall. Measured on the file of the report's design, rebuilt with text: of 2706 triangles of the logo's outer face, the cut left skin over about 110 before and none now (the cut's skin triangles fell from 1543 to 922). Not looked at on screen. Reported by @makinglayerschannel on GitHub.
- **The chamfillet on a cylinder sits at the foot (#570):** A chamfillet on the bottom edge of a cylinder came out reversed: the fillet at the foot, the chamfer above it, and the rim of the top rounded as well. The treatment took the cylinder wall, left by the chamfer, for a new face, because it compared the wall at a point on the axis, which says nothing about a curved face. It now compares the wall at points on its own edges. The foot is a chamfer on the plate that blends into the wall, and the top is left alone.
- **An SVG whose shapes touch in a corner is imported whole (#584):** In a traced picture two shapes often meet in a single point. The two bodies then shared an edge of four faces, the build was refused, and the import fell back to building shape by shape and left out the ones that did not close, so a picture came in smaller and without some of its parts. The outlines are now joined as flat areas first and a point where two of them meet is parted by a hair (0.01 units) before the body is extruded once.
- **The wheel in a size field stops at the lower limit (#576):** The mouse wheel in a number field of the properties went on below the smallest size, into negative numbers: the body kept the limit of 0.01 mm, but the field showed the negative figure until it was left. The figure the wheel sets is now held at the field's lower limit (positions, which may be negative, are not held). Tried in the browser with a box: ten notches down stop at 1, five more at 0.01. Reported by @prmod3d on GitHub.
- **A very small body can be moved with a finger (#574):** On a touch screen each handle catches a finger from 15 pixels around it. A body that is under about 80 pixels across on the screen was covered by those zones all over, so a touch on it grabbed a handle and resized the body instead of moving it. For such a body the wide zones are now left out and the handles are caught only on their own square, so the middle is free to drag. Tried with touch emulation in the browser: a 3 mm body loses the zones, a 60 mm body keeps them; not tried on an iPad. Reported by @grandmaofdragonz on GitHub.
- **Japanese can be typed into a text:** Typing "ishi" with a Japanese IME into the Text field of a text or a name tag came out as "いｓい" and could not be converted. The field committed every keystroke through the editor, for a name tag after an await, and was set back to the older text meanwhile, which cut the word being composed short. The field now keeps what is typed while it is being edited, and a word being composed (Japanese, Chinese, Korean) is committed once it is done.
- **The cut face of a see-through body is see-through (#575):** The filled cut face of the section view (#516) was always drawn solid, so a transparent box showed an opaque face at the front. It follows the body now: a body set to transparent gets a cut face with the same opacity (0.4) and without writing depth. Not seen in a browser, the 3D view does not draw in the test window; the type check and the tests pass. Reported by @Elkniwt on GitHub.
- **The sketch's shape panel has its own place, pin and size (#577):** The Shapes panel of the sketch and the one of the 3D editor were the same panel and saved their position, pin and size under one name, so moving or pinning one moved or pinned the other. The sketch now keeps its own (`layerling.sketch.shapeMenu*`), and its edges and corners pull its size like the 3D editor's panel; narrow, only the pictures stay. Tried in the browser: size and pin of the sketch are stored apart from those of the 3D editor. Reported by @prmod3d on GitHub.
- **The wheel in a number field: Hollow steps a whole millimetre, and the Sketch shape fields have it (#536):** In the Hollow window the wall's range of a body of 40 mm runs to 19.6, and a range under 20 counted as a short one, so one notch changed the wall by a tenth, ten times less than in every other panel (Shift gave the whole number). A range of 8 or more counts as long now, so a notch is a whole unit there too. The two fields of the shape menu in sketch mode, **Polygon sides** and **Star: inner size**, had no wheel at all; they take it now: one notch is one, Shift ten. Reported by @prmod3d on GitHub.
- **Thin, small letters are no longer lost in the export (#585):** A small flat text lost letters in an STL, 3MF or OBJ: measured with "Breakfast Point  Men's Shed", 24 pieces at 86 mm wide and 10 mm high, but 23 at 0.4 mm high, 18 at 40 mm wide and 0.4 mm high, and 3 at 31 mm wide and 0.4 mm high. The export leaves out tiny loose pieces that a cut or the colour separation leaves behind (#352; a closed piece under half a cubic millimetre), and it applied that to every body, so the dot of an i, an apostrophe or a small letter in a thin text went with them. It now looks only at bodies that a cut, a merge of several bodies or the colour separation changed; a body they did not touch is written as it is. With the fix all 24 pieces are there in all nine sizes tried, in STL and in 3MF. Not tried in Bambu Studio itself, and a text that is cut by a hole or merged with another body can still lose a piece under that limit. Reported by @Keving1950.
- **A traced drawing no longer makes hundreds of thousands of triangles (#573):** An imported SVG with very many curves - a traced drawing of about 900 curves, 60 mm wide - came out of the kernel as 353,000 triangles, so a few copies of it could not be joined: the limit for cutting and joining is 250,000. The kernel cut every tiny curve into pieces of at most 9 degrees, long after the 0.05 mm of accuracy was met. A sketch with more than 200 curves is now cut with an angle of 0.5 radians (about 29 degrees) and the same 0.05 mm: the drawing is 35,000 triangles, ten times fewer, and looks the same at that size. A sketch drawn by hand, with a few curves, keeps its fine pieces. The fineness is kept with the body, so that an edge treatment later does not cut it coarser. Reported by @shanni1783 on GitHub.
- **No offline promise where there is no service worker (#571):** The card "Install layerling as an app" ("works without internet") and the "Works offline" tile of the start page appeared wherever the browser could in principle run a service worker, that is on any https address, but the service worker is written and registered only in the static export. The Docker image, a Node server, never has one, so behind https it promised offline use that did not work. Both now ask one place (`lib/offlineSupport.ts`) first: static export, a secure address and a browser with service workers. Tried in a browser: the static export on localhost shows card and tile as before, the development server (not an export) shows neither. Reported by @foofoodog.
- **The view cube can sit in any corner:** Settings > Appearance has a new **View cube position**: Top left (as before, and the default), Top right, Bottom left or Bottom right. In any corner but the top left, the camera controls on the left move up into the spot the cube left. Bottom right puts the cube just above the snap grid control, and the snap menu still opens over it. While the properties of a shape are docked on the right, a cube on the right steps to the left of them. The corner is kept in this browser like the toolbar size, and a script in the page head sets it before the first paint, so the cube does not jump on load. The cube's styles moved out of `globals.css` into `viewCube.css`. The guide (chapter 2) describes the setting in every language. A phone-wide window (640 px or less) has no room for another corner: the setting is hidden there and the cube stays in the top left, whatever was chosen; a wider window brings the chosen corner back.
- **The History view says what the arrows undo and redo:** Under the slider of the History view, a new row names the step the left arrow would undo ("Last step: Moved Box") and the one the right arrow would redo ("Next step: Grouped 2 objects into Group"), with "none" at the oldest and the newest state. The history keeps no name for the action behind a step, so the step is read from the difference between the two states, which also works for the history of a design saved before. It tells adding, deleting, duplicating ("Made 5 copies of Peg"), grouping, ungrouping, opening a group with Edit group and closing it, splitting, moving, turning, resizing, colour, hole or solid, renaming, mirroring, fillets, chamfers, hollowing and deleted faces, hiding, locking, notes, reference points and the workplane apart; whatever else changed reads "Changed Box". Opening a group for editing and closing it change the bodies just as ungrouping and grouping do, so they are told apart only within the session; in a history opened from a file they read as Ungrouped and Grouped. The sentences are in all 14 languages. The MCP action `layerling_set_history_view` reports both steps as `lastStep` and `nextStep`. The three History actions of the MCP bridge moved out of the editor into `historyViewMcp.ts`. Tried with unit tests built on the editor's own grouping; not yet tried in the browser.

- **The video lists of the READMEs follow the shared list:** The video list in README.md, README.de.md and README.nl.md was written by hand and had fallen behind (four of nine videos, one of them replaced since). `npm run videos:sync` now fills all three from the video chapter of the guide in their language, and `npm run videos:sync -- --discussion` does the same for the first post of Discussion #296 (with `--apply` it writes it). A test checks that the READMEs equal the chapter and list the videos of `layerlingVideos.ts` in the same order. No change in the app.

## 1.91.0

- **A loose hole is no longer lost in the export when its body touches another (#566):** A hole left ungrouped is cut out of the body it reaches when you export. But when that body touched or overlapped another body of the same colour, the export joined the two again from their uncut shapes, and the hole was gone from the STL, 3MF or OBJ file and from the material estimate, which counted the part as solid. A body that a hole was cut from is now joined as its cut mesh. The cut is checked in the export by volume: two boxes of 20 x 20 x 10 mm side by side with a hole of 8 mm through the first one export 7,497.5 mm³ instead of 8,000. Found, with a test and a patch, by @aktrftm on GitHub.
- **The cut face of the section view no longer lags behind the plane (#516):** The face was cut a moment after the plane moved, and that moment started again with every move, so a plane moved faster than that moment (30 ms) never let the face be cut, and it came back only when the hand rested. That is the lag @rondlh saw with 1.90.0 on a fast machine, up to about a second. A round now starts for the newest plane at once, one at a time; the rounds in between are skipped instead of thrown away. Measured in a test browser with a plane moved every few milliseconds: with the old delay lengthened to 100 ms to stand for a faster machine the face was cut once, at the end of the drag; now it is cut about every 50 to 100 ms during the drag and 25 ms after the last move. The lag on his machine itself was not seen, only the mechanism.
- **A long file name no longer widens the Export window (#563):** With a file name linked to the design (STL in Chrome and Edge), the footer showed "Saves into <name>" on one line next to the buttons, and a long name made the whole window as wide as the name: its text was cut at the edge and a scroll bar ran along the bottom. The name now has its own line, wrapping if it must, and the buttons stand under it. Reported by @ZaJules on GitHub.
- **Ctrl and the arrow keys pan the view:** With nothing selected, the arrow keys turned the view (15° a press, 90° with Shift), but moving it sideways needed the mouse. With Ctrl (Cmd on a Mac) held they now slide the view across the screen: a tenth of the picture's height a press, half of it with Shift as well. Like the plain arrows, they move the model the way they point, and the pivot stays within the same range as with the mouse. With something selected, Ctrl+↑ and Ctrl+↓ still raise and lower it. The shortcuts window lists the new keys. The view code behind the arrow keys moved out of the viewport into files of its own (`viewArrowKeys.ts`, `viewportKeyboard.ts`).
- **Raised floor for Gridfinity bins (#455):** A bin with a raised inner floor, a blank to cut the shapes of tools into, had to be made by grouping a box into it. A Gridfinity bin now has **Floor height**, measured from the underside of the bin (5.95 mm until it is set), and the bin is solid up to it. At the highest, where the lip's support leaves the wall (19.35 mm in a bin of 3 units), the lip stays whole and the feet of a stacked bin stay 1.3 mm clear; without the lip the floor goes up to the top, and the bin is a solid block. A bin of 1 height unit with the lip has no room for it. Finger scoops start on the raised floor and label shelves can reach less far out above it; once the floor leaves them no room, their switches go until it is lowered again. The bin stays an exact body for the STEP export. Also through the MCP action `layerling_create_shape` with `gridfinityFloorHeight`. Suggested by @tskmstr. Contributed by @mlugo-apx.
- **Folders for custom shapes:** The custom shapes can be sorted into folders, three levels deep. The new folder button next to Save selection makes one at the top, in this browser or on the server, and the folder button with the plus on a folder makes one inside it (a folder on the third level holds shapes only). A folder opens and closes with its arrow, its number counts the shapes in it and below it, and its pencil and bin rename and delete it (deleting takes the folders and shapes in it along, after asking). Saving a selection offers the folders next to the name, shown with their path, and a new button on each tile moves the shape into another folder or back out; folders themselves do not move. On the server they are folders inside `Custom shapes`, so the start page shows them too; in the browser a backup keeps them. The MCP action `layerling_save_custom_shape` takes a `folder` path such as `Brackets/M3`, and `layerling_list_custom_shapes` reports the folder path of each shape. The guide describes folders in every language.

## 1.90.0

- **Custom shapes keep their pinned notes (#109):** A custom shape saved from bodies that had notes pinned to them takes the notes along. When the shape is put into a design they come in closed, pinned to the new bodies and with new numbers: if the design already has the notes 1 to 3, the shape's two notes become 4 and 5. Free notes and reference points belong to the design and stay behind; a design that has no room for more notes takes as many as fit and says so. The guide's chapter on custom shapes says so too. Contributed by @gogades.
- **Arabic release notes:** The "What's new" card has its title and text in Arabic for the 139 release notes from 1.72.3 to 1.88.0; later notes show in English until their Arabic follows. Contributed by @sevagjb.
- **A pocket of a whole depth can be chamfered (forum, DrJones):** A pocket whose hole ended exactly on a face of the body - a hole 5.000 mm high pulled through a workplane on a slope, where the snap grid gives whole values - made the exact body for the edge tool with a skin as thin as a rounding error: the edge tool listed two edges on one line and more edges than there are, and a chamfer on the pocket's rim failed with "cannot be chamfered together at this size", while 4.99 or 5.001 worked. The cut of a hole from a body now counts faces and edges within a thousandth of a millimetre as one, so a pocket of exactly 5 mm gives the same faces as one of 5.001. Reported by DrJones in the forum, with his bug report.
- **The slider of the fillet and chamfer moves in tenths of a millimetre (forum, DrJones):** The slider for the distance or radius had steps of a thousandth of a millimetre over its whole range, so it gave values like 2.347. It now moves in tenths of a millimetre (a sixty-fourth of an inch with inches) and goes to 10 mm; a larger value is typed into the field, which still takes any value up to what the body allows, to the thousandth.
- **QR codes (#553):** The shape library has a new entry **QR code**, after Icon tag. It opens a window: type a text or a web address (several lines too; the window counts the bytes against what the chosen error correction holds), choose the error correction L, M, Q or H, the width or the module size, the quiet zone and the build - the code alone as a sketch body 4 mm high, raised 1 mm on a 3 mm plate as a bundle of two colours for a multicolour print, or engraved into the plate - and see the code in a preview before OK. The modules are merged into one outline, so there are no gaps or seams between them; modules that only touch at a corner are joined by a tiny bridge so every code is a clean solid. Modules under 0.8 mm get a warning (a rule of thumb, not a guarantee). The body scales, moves, turns, groups and becomes a hole like any other, keeps its settings in the project, and its properties have a **QR code** card to change the text and make the code again where it stands. The encoding is the MIT-licensed `qrcode-generator` package; the tests read every level back with `jsQR`. The AI bridge has `layerling_create_qr_code`, and the guide (chapters 8 and 14) and both skills explain it. Asked for by @DannyDesignStudio.
- **Angles keep two decimals (#552):** A rotation was rounded to one decimal, so 11.25° (the middle between 0° and the 22.5° ticks of the rotation circle, which Shift offers) became 11.3°, and the number fields showed whole degrees, rounding what was typed. An angle is now kept with two decimals, also after a turn with the rotation circle, a boolean step or an edit in the field, and the angle fields show them (11.25 stays 11.25). A turn of less than half a degree still counts as no turn, as before. Asked for by @DannyDesignStudio. Tried in the number field in the browser; the turn with the rotation circle itself was not dragged.
- **The revolve sketch shows the front view when it is edited again (#525):** The side view of the other bodies in a revolve sketch (1.89.0) only applied to a sketch drawn on the base plate. Editing a revolved body starts from a workplane at the foot of the body, which is above the plate as soon as the body stands on something, so the sketch fell back to the view from above and showed the plate as a flat rectangle. A revolved body that stands upright now shows the same front view when it is edited. A body drawn on a workplane on a face is unchanged. Reported by @willi1955. Tried by reading the code and with a test of the rule, not in the editor yet.
- **The filled cut face follows the plane (#516):** The cut face of the section view (1.88.0) appeared only a moment after the plane stopped, so while you moved the plane the body looked hollow again. The slow part of the cut - joining the bodies of the design - does not depend on the plane, so it is made once for a state of the design and kept; moving the plane now only slices it again, and the face follows within a few hundredths of a second. A changed design still removes the old face at once. Reported by @rondlh on GitHub.
- **The mouse wheel changes numbers in the tool panels too (#536):** The wheel in a clicked number field (#226) only worked in the properties of a shape. It now works in the fields of Chamfer, Fillet, Hollow, Pattern, Hole pattern, Face, Mate faces, Scale in percent, Split (position and tilt) and the Section view, with the same keys: a step a notch, ten times as much with Shift, a tenth with Ctrl, a hundredth with Alt. One shared helper (`useWheelField`) does it for all of them. Asked for by @prmod3d. Tried in the browser with a synthetic wheel turn in the Section view, Split and Hollow; not tried with a real wheel or a Mac.
- **Italian interface completed:** 112 texts that were still English in Italian are translated - the folders of the dashboard, the interactive help, the Face and Sweep tools, bringing objects back from the history, the edge window and more - so the Italian interface now covers all 2,623 texts. Contributed by @maxciava in PR #543.
- **Notes stay with their bodies through grouping, ungrouping and editing a group:** A note pinned to a body was cut loose whenever the body got a new id - when it was grouped, when its group was ungrouped, even when its group was opened with Edit group - and then fell back to the spot where it had first been placed, which after a move could be far away or inside another body. Now Group takes the note into the group, and Ungroup hands it to the visible part under it, a group inside included; hidden parts are passed over. While a group is open for editing, its notes go with the part under them, so they move when that part moves, and Done pins them to the group again where they stand; Cancel puts them back as they were. Other steps that replace a body, such as a cut or Separate parts, hand its notes to the new body under them in the same way. A note whose body is deleted, or that has no visible body under it any more, stays where its body last had it, free on the workplane. Grouping used to let go of notes on purpose; it now keeps them, as closing an edited group does.
- **Half-size grid for Gridfinity bins (#455):** A Gridfinity bin was always whole units of 42 mm. With **Half-size grid (21 mm)** switched on, a bin's units are cells of 21 mm, up to 12 across and deep, each with a foot of its own, 20.5 mm across at the top with the specification's steps; four of them stand in one socket of an ordinary baseplate, so bins of 1½ or 2½ units fit beside whole ones. The bin keeps its outline when the setting is switched on, and an odd number of cells is rounded up to whole units when it is switched off. With **Magnet holes** each foot gets one hole, placed where a magnet baseplate from another generator has its holes, as long as the bin's left back corner stands on a corner of the grid. The baseplate stays on the 42 mm grid, and a bin with the setting off is unchanged. Also through the MCP action `layerling_create_shape` with `gridfinityHalfGrid`. Suggested by @9ballrider. Contributed by @mlugo-apx.
- **Finger scoops for Gridfinity bins (#455):** Small parts sat in the edge between a bin's floor and its walls and had to be picked out. Any Gridfinity bin of 2 height units or more can now have a finger scoop along any of its four walls, several at once: **Finger scoop at the back**, **Finger scoop at the front**, **Finger scoop on the left** and **Finger scoop on the right**. The floor rises along a quarter circle into the wall, from wall to wall, so parts can be pushed out up the curve, and every floor edge of the bin is rounded by 2.8 mm. **Scoop radius** applies to all of them, at least 3.5 mm; until it is set, it is half the height up to the lip's support (6.7 mm in a bin of 3 units with the lip). The bin stays an exact body for the STEP export. Also through the MCP action `layerling_create_shape` with `gridfinityScoopFront` and the other `gridfinityScoop` settings. Suggested by @9ballrider. Contributed by @mlugo-apx.

## 1.89.0

- **A narrower shape library holds two columns (#527):** A shape library pulled to its own width needed 206 px for each column, so two columns took about 450 px (360 px at the small toolbar size), a little more than the Properties panel beside it. A column now needs 190 px: two columns fit in about 415 px, 32 px less at the default toolbar size, 26 px at the small one, and the names still fit (checked in German, where they are longest). Asked for by @prmod3d.
- **A diagnostic strip for devices we cannot try (#524):** With `diagnose` in the address, for example `https://layerling.com/?editor=1&diagnose=1`, a small box at the top left shows whether the page is still alive (two counters that stop when it hangs), the last touches and clicks with the element they hit, the longest task and the last errors. It is plain DOM, so it still draws when the app is stuck, and it is not there without the switch. For an iPad on which nothing answers any more after two taps.
- **The revolve sketch shows the other bodies from the front and puts the body where the profile was drawn (#525):** The revolve sketch drew the other bodies of the design as their outline from above (a plate as a flat rectangle), while the profile is a side view with the axis standing upright, so the profile could not be lined up with them; and the height at which it was drawn was dropped, the body always standing with its foot at height 0. On the base plate the other bodies are now projected onto the front view through the origin (width across, height up, the axis at the middle), the part edges too, and the finished body stands where the profile was drawn: the lowest point of the profile is its foot, the horizontal line through the middle is the ground, so a profile above a plate makes a body that stands on it. A body that is edited keeps its place as before, and a sketch on a workplane on a face is unchanged. Asked for by @willi1955 with Tinkercad's revolve sketch as the example.
- **The mouse wheel in a number field with Shift on a Mac (#226):** With Shift held, a Mac turns the wheel sideways and reports it as such, and the field only listened for the up-and-down turn, so Shift (ten times as much) did nothing there while Ctrl and Alt worked. The field reads the sideways turn too now when Shift is held. Reported by @WagnerBoyzRacing.
- **An exported OBJ stands upright in the slicer (#541):** STL and 3MF were written with Z up, as slicers expect, but the OBJ with Y up, so Cura and the Creality slicer showed such a part on its side. The OBJ is now written with Z up too, and says so in its header; layerling's own import knows that line and stands the file upright again. OBJ files exported before keep their Y-up header and still read as they did. The message after saving an STL to a file is worded more clearly (reported by @rondlh).
- **Fillet and Chamfer: several quick buttons, Horizontal edges, and the window stays open after Apply (#538):** A new quick button **Horizontal edges** picks the edges that lie flat (the rims of the top and the bottom of a box, a flat circle, any ledge between). With Shift, Ctrl or Cmd a click on a quick button adds its edges to the ones picked instead of replacing them, and the buttons whose edges make up the picking light together (Top and Bottom edges both, when those two were clicked). After **Apply** the tool opens again on the new body (the way it does since #514 after a feature is taken back), with the same kind and size and no edge picked, so that fillets of different sizes follow without opening the tool each time; its button then reads **Done** and closes it, as do Esc and the cross. Reported by @rondlh.
- **The list of features in the Fillet and Chamfer window stays open when one is taken back (#517):** Taking a feature back opens the tool again on the restored body (since #514), which builds the window anew, and the list of features shut itself with it, so every further feature meant opening the list again. The list now stays as it was left. Reported by @rondlh.
- **History view: open a group and bring back its parts (#540):** A group could only be brought back from the History view as a whole. Now select a group and choose **Open group** (or press E, as in the design): its parts are laid out to look at, and you pick one or more of them (click, Shift and click) and bring them back. Groups inside groups open the same way, the path is shown with a chevron, and **Back out** closes one level at a time. Contributed by @gogades.
- **A pinned shape library opens again at the next start (#505):** The pin was remembered in this browser, the panel was not: after closing the app the Shapes panel stayed closed with its pin still set, and only the next click on it showed it again in its place. A pinned panel now opens by itself when the editor starts. Leaving the sketch still closes the sketch's own shape palette; only the start no longer closes anything. Reported by @prmod3d.
- **On a phone the designs of the start page come first:** On a narrow screen (560 px and less) the saved designs started about 990 px from the top, more than a screen, behind the three big tiles and the backup note, so there was scrolling at every visit. Once there are designs, the three tiles are a short row of three, and the note that the designs live in this browser, with its backup buttons, stands behind the designs; the first design is in the first screen. On wider screens and with no designs yet nothing changes. Asked for by a supporter by mail.
- **Changing the height of a turned body keeps its base in place:** A body stands on its base (its position is the middle of its bottom face) and is turned about its centre, so a height typed or slid in the Properties panel moved the centre straight up by half the change and not along the body's own up axis; a body turned by 45 degrees slid by 3.5 mm for a change of 10 mm. A pocket on a sloped workplane therefore wandered along the plane when its height was changed. The patch now carries the position that keeps the middle of the base where it was; a patch that sets the position itself and a body that is not turned are left alone, and the same holds for the MCP action `layerling_update_object`. Reported by @DrJones in the forum.
- **iPad: a lost finger no longer locks the editor (#524):** The editor counts the fingers on the screen, and from the second finger on the view belongs to the camera and nothing is selected. A finger whose end never arrived, for example because the handle or number it touched vanished when the selection changed, stayed in that count, so every later touch counted as a second finger and nothing answered until the page was reloaded. A new first finger now starts the count afresh, and so does leaving the app and coming back.
- **Fillet and Chamfer on a group start faster the second time:** A group that holds filleted or chamfered parts is a mesh, and every start of the edge tool turned that mesh back into an exact body by sewing its triangles (about 7 of the 10 seconds measured for a pocket in a filleted body of 3,400 triangles; 21 seconds on the forum member's laptop). The worker now keeps that solid as its BREP, up to four of them, as long as the triangles are the same, so cancelling and starting the tool again, or Undo and Redo, skips the sewing: 3.5 instead of 10 seconds in the same design. The first start takes as long as before; the cure for that would be to keep the exact body with the group, which is a bigger change. Reported by @DrJones in the forum.
- **The two-finger zoom of a trackpad works in the sketch too (Safari on a Mac):** Safari sends a pinch as a gesture with a scale, not as wheel steps; the 3D window already turned it into a zoom, but the sketch did not refuse it, so the whole page was zoomed instead of the drawing plate. The sketch now zooms under the fingers, at the zoom speed of the settings (at the default one the plate follows the fingers one to one). Asked for by @Jenserer in the forum. Not tried in Safari by us: the gesture was replayed in the browser, the pinch itself cannot be.
- **Fillet and Chamfer in the sketch follow the order of the editor (#533):** The symbols were the same, but the sketch put the Fillet button before the Chamfer one while the editor puts the Chamfer before the Fillet, so a hand that switched between the two reached for the wrong one. The sketch has the editor's order now, in the toolbar and in the command search. Reported by @AkiraNorthstar.
- **Chamfillet in the Chamfer tool (#513, #531):** The chamfer window has a choice at the top, Chamfer or Chamfillet. A chamfillet puts a chamfer at the plate on the chosen edges that blends tangentially into a fillet toward the wall, so the first layers need no support and the edge still ends soft; with Bottom edges it takes the whole foot of a part in one click. It is built in the CAD kernel, so the STEP export keeps it exact. The edge tool of the AI connection takes the kind `chamfillet`, too. The kernel's smooth surface normals now also stay in use for every edge treatment, which makes fillets render smoother. Contributed by @corefile.
- **Arabic: the guide pictures from the Arabic interface (#534):** The 35 pictures of the Arabic guide are now screenshots of the Arabic interface, the same set as in the other languages. Contributed by @sevagjb.
- **Folders for your designs (#496):** With dozens of designs the start page had only one list. **New folder** now makes a folder for the designs of this browser, and folders can hold folders. A design goes into a folder by dragging it onto the folder or with **Move to …** in its menu, which lists every folder; a new design, and a file opened or imported, made while a folder is open lands in that folder. Inside a folder the first tile, `..`, and a path under the heading lead back, and take a dropped design or folder as well; a folder dragged onto another goes inside it. A folder's menu renames and deletes it, and deleting a folder deletes no design: what was in it moves one level up, and the dialog says where. The search looks through every folder and shows the folder of each design it finds, as a button that opens it. **Back up all** writes the designs into their folders in the ZIP (under `Folders/`, empty folders included), and restoring brings the folders back; designs in older backups land at the top level as before. The folders are kept in this browser beside the list of designs and follow in another open tab. The names follow the rules of the folders on the server. Asked for by @uowmag in #496.
- **Japanese:** The interface speaks Japanese, all of its sentences, and the guide pages have their Japanese words; the chapters are not translated yet and show in English. It is written for upper primary school children and their teachers, many of whom know Tinkercad: short sentences, plain words instead of hard compounds, and Tinkercad's Japanese terms where Tinkercad has one (デザイン for a design, シェイプ for a shape, エクスポート, ビュー). Words that are English in katakana stay as they are, such as ボディ, ミラー and フィレット, and a sentence names a button or a shape exactly as its label does. The Bricks units read ブロック and 1:1（ポッチ）, words children know.

## 1.88.0

- **The Arabic guide reads from the right (#530):** The pages of the guide in Arabic get the right-to-left direction, so text, lists, tables and the chapter list read from the right; key combinations such as Ctrl+S and code blocks keep their left-to-right order. The other languages look as before. Contributed by @sevagjb.
- **"Save as default" is kept for new designs, and the wheel keeps the value selected (#483, #226):** The button kept the values of a shape kind only in the design that was open, so a new design started with the colours of the app again (reported by @bdeboer58 for the green cube). It now also writes them into the default of this browser, the one a new design from the start page reads, and "Reset defaults" takes the kind back there as well; nothing else of that default changes. And after a turn of the mouse wheel in a clicked number field the whole value stays selected, so typing replaces it, as it does after the click.
- **Arabic: chapter 17, the Gridfinity section and bring back from history (#529):** The Arabic guide has the chapter on tablets and touch, the Gridfinity section with the label shelves and the sentence on the pinless hinge, and the eight interface sentences of bringing a shape back from the history are in Arabic. Contributed by @sevagjb.
- **Turkish: the sentences added since the first version (#528):** Fifteen interface sentences that came after the Turkish translation went in - kept colours, resizing the shape library, resetting the grid, bottom edges, hidden shapes on opening and the print button of the shortcut list - are now in Turkish. Contributed by @murartdursun25.
- **Interactive Help answers better inside the tool windows and in a few places that had none (#424):** @prmod3d checked the windows of the ribbon and found about a hundred places where the help led to the wrong part of the guide or to none. Most of them had one cause: the help asks the guide for the name of what was clicked, and "Cancel", "Create", "Depth" or "Cylinder" are written in dozens of sections. Now the buttons of a tool window that say nothing about the tool (Cancel, Apply, Clear ...), and its fields and sliders, ask about the window's title ("Chamfer edges"), and a button with a name of its own ("Bottom edges") keeps it. The question mark of a window leads to its section. The shape library (its tiles, its edges, Save selection), the object list and the Export window answer with their section as a whole, the view cube and the camera bar for the places between their buttons; the guide has a section each for the cube and the bar. Reported by @prmod3d; the Settings window and the property windows of the shapes are still to be checked.
- **Mirror in the sketch (#512):** Select points and lines in the sketch and click **Mirror** in the bar: **Left-right** mirrors across the vertical axis through the middle of the plate, **Top-bottom** across the horizontal one, and **Across a line** across a straight line of the sketch that you click next. The copy is added on the other side and the original stays; nothing is linked. Points on the axis are shared, so half an outline whose ends sit on the axis becomes one closed outline. Suggested by @structurednewstore-rgb and @PRMOD3D.
- **The mouse wheel changes a number in a field that has been clicked (#226):** Asked for by @prmod3d, and a way found with @WagnerBoyzRacing's help: the wheel works only in a field of the properties that has the keyboard focus (a click into it), so the panels still scroll under a pointer that merely passes over a field, which was the reason it had been turned down. One notch is a unit (a millimetre, a degree, an inch is a sixteenth; a tenth on a short range), ten times as much with Shift, a tenth with Ctrl and a hundredth with Alt; the object follows at every notch, and typing a number still wins. The sliders stay as they are.
- **A chain of lines in the sketch ends with a right click or a double click (#520):** With Line, Bézier curve or Smooth curve, a right click that does not move the pointer ends the chain, and so does a double click on the last point; the tool stays selected. A right button dragged to pan the view leaves the chain alone.
- **The ruler has a scale in inches (#500):** The printed scale of the ruler was always in millimetres, with the tens numbered. With the unit of the workspace on inches (the switch at the bottom right), the ruler now carries an inch scale: a line every sixteenth of an inch, longer at the eighths, quarters, halves and whole inches, which are numbered. The rulers are built again when the unit is switched. Asked for by @HWKPARISTX.
- **Erasing in the sketch: the button erases the selection, and the tool takes a window (#519):** With points and lines selected by a window of the Select tool, the **Erase** button did not erase them, it only dropped the selection and switched the tool, and the Erase tool then had no window, so they had to be clicked away one by one. Now the button erases what is selected (as the Delete key does), and with the Erase tool a window dragged over several takes away every point and line it holds, as the Select tool's window would pick them; a click on the empty plane still does nothing. One undo step each. Reported by @rondlh.
- **The shape library opens where it was left, also at a size of its own (#505):** A library that had been pulled narrower and moved to the right edge opened far to the left the next time, as if an invisible edge held it back. The remembered position was held inside the window with the usual, much wider size of the library, because the remembered size was only read after that; the size is read first now. Reported by @prmod3d.
- **The editor is not a page of text on an iPad (#524):** A long press on the working surface of an iPad could start the system's own text selection, when it met a word: in the menu that the long press opens, in the toolbar or in the foot of the page. The next touches then went to the selection (the "Copy / Look Up / Translate" bar and blue handles in the report's video) and not to the program, so the selected object seemed to stay in edit mode and nothing else answered. The editor, the 3D view and the sketch, is now not selectable (`user-select: none` and no touch callout), the text fields keep their selectable text. Reported by @andrej-cds; not tried on an iPad by us, as the cause is read from his video.
- **The Fillet and Chamfer window answers the pointer and shows its quick pick (#517):** The row of quick buttons (All sharp edges, Top edges, Bottom edges, Vertical edges, Clear) was one line too wide for the window, which gave it a horizontal scroll bar of a few pixels; the buttons wrap now and the window no longer scrolls sideways. The quick buttons and the footer buttons light up under the pointer like the Edit button, and the quick button whose edges are the ones picked now stays lit, so you can see which one made the current selection. Reported by @rondlh.
- **The section view shows the cut face (#516):** The view was only cut away and the cut left open, so a solid and a hole looked the same inside, and with a hole in a body you could not tell what was solid. Where the plane now goes through a solid, the cut is filled with the colour of the body, a little darker, and a hole inside it stays empty. It is made from the same bodies as the section SVG (holes taken out of the solids, overlapping parts of one colour joined) a moment after the plane or the design stops changing, and it is gone while they move. Reported by @rondlh.
- **A group with a fillet or chamfer can be ungrouped, and the tool stays open when a feature is taken back (#514):** A group that got an edge feature is one mesh with the history of its features, so Ungroup had no parts to work on, stayed grey and gave no reason. It is available now: it takes the edge features back first, as the first of them remembers the group, and then dissolves it, in one step that Ctrl+Z undoes, with a message that says so. The context menu and the MCP action `layerling_ungroup_objects` do the same. And taking one edge feature back in the Fillet or Chamfer tool closed the tool, so each further feature meant choosing the tool again; it now stays open on the restored body. Reported by @rondlh.
- **The outer diameter of a tube shows its unit (#522):** With inches set, the Outer diameter of a round tube or ring still stood in millimetres ("109.60") while the inner diameter and the wall showed inches. Outer diameter, the radius of curved text and the two offsets of an extruded outline's top now follow the unit like every other length. Reported by @WagnerBoyzRacing.
- **Video list:** "10 Things Layerling Can Do That Tinkercad Can't!" by Print N Repeat is added at the top, in the start page list and in the guide. The video "10 things I love about Layerling and 3 things that Tinkercad does better" by TECHNOBENTO gives way to that channel's playlist "Layerling", where new videos arrive without a release.
- **Japanese text, name tags in Japanese:** The seven built-in typefaces have no kana and no kanji, so a Japanese name on a text or a name tag came out as question marks. Every typeface now borrows them from Zen Maru Gothic, a rounded sans under the SIL Open Font License (the font file is in the repository), cut down to ASCII, the kana, symbols, Greek and Cyrillic of JIS X 0208 and all its kanji: the 2,965 of level 1 and the 3,390 of level 2, so rare kanji in names and places such as 邉, 澤, 槇 and 檜 work too. The face is a typeface of its own, **Japanese**, and is fetched only the first time a text needs it or it is chosen; a text waiting for it shows question marks for a moment. Fonts of your own now keep the common Japanese characters first when they have more than 8,000: by code point, a Japanese font lost the common kanji late in the block (郎, 高, 鈴), so level 1 comes first, then level 2.
- **Japanese letters are not part of the first download:** Few visitors write Japanese, so the typeface is two plain files that are neither a chunk of the program nor part of the offline copy: the common letters (kana, symbols, the level-1 kanji; 2.6 MB) are fetched the first time a text needs a Japanese character, and a second file with the level-2 kanji (3.3 MB) only when a text has one of those. Both are kept on the computer after the first use, so Japanese also works offline from then on, and survive updates unless the font itself changes. Outlines read from a font file leave out the lines that go nowhere, which opentype.js draws after many curves: a quarter of all segments in Zen Maru Gothic, and fonts of your own are smaller for it too.
- **Texts drawn again when their letters arrive:** A Japanese text or name tag opened after a reload, or given its first Japanese letter, kept showing question marks until it was touched, and a name tag's wider layers stood as plain letters while the 2D kernel was on its way: the plate and the ghost of a shape being placed are now built again when a font or the kernel arrives, and the kernel comes along with the editor.
- **Label shelves for Gridfinity bins (#455):** A Gridfinity bin had plain inner walls with no place for a label. A bin of 3 height units or more can now have a label shelf along any of its four walls, several at once: **Label shelf at the back**, **Label shelf at the front**, **Label shelf on the left** and **Label shelf on the right**. A shelf is flat on top, with a 1.2 mm strip down at the front and an underside rising at 36 degrees, and sits 0.5 mm under the feet of a bin stacked on the lip. Each shelf has its own **Shelf width** (14 mm), **Shelf length** (0 runs it from wall to wall) and, when it is shorter, **Shelf position** along its wall. Two shelves that meet in a corner join into one, and where a shelf meets a wall or another shelf, **Shelf corners** rounds or chamfers the corner by **Corner size** (2 mm; 0 leaves it sharp). The bin stays an exact body for the STEP export. Also through the MCP action `layerling_create_shape` with `gridfinityLabelBack` and the other `gridfinityLabel` settings. Suggested by @9ballrider. Contributed by @mlugo-apx.

## 1.87.0

- **Turkish interface (#511):** The interface is available in Türkçe, translated by @murartdursun25 with the help of Claude and read by him as a native speaker: 2547 of 2562 sentences; the others and the chapters of the guide show in English until they are translated.
- **The shape library no longer meets an invisible edge when it is moved (#509):** The library follows the toolbar size with a zoom of its own (0.75 for small, 1.25 for large), and the code that moves a panel took no notice of it. With the small toolbar the library stopped a good way before the right edge of the window and was cut short at the bottom long before the window's edge; with the large toolbar it could be pushed out of the window on the right and below. The position now counts in the panel's own zoom, so the library goes as far as the window's edge on every toolbar size, and when it is pulled down it only gets shorter, down to its title bar, so that it stays inside the window. The same count keeps a library that is pulled wider from its left edge standing on its right edge (#505).
- **The view can be panned to parts far beside the plate (#502):** The point the view pans and turns around was held to the plate, and only went further after "Center view on selection" had taken it to a part. A design with parts parked far to one side of the plate could not be panned over to them with the mouse. It now reaches every body of the design, out to the far side of the farthest one, as it already did upwards (#425). The plate stays the least range. Shown in a video by @gquiring, whose parts lay far to the left of the plate.
- **A letter edge for the Text shape (#499):** The name tag could round or chamfer the top edge of its letters, up to 5 mm, but a plain text had only the **Bevel**, which rounds about 1.8 mm at its largest. The Text properties now hold the same setting, **Letter edge**: None, Round or Chamfer, with a size (0.4 mm to start with, at most as high as the text and 5 mm). It builds the same smooth skin as on a name tag, works for every font, fill and curved text, and is what the export writes. A rounding goes as far as the strokes are wide: where a stroke is thinner than twice the size, the edge ends in a narrower top. The underside stays flat for letters that are glued on afterwards. While an edge is chosen it takes the place of Bevel and Segments. The `letterEdge` of the MCP set it already. Asked for by @KenoGuy.
- **Keep colours for good (#507):** The colours you mix yourself were saved on their own under "Recently used", but only eight, and the oldest dropped out when a new one came. A pin button next to the new row **Kept** keeps the colour you have chosen: it stands in a row of its own, newer colours never push it out, and the same button lets go of it again. Up to 21 colours are kept, in rows of seven, in this browser like the recent ones; palette colours do not need it, and a colour that is kept is not shown twice. It works for one body and for several. Suggested by @prmod3d.
- **The ruler's handles pull it as far as its slider (#500):** The handles of a body stop at the size of the plate (at least 220 mm), but the Length slider of the ruler goes up to 500 mm, so a ruler could be dragged no further than 220 mm and typed or slid to more. A ruler only measures and is never printed, so its handles now go as far as the slider: 500 mm, or the plate if that is larger.
- **The shape library can be pulled to any size (#505):** The library that the Shapes button opens had one width (three to five columns, by toolbar size) and a height of at most 650 px. Its edges and corners now pull the size, as wide and high as the screen allows, which suits very large and very small monitors; a left edge moves the panel so that its right edge stays. The size is kept in the browser, a double-click on an edge sets it back, and the edges also answer the arrow keys. Pulled narrower than 300 px the library leaves out the names and shows only the pictures, as Tinkercad does; every tile carries its name as a tooltip. The sketch's shape menu keeps its size. The pin in the title bar is larger now (36 px instead of 28, the symbol 20 instead of 16), as @prmod3d found it hard to hit on a high-resolution screen. Asked for by @ARSoderberg and seconded by @prmod3d.
- **Reset grid (#503):** Someone who had set the grid colour to that of the plate, and the darker lines too, saw no grid at all, and a new design looked the same: the settings saved with "Make default" are kept for the whole browser, and removing the browser does not clear them. The Workplane page of the settings now ends with **Reset grid**. It puts the grid's visibility, colours, spacing and line weight back to what the app starts with, for the design you are in and in the default saved with "Make default", so new designs start with a visible grid again. The size of the plate and the printer are not touched; an inch grid goes back to 1/4 in. Asked in a question from @Fixer322.
- **A tapered or leaning cylinder keeps its turn when it is grouped (#504):** A round cylinder that was tapered or leaning and then turned by 90 degrees came out of Group, Export and the Boolean tools as if it had never been turned, so the group had other overall measures than the parts had on the plate. The turn of a round body was dropped because a plain cylinder looks the same turned about its own axis, but a taper, a lean or a twist follows the body's own axes. The turn is kept for such a body now, in the mesh and in the STEP yaw.
- **`sitemap.xml` is no longer kept in Git (#498):** The file is written by the build on every `npm run dev` and `npm run build`, but it was also committed, so a tree that had been built could refuse `git pull` with "your local changes to apps/web/public/sitemap.xml would be overwritten". It is in `.gitignore` now and made by the build, like the other generated guide files; the deployment and the static export build it themselves. If you still get the error once after updating, run `git restore apps/web/public/sitemap.xml` and pull again. Reported by @foofoodog; @freddysae0 explained the cause.
- **Names of controls under a hovering Apple Pencil (#495):** An Apple Pencil can hover above an iPad screen, and Safari reports it as a pen that moves without pressing, but it shows no tooltip for it, so the names of the buttons stayed hidden. A pen that rests on a control for a moment now shows its name in a small bubble, the text a mouse tooltip shows; pressing, moving away, scrolling or a key hides it again. It is only active on an iPad or iPhone, where the browser shows nothing itself; on other devices the browser's own tooltip works for a pen as before. Asked by @DarkKnightForensics.
- **Print the list of keyboard shortcuts (#490):** The window with the shortcuts, in the editor and in sketch mode, has a printer button next to the question mark. It opens the print dialog of the browser with only the list on the page, black on white and in large type, which also makes it a clear PDF through "Save as PDF"; Ctrl+P while the window is open does the same. It prints the list for the mode you are in. Asked by @WimSnor, who had been taking screenshots of the list and found the colours made them blurry.
- **The whole user guide in Polish (#501):** All 17 chapters are translated, with every button named as the Polish interface names it. The Polish tape measure is called "Zmierz", as in Tinkercad, and the sentences for bringing shapes back from the history are in. The language stays marked as not complete until a second Polish reader has looked at it. Besides that, the letter "ł" is now treated as "l" in the addresses of guide headings, in the command search (Ctrl+K) and in the search of the guide, so "polóz" finds "Połóż" and a heading like "Własne czcionki" gets the address `wlasne-czcionki`. And two places in the guide that showed a raw `{count}` in every language (the darker grid line "every 10 grid steps" in chapter 3 and "Show earlier versions" in chapter 1) read right now. Contributed by @cyryllo, who also found the three problems.
- **`layerling_save_project`: the MCP saves the editable design as a .lyl (#494):** An AI could write STL, 3MF and OBJ to a path through the MCP, but the editable project only through the browser's save dialog. The new tool takes a `path` (a missing .lyl ending is added), `overwrite` (an existing file is only replaced when it is true) and `history` (steps of undo history, 0 for none, or `all`), packs the project as the export window does and writes it, through a temporary file, at that path on the computer where the MCP server runs. The answer gives the full path, the size and how many objects and history steps the file holds. The target of Ctrl+S in the editor is not touched. Suggested by @FrankMozingo on GitHub.
- **The Crescent no longer comes out hollow where it overlaps another body (#493):** The two lids of the Crescent were turned the wrong way against its walls, so the mesh had edges that did not meet. A slicer then treated the part where the Crescent overlaps another solid as a hole, and the merge in the export (several bodies into one) failed for it, so the Crescent was written as a separate overlapping shell. Lids and walls now face outwards together, the mesh is closed, and a Crescent merges with other bodies like any other shape. A test now checks that every edge is walked once in each direction. Reported by @uowmag, who saw the overlap of a Crescent and two cylinders hollow in OrcaSlicer.
- **Hidden shapes show a number, and a design says so when it is opened (#489):** The count of hidden shapes stood only inside the Visibility menu ("Show all hidden (n)"), so someone who hid a shape, left the design and came back had nothing to tell them. A small number now sits on the button of the Visibility group while something is hidden, and a click on it shows everything again. A design that is opened with hidden shapes says so once, with a button "Show all"; a message may carry one button now. The shapes are not unhidden on opening: hiding is saved with the design on purpose. A design opened again in the same session without reloading gets the number only. Reported by @rondlh, who knows Tinkercad's light for hidden objects.
- **The hide button stays when several bodies are selected (#491):** The eye in the title bar of the properties window was only there for a single body. For two or more it is back beside the lock: it hides all the selected bodies, and shows them again when all of them are hidden, as Ctrl+H does. Reported by @rondlh on GitHub.
- **The height value also stands at the height handle (#488):** While the top middle handle was hovered or pulled, the height stood only at the vertical edge that is furthest right on the screen (a wish from the forum, so that the number stays off the body), often far from the handle, and it left the view when you zoomed in on the middle of the body, with no way to type a value. A second number now stands right at the handle, above it (below, for the lower handle), and can be clicked and typed like the one at the edge. The measure at the edge is unchanged, and with Dimensions always visible only that one shows. Reported by @rondlh on GitHub.
- **Bottom edges in the Fillet and Chamfer tools (#473):** The tools had a button **Top edges** that picks only the edges along the very top of a body, but nothing for the bottom, so a fillet or chamfer on the foot alone meant clicking the lower edges one by one. **Bottom edges** is its mirror: it picks only the edges along the very bottom. The MCP action for edge treatments takes `"bottom"` for `edgeIds` next to `"all"`, `"top"` and `"vertical"`, and the guide and the MCP skill say so. Asked by @gquiring, who had to cut a part in two to round only one end; the Rounded Box's own **Top/bottom fillet** stays one value for both.
- **A shape on the pointer shows no measures at the old place (#463):** A custom shape - and parts picked up with C - hanging on the pointer showed their measures and handles at the middle of the plate, where the parts still stand in the design, until the click set them down. They stay out while the parts are carried now. Reported by @t9vh6j9rt4-collab on GitHub.
- **Arrow keys: the view follows only a body that would leave the picture (#486):** The arrow keys move a body along what the screen shows, and the view takes the same step when a body would be pushed past the edge, so that it stays in sight. That rule also fired for a body that already lay at or past the edge, for example after zooming in far: the body stood still on the screen and everything else, the plate and the other bodies, slid past, which felt reversed. Now the view follows only a body that is in the picture and would leave it; one that already lies outside simply moves. Reported by @rondlh.
- **The tablet chapter says when a tap on empty space does not clear the selection (#487):** The table said only "Tap empty space". With Touch rotate on, one finger turns the view instead, and in Select multiple the first tap on empty space only ends the mode and leaves the selection as it is. Both are now in the row.
- **A typed measure is kept when you click beside the box (#486):** In the box beside a body, and in the angle box, a value you had typed was thrown away by a left click on the empty plate and applied by a right click, the wrong way round. The click on the plate takes the selection away, and with it the box, before the box could lose its focus and apply the value; a right click selects nothing, so there it did. Now any press outside the box applies the value first, with the left button as with the right. Esc still cancels. Reported by @rondlh.
- **Alignment handles keep apart and stay when you zoom in (#486):** The nine handles of Align stand where the parts line up, so on small parts, or seen from afar, the three of an axis ran into one blob that could not be picked, and zooming in far enough took all nine away at once, because one corner of the selection lay too close to the camera. The three handles of an axis now keep at least 28 pixels from each other on the screen, and each handle is judged by its own place, so those still in front of the camera stay. Reported by @rondlh.
- **"Save as default" keeps the colour, too (#483):** Suggested by @bdeboer58, who would like to see colours for the shapes of the library. The pictures in the library stay as they are, but the colour is now part of what the button **Save as default** in the Properties panel keeps for a kind of shape: colour a cylinder green, save, and every new cylinder starts green; **Reset defaults** gives the app's own colour back, and the other kinds keep theirs. **A flaw found on the way:** inside one design, a second **Save as default** and **Reset defaults** did not reach the next new shape, because the editor took the change for an echo of its own settings and kept the old ones until the page was reloaded. The default of a size was affected as well as the colour. The settings window used the same route and passes it the same way now.
- **A copy can be moved with the mouse and copied again (#484):** Duplicate, move the copy, duplicate again repeats the step (1.10.0). It only held when the copy was moved with the keys: with the mouse, the press landed on the copy and on the original it lies on at the same distance, the ray took whichever came first in the list - the original - and selected and moved that one, so the chain of copies was gone and the next copy stayed at the old place. Among bodies that lie at the same spot, a press now means the one already selected. Reported by @willi1955 on GitHub.
## 1.86.2

- **The shape library moves, and can be kept open (#433, #447):** The library that the Shapes button opens hung under its button and covered the spot where a shape is going to be put. Its title bar now moves it - over the Properties panel, say - and where it stands is kept in the browser; a double-click on the title bar puts it back under its button. A pin in the title bar keeps it open while several shapes are put one after the other; the click that places a shape and Esc then leave it open. Without the pin it closes after each shape, as before. The sketch's shapes use the same panel. The order of the ribbon is untouched. Suggested by @prmod3d, with the idea of splitting the group by @RobbieKnobbie noted next to it.
- **Interactive Help answers for buttons that are switched off (#424):** A button that is greyed out, because nothing is selected, got no answer at all from Interactive Help: the browser sends no mouse event for a disabled button, not even to the page, so the click was lost and the window stayed as it was. Reported by @prmod3d for "Center view on selection"; the same held for every greyed-out button (Copy, Delete, Group, Fillet and the rest, 30 in an empty design). While the help is on, a disabled button now lets the pointer pass, and the help finds it by its place, as it was meant to. Checked by clicking all 30 in an empty design: each one answers with its own name.
- **A number at the mouse pointer counts the selection (#481):** Suggested by @prmod3d. While Shift is held and two or more bodies are selected, a small number follows the mouse pointer, just below it, so the tally of what has been selected stays where the eye is while clicking one body after the other. It is hidden over the panels, and shows for a mouse only: a finger would cover it.
- **Back up all stands once on the start page:** The button was there twice, in the notice about designs kept in the browser and again in the row above the design cards, with the same words one below the other. The one in the row is gone; the notice keeps Back up all and Restore backup.
## 1.86.1

- **A number on the edge of a body lying on its side changes the right measure (#480):** With a workplane on a face, 1.81.0 matched the numbers on the horizontal edges to the body's own sides for a body turned by a quarter, but not the number on the vertical edge. A Round Roof turned by 90° about X and given a new height by that number changed its depth or width instead, because the two swap places when the body lies down. Every mark - width, depth and height - is now matched to the side it stands at. Reported by @SteveGork on GitHub.
- **The tablet hint now also shows on an iPad, and in the editor (#467):** The card also floats up in the editor now, after a quarter of a minute, once (closing it on either page closes it for good): whoever opens layerling on a tablet often lands straight in a design, for the installed app opens the last one, and never saw the card on the start page. **Detection:** The card that points to the guide chapter for tablets looked at the media queries for a touch screen without a mouse. Safari on an iPad has answered those differently from one iPadOS version to the next, with a trackpad or a Pencil attached, so on an iPad the card stayed away. An iPad and an iPhone are now recognised by what they call themselves (a Mac with several touch points, or an iPhone/iPad), as the file picker already did; an Android tablet is still recognised by the queries, and a computer with a touch screen and a mouse still sees no card.

## 1.86.0

- **A clear recommendation to install layerling as an app:** The small strip on the start page is now a card that is meant to be seen: as an app layerling starts faster, works without internet, has its own icon and window and does not have to be loaded again each time, and nothing is lost, as layerling still tells you when a new version is out. Where the browser has an install button it is on the card; "Not now" hides the card for two weeks, the cross for good. In the editor the same card appears at the foot of the work area after half a minute of work, so it never gets in the way of the first clicks, and it steps above the support reminder when both are there. Its icon is the same size as the one on the backup notice. It still shows only where installing works (https), never inside the installed app, and not in an ordinary browser tab either once the app has been installed or opened on this computer. The guide's chapter on installing says why it is worth it.
- **The view follows bodies set high above the plate (#425):** With the height no longer held to 220 mm, a body typed in high above the plate could not be centred: Zoom to the selection (F) let it slip off the top edge, because the camera's pivot was held to 70 mm below and 120 mm above the plate. The pivot now reaches to the top of the highest body and the foot of the lowest. Found from a report that bodies at that height could hardly be picked or resized; picking itself worked in my tests up to 900 mm, so that report is not settled.
- **An open sketch line points to Stroke (#478):** Extrude Sketch needs a closed outline and said only to close a loop, although an open line can be built as a stripe with a width: tick "Build as a stroke" in the Finish section. The message now says so, in every language. Asked by @Cowski4444, who wanted Tinkercad's three-sided box. **French:** the stroke was called "Accident vasculaire cérébral" and "Construire comme un coup" (a stroke in the medical sense); it is "Trait" and "Construire comme un trait" now, and "Poste" and "Se termine" in its panel are "Position" and "Extrémités".
- **A twisted star stays straight (#476):** A star twisted about its height turned about the middle of its box, not about its own middle. A five-pointed star is not centred in its box (it reaches further up than down), so its middle circled round the axis and the tips came out at different distances: with a twist of 360 degrees they reached 30 mm from the axis at the bottom and 35 mm in the middle. The star now turns about its own middle in the editor, in the exact body and in the exports; taper works about the same point. Other shapes keep turning about the middle of their box. Reported by @RobbieKnobbie on GitHub.
- **The "What is new" card says it is an excerpt, and links the release notes:** The cards show a selection of what came with a version. A note at the top of the card, and of the list shown before an update, now says that a release can contain more, smaller changes and bug fixes, and links the release notes on GitHub, where everything is listed.
- **F zooms to the selection (#465):** Suggested by @WagnerBoyzRacing, who came from Tinkercad and found F taking him to the middle of the grid, with the selection one Shift away. F now looks at what is selected and, with nothing selected, still brings the whole scene into view; Shift+F works as before. The whole scene is always shown by [Home] and the house button, whose tooltip now names that key. The sketch behaves the same. This changes what F does while something is selected.
- **Interactive Help finds the right place for the buttons that missed it (#424):** Reported by @prmod3d, who went through the program button by button. Some places found nothing (visibility options, the command search, the design name, the footer links, the snap grid list, the mm and in switch) and some led to the wrong section (Paste, Delete, Ungroup, Workspace settings, Theme, Rename design, Hide selected and more), because the guide did not write these names or another section wrote them more often. Chapter 1 has a new section, **The buttons around the work area**, in every language, with one short entry for each and one for the footer; the snap grid list, the mm | in switch and the version link now ask the guide for the right name. More places are to follow as they are found.
- **A line round a traced sketch no longer times out (#461):** A sketch from an SVG with hundreds of small curves stopped with "OpenCascade timed out while building the sketch" once a line was set round it. The kernel could not move the outline, so the line was worked out as straight pieces, 32 for every curve - 29 000 edges for a bulldog drawing - and the kernel's validity check alone took 51 s of a 56 s build. Curves are now cut only as finely as 0.01 mm needs (a short curve gets 4 pieces, a long one at most 32), and a sketch of more than 1 500 edges is checked by its volume, not by the validity check. The same drawing builds in 1-5 s.
- **A design too big to save says why, and is saved anyway (#475):** A design with very large meshes and a long history failed to save after almost every change, with the bare text "Could not save design shapes", and every change felt slow. Now the message names the reason when it is one you can act on: the design is too big for the project file (delete the history or simplify the heaviest bodies), or the browser has no room left. And when only the whole history is too much, the stored copy in the browser keeps the newest steps that fit, so the design is saved at all; the message says how many steps it kept, once per design. Undo in the open session still has every step. Reported by @grmonsey on GitHub.
- **French: five more shape names (#406):** Suggested by @medmed58, who works with these words: the screw-head cutters are holes, so "Trou lamé", "Trou fraisé" and "Trou doublement fraisé" instead of "Lamage", "Fraisage" and "Double fraisage"; the bent tube is "Tube cintré" (the craft word for a bent pipe, not "courbé"); the round roof is "Demi-cylindre". The guide and the start page follow, and the old words still find the shapes in the command search.
- **A custom shape hangs on the pointer (#463):** A click on a tile of the custom shapes put the shape into the design at once, at the middle of the workplane; only dragging it out of the list chose the place. Now it hangs on the pointer like the shapes of the library: it follows it, also over the faces of other bodies, a click sets it down and Esc puts it away. With "click to place" switched off in the settings it is still put at the middle at once. Reported by @t9vh6j9rt4-collab on GitHub.
- **A chapter of the guide for tablets, and a hint on the start page (#467):** What a tablet needs was spread over the guide: the long press menu, Select multiple, turning with one finger, the Touch rotate switch, the stylus. The new chapter **Tablet and touch** (chapter 17) puts it in one place, with what to know on an iPad: install it as an app from the Home Screen, that Safari deletes what a website stored after seven days without a visit unless it is a Home Screen app (as WebKit describes it), downloads instead of saving into a folder, and **Back up all**. It is there in English, German, Dutch, French, Russian, Czech and Slovenian; the others show the English text. On a device whose main input is touch, a strip on the start page points to it once: it has a button to the chapter, a cross, and is gone for good after either. A computer with a mouse never shows it. Suggested after a question from an iPad user.
- **The Face tool starts from its defaults (#471):** It opened with the values of its last use, even when nothing was created, so an inset that fitted one face came along to the next, where it could be too wide and leave little of the face, or nothing. Each time it opens it now starts from an inset of 0, no corner radius, a depth of 2 mm and **Out: add material**; choosing another face while it is open keeps the values. Contributed by @mlugo-apx.
- **Delete face (#451):** A fillet, a hole or a boss that came with a part imported as STEP could not be taken off in layerling at all, and one in a group an edge treatment had turned into one body only by taking back that treatment and every one after it. A new tool in the Modify group, **Delete face**, takes them off: click the faces of what should go (a picked face lights up, another click takes it out again), then **Delete** or the Delete key, and the faces around them grow until the gap is closed. The body stays exact, so Fillet, Chamfer and the STEP export work on it as before; the deletion is one undo step and is listed with the fillets and chamfers under **Undo an edge feature**, where it can be taken back. Where the faces around them cannot close the gap, such as a main side of a box, nothing changes and the panel says why. Deleting only the floor of a pocket makes it a hole straight through. On a detailed imported part a few faces can take the CAD kernel too long; after 45 seconds the deletion is stopped and nothing changes. It needs exact faces: whatever layerling keeps as a mesh (an imported STL, OBJ or 3MF, a split, wrapped or simplified body, a very detailed shape such as a long text) gets a message saying so. The ribbon has one more button, so its three width steps moved to 2081, 1765 and 1615 px. Also through the MCP actions `layerling_list_faces` and `layerling_delete_faces`. Contributed by @mlugo-apx.

## 1.85.0

- **A width for an SVG or a picture in the import window (#448):** An SVG came in at its own size and had to be resized afterwards, where Tinkercad asks for the size before the drawing lands on the plate. The import window has a field **SVG and picture: width in mm** now: type the width, choose the file, and the drawing is scaled to it, its sketch with it, so the body is built clean at that size. Empty keeps the own size (a picture 50 mm wide as before). The width counts for the next import only and is empty again after it. Through the AI bridge `layerling_import_file` takes `svgWidth`. Asked for by @shanni1783 on GitHub.
