- **Bring objects back from the history:** In the History view, click an object (Shift for several) and press Bring into design: each comes back as a copy, as it was then and where it was then, named after the time of that state, such as Box (12:32). It is one step that Undo takes back; the history before stays as it was. For an AI there is `layerling_restore_from_history`, which also takes a part from inside a group. By @gogades.
- **French: dovetail, knurl, teardrop, name tag and icon tag renamed (#406):** Read by @jeromefc50, who finds "Queue d'aronde" a word of carpenters: the dovetail is "Prisme trapézoïdal", the knurl "Cylindre moleté", the teardrop hole "Goutte d'eau" (it is not always a hole), the name tag "Badge nominatif" and the icon tag "Badge d'icône". The old words still find the shapes in the command search. The guide has the new headings; its text still says "balise" and "étiquette" in places.
- **Select multiple for a tablet (#337):** A tablet has no Shift key, so only one body or all of them could be selected. The menu of a long press, on a shape or on empty space, has **Select multiple** now: each tap on a body adds it to the selection or takes it out again, a message says the mode is on, and a tap on empty space or [[Esc]] ends it and keeps the selection. A finger on empty space still turns the view. Suggested by @cyryllo on GitHub.
- **Tab moves through the dimension numbers on the shape (#300):** The fix of 1.68.1 only covered the properties panel. In the number box that opens when you click a dimension on the shape, Tab left the box and landed on the corner handles. Now Tab keeps the number and opens the next one (width, depth, height), and Shift+Tab goes back. Reported by @Cowski4444 on GitHub.
- **A Spanish video about layerling in the list:** "Layerling la nueva herramienta gratuita de modelado 3D, más completo que Tinkercad" by Fulcro 1900 is in the list of videos on the start page and in the guide.
- **Separate Parts in the right-click menu:** A shape made of several loose pieces, such as an STL of separate parts or a text of single letters, could only be taken apart with the button in the properties. The entry is now in the menu of a right click on the shape as well. Asked for by a YouTuber who makes videos about layerling.

## 1.84.0

- **A hinge without the printed pin (#445):** A switch in the hinge settings, **Without pin (use your own)**, leaves the printed pin out and runs the bore (the pin diameter plus the clearance) through every knuckle, the ends included, for a pin of your own such as a piece of filament, which is smoother than a printed pin on a small hinge. Before, you had to make a hinge, make a cylinder of the hole size, line it up and punch the hole by hand. The exact body for the fillet tool and the STEP export follows it, and the AI bridge takes `hingePinless`. Suggested by @vinmangraphics on GitHub.
- **A Dutch video about layerling in the list:** "Layerling - Tussen Tinkercad en Fusion 360 in, en Helemaal Gratis!" by Werkplaats Marc, a first look for Tinkercad users, is in the list of videos on the start page and in the guide.
- **An imported SVG whose outlines touch can be cut with (#420):** A drawing made of panels that share edges - a football drawn as an outline with its panels inside - came in as a body that looked right but was not a clean solid: its top and bottom faces overlapped, so grouping it as a hole with another body did nothing, after about ten seconds of waiting, and some such drawings lost whole shapes on import. The body is now made by the exact kernel, where outlines that touch or lie over each other come out as one clean solid. Designs already in your list keep their old body; import the SVG again. Reported by @stainlessdmc12 on GitHub.
- **Esc cancels a drag that is still going on (#440):** If you moved a body, pulled a handle to resize, rotate or lift it, or drew a selection frame and then pressed Esc while the mouse button was still held, Esc ended the selection but the new position was kept, and you had to undo it. Now Esc takes the drag back at once: everything stands as before, nothing is added to the history, and the selection stays. Suggested by @ucito on GitHub.
- **Switching to inches and back keeps the grid size (#442):** Changing the unit from mm to in and back set the plate's grid to the usual 5 mm, also when it was 1 mm before. The grid of the unit system you leave is now remembered, so switching back brings it again. Reported by @t9vh6j9rt4-collab on GitHub.
- **A group of two colours grouped with a hole keeps both colours (#418):** A group made of a red and a white part, grouped afterwards with a hole object, came out in one colour, and Multicolor only switched between the two: the outer group knew the inner group as one body of one colour. A group that shows its parts in their own colours now opens into its parts when it is grouped with something else, so the outer group carries the red and the white part itself and Multicolor shows both. A group of one colour, a bundle and a hole are left as they were. Reported by @djelliott19 on GitHub.
- **A pinch on the trackpad zooms in Safari on a Mac (forum):** Safari sends no wheel steps for a pinch with two fingers on a trackpad, only gestures with a scale, and layerling threw those away so that the page itself would not zoom; the pinch did nothing. Each change of the scale is now passed on as the wheel step other browsers send for a pinch, with the zoom speed of the settings, so spreading the fingers comes closer and pinching them together goes away, towards the pointer as the wheel does. Reported by @Jenserer in the forum.
- **The language menu is sorted:** The languages stood in the order they were added, with every new one at the end. Now English comes first, then the languages in the Latin alphabet by the name each gives itself (Čeština, Dansk, Deutsch, Français, Italiano, Magyar, Nederlands, Polski, Slovenščina), then Русский and العربية. TRANSLATING.md says where a new language goes in.
- **An empty design on the server is asked about too:** Going back to the start page from an empty design asked whether to keep it only for a design saved in the browser; one in a shared folder on a server stayed in the folder without a question. Both are asked now, with the same question. **No** on a server design deletes the file from the server, for everyone who uses it, together with its working copy in the browser, as **Delete** in the folder does. The removal waits for the upload that leaving the editor starts, so it does not fail on a file that has just changed.
- **Gridfinity bins and baseplates (#381):** Gridfinity is the open storage system of bins on a 42 mm grid that stand in a baseplate and stack on one another, and a bin had to be drawn from its specification step by step or come from another program. A new shape, **Gridfinity**, in the shape library after the spring, makes either: under **Type**, a **Bin** with a foot under every grid unit, its units across and deep, its height in units of 7 mm (up to 20), the stacking lip on or off and holes for 6 × 2 mm magnets under the feet if you like, or a **Baseplate** with a socket for every unit and no magnet holes. The measures follow the published specification (the Gridfinity design reference linked from gridfinity.xyz), so the parts fit bins and baseplates made with other generators; the size is always whole units, up to 6 × 6, and a drag on a handle snaps to them. The wall (0.95 mm), the floor (1.2 mm) and the depth of the magnet holes (2.4 mm) are not in the specification and are fixed; the top of the lip is sharp, as the specification draws it, and the edge tool rounds or chamfers it. Both are exact bodies, so Fillet, Chamfer and the STEP export work on them. Also through the MCP action `layerling_create_shape` with `kind: "gridfinity"`. Contributed by @mlugo-apx.

## 1.83.0

- **A round tube or ring has an outer diameter, and outer, inner and wall follow one another (forum):** The tube had a width, a length and an inner diameter but no outer diameter, and typing the inner diameter first clamped it to the width minus 1, which left the wall at its thinnest, so setting the width afterwards ended 0.5 mm off. A round tube or ring now shows **Outer diameter**, **Inner diameter** and **Wall thickness**, and any two give the third whichever you type first: the outer diameter keeps the bore, the inner diameter keeps the outer one, and a bore wider than the tube makes the outer diameter grow by the wall. The two sides Length and Width, and the old rule that the wall stays, only remain for a tube that is oval. The inner and outer diameter can be typed beyond their sliders. Reported by @Jenserer in the forum.
- **Arabic (العربية):** The whole interface and the 16 chapters of the guide are in Arabic, with the language list and the automatic choice for a browser set to Arabic. It is not marked complete: the layout is not mirrored for right-to-left yet, the headings of the guide carry their English name in brackets so that the links to them keep working, and a second Arabic speaker has not read it yet. Made by @sevagjb (#435); wrong words are fixed with a small pull request.
- **Italian (Italiano):** The interface is in Italian (97 % of the sentences), with the language list and the automatic choice for a browser set to Italian. It is not marked complete: the 80 sentences added since 1.77.0 show in English until their Italian follows, and the guide chapters are still English. Made by @maxciava (#395); wrong words are fixed with a small pull request.
- **A text made smaller takes its box with it (#432):** The letters of a straight text fit their box in one scale, so a box made much lower than the line needs stayed as wide as before, and the measures and handles stood far from the small letters, out of reach when zoomed in. When a change of the box makes the letters smaller, the box now becomes the one the letters need at that size; a box made larger, a stretched text and a curved text are left as they were. Reported by @MartinSpanjaard on GitHub.
- **Copying a large imported model no longer freezes the page (#431):** A copy was also written through a hidden text field and the browser's copy command, and selecting a text of tens of megabytes in a field takes seconds, and more the larger it is: a mesh of 45,000 triangles held the page for 4 s, one of 245,000 for 23 s, one of 720,000 for minutes, so the browser had to be closed. The copy is now written through the clipboard API alone when it is larger than a megabyte (the hidden field stays for small copies and for pages without the API); the same copies now take 0.2 s, 0.9 s and 2.5 s, and pasting them is as fast as before. Reported by @Cranborlie on GitHub.
- **More French names corrected (#406):** The thread was called "Sujet" (a subject) and the nut "Noix" (a walnut); they are "Filetage" and "Écrou" now, on the shape tile, in the properties and in the guide, which also called a threaded rod "tige congéée". The label "Type" of the thread properties read "Tapez" (the command to type), the wedge is "Prisme triangulaire" instead of "Coin", the torus "Anneau", "Moleter" is "Moletage", "Ellipses" is "Ellipse" and "Coeur" is "Cœur". Suggested by @jeromefc50.
- **Interactive Help: the pointer says where there is no help (#424):** Over a place the help has nothing about, such as the empty parts of the ribbon or the foot of the page, the pointer in the help mode is the "not allowed" sign instead of the question mark, so no click is wasted there; a click still opens the notice. Suggested by @prmod3d.
- **The selection frame can take only what it encloses (#430):** A frame dragged on the empty workplane selects every body it touches, whichever way it is dragged, which makes it hard to pick one part out of a cluster. A new switch in the settings (area Appearance), **The direction of the frame decides**, lets the direction decide as in SketchUp and CAD programs: a frame dragged from left to right takes only the bodies that lie completely inside it, one dragged from right to left everything it touches, and its line is dashed. It is off by default, so the frame works as before. Suggested by @foofoodog on GitHub.

## 1.82.0

- **Interactive Help: click anything to read about it (#424):** As the program and the guide grow, it got hard to find where a button or a field is explained. A new question-mark button in the **Help** group of the ribbon (and **Shift+F1**, and the command search) switches the mode on and off. While it is on the pointer is a question mark and whatever has a name under it is outlined; a click on a button, field, switch, handle, ribbon group or window title does not do its usual thing but opens a small window you can move: the name of the element, a short summary of the part of the guide that fits it best, up to two more places as links, and **Open in the guide**, which opens that section. The help is for the element you pointed at, not for the window around it; an element without a name takes the nearest one around it or the question mark of its window, and where the guide has nothing the window says so and offers the guide's search. Controls that are switched off can be asked about too. **Esc** leaves the mode; on a touch screen a tap opens the window directly. There is no list of labels to keep: the guide writes buttons as `{{ui:key}}`, so the name on the element is the name in the guide, and it is looked up in the guide's own search index, in the language of the program, in the browser and offline. The matching of the guide's search box moved into one module (`guideSearchCore.ts`) that the guide pages and the program share, and a section the program sends you to is now outlined in colour in the guide, from its heading to its end. The ribbon has one more button, so its three width steps moved to 2038, 1728 and 1582 px. Asked for by a user in #424.
- **Vertical edges in the edge tool (#427):** Next to **All sharp edges** and **Top edges** there is **Vertical edges** now: it picks every edge that stands straight up, such as the corners of a box, so one fillet or chamfer rounds all the vertical corners at once. Through the AI bridge `layerling_apply_edge_treatment` takes `edgeIds: "vertical"`. Asked for on GitHub.
- **Loose pieces of no volume are left out of an export as well (#352):** The export already left out tiny loose grains. A cut and the colour separation also leave flat sheets of two faces on each other behind, up to ten millimetres long, which hold nothing and which a slicer shows as thin strips. A loose piece that holds less than a hundredth of a cubic millimetre now goes, whatever its size and number of triangles. Reported by @Mycraftymama on GitHub.
- **Touch: handles hold the drag, a long press opens the menu (#337):** A finger on a resize or rotate handle was taken over by the browser for a scroll after a few pixels, so the drag ended at once: the handles say `touch-action: none` now. For a finger or a rubber stylus the handles catch from 15 px further out, without changing their look. A finger held still for half a second on a shape or on empty space opens the menu a right click opens, with **Select all** on empty space, since a tablet has neither a right button nor Ctrl+A. The guide says that a plain rubber-tip stylus counts as a finger. Reported by @cyryllo on GitHub.
- **A switch between millimetres and inches beside the snap grid (#422):** The unit was only in the settings, and changing it there each time took a detour. The small switch **mm | in** beside the snap grid at the bottom of the properties window does what the unit field of the settings does: the unit, the plate's grid and the snap step follow, for this design. Suggested by @Mycraftymama on GitHub.
- **Messages stay as long as they take to read, and can be closed (#422):** A message of the status line went after four seconds whatever its length, and an error text of two lines was gone before it could be read. Its time now follows its length (up to half a minute), it stays while the pointer rests on it, and a small cross closes it at once. Suggested by @Mycraftymama on GitHub.
- **The browser no longer translates layerling (forum):** In the installed app in Chrome, a German browser translated the page: "Hell" in the theme menu came out as "Hölle" ("Hell" is also an English word), and an English menu showed German words. The page said it was English until the chosen language was in, so the translator started on it. The page now says it is not to be translated (`translate="no"` and the `notranslate` hint), as layerling has its own languages. Reported by @Noberty in the forum.
- **The question mark of the notice about designs in the browser sits in its heading:** On the start page it stood at the end of the text, where its box made the last line stand further from the others than the rest. It is behind the heading now, smaller, and the three lines of the text are even.

## 1.81.0

- **`layerling_export_mesh`: STL, 3MF and OBJ over the AI connection:** The MCP bridge could import files and make a drawing, but not write a model file. The new action writes what the export window writes, from the same bodies (visible solids, loose holes taken off, overlapping bodies merged for STL or separated by colour for 3MF and OBJ, tiny loose pieces left out). `format` picks stl, 3mf or obj, `ids` limits it to some objects, and with `path` the file is written to that place on the computer; without it the file comes back as base64. The answer lists size, bodies, triangles, merged bodies and the loose pieces left out. Chapter 14 of the guide and the skill list it.
- **The height of a body is no longer held to 220 mm (#425):** The field **Z (height)** (and the pivot's) jumped back to 220 whatever was typed, and the sliders and handles stopped there, also with "No printer - free size". A number can be typed in beyond the slider now, up and down, to 100 m as a guard against a typo, on X, Y and Z alike. The sliders reach a plate and a half across, as far as the printer is high where that is more, and never less than before. Reported through the contact form.
- **Tiny loose pieces are left out of an export (#352):** A cut, and the colour separation of a 3MF export, can leave hundreds of tiny loose pieces behind: closed shells of a few triangles, grains of sand a few tenths of a millimetre across that lie in or on the surface of a layer. They cannot be seen in the editor, but a slicer shows them as spikes, and they can lift a flat design off the plate. A design of one logo in six layers carried between 100 and 570 of them per layer. The STL, 3MF and OBJ export now leave out loose pieces that are tiny or thin (under half a cubic millimetre, at most 6 mm across, or a few triangles of no thickness), never the body itself, and the notice says how many were left out. A drawn small cube or pyramid stays. Reported by @Mycraftymama on GitHub.
- **A number on the edge of a turned box changes the side it stands at (#419):** With a workplane on a face, and a box turned by a quarter, the number typed into the long edge changed the other side: the box became a square instead of a bit shorter. The numbers on the edges count along the workplane, the fields Length and Width along the box itself, and the two were not matched up. Now the number on an edge changes the side it stands at, and the edge that was held stays where it was. Reported by @gquiring on GitHub.
- **A large circle in an imported SVG is round (#420):** Every curve of an SVG was cut into the same small number of pieces, so a large circle came out with about 32 corners that could be seen, and there was no setting for it. The pieces now follow the length of the curve (the longest piece is a two-hundredth of the drawing, at most 400 pieces per curve), so a circle that fills the drawing is round, and a small curve keeps its old number of pieces. A football logo of 212 mm has 11 % more triangles than before. Reported by @stainlessdmc12 on GitHub.
- **Grouping says so when imported parts above the triangle limit are only laid together (#423):** Above the limit (200,000 triangles by default, a setting of the workspace) grouping imported solids skips the merge and only lays their meshes together, which an export keeps as overlapping parts; a slicer then reports non-manifold edges. The status line now says that the parts are grouped but not merged, with the triangle count and the limit, as the cut already did, also when a group is closed and through the AI bridge. By @mimaroraima on GitHub.
- **A "Restore backup" button next to "Back up all" (#407):** A backup made with "Back up all" came back through "Open a design or import geometry", which few people look under. The notice about designs kept in the browser now has **Restore backup** beside **Back up all**; it asks for a ZIP file and does the same restore, skipping designs that are already there. It is also there on a new computer with no designs yet, where a backup is restored. Suggested by @danyvanimpe-lab on GitHub.
- **A picture with a transparent edge is traced as its figure, not its details (#345):** A black cat sticker on a transparent background came in as a handful of loose pieces (the light whiskers, eyes and buckle) instead of one outline. The import and the icon tag choose the part of the picture that is not its background, and a few black pixels of the figure touching the border made the dark part look like the background. When most of the edge is transparent, the transparency is the background now, and the part with more pixels is traced. Reported by @bryantwalley on GitHub.
- **The thread tile and the sample names showed a line-break code in Danish and Russian (#416):** The tile for thread, screw and nut in the shape library, and the sample names in the layered text window, were written with a doubled backslash in the Danish and Russian files, so they showed the code for a line break instead of three lines. They are three lines now, and a test checks every language for it. Reported by @Henrikth12.
- **A 2D drawing of a smooth, curved part (#414):** The drawing export refused a part of a few centimetres with the message that its views take more than 100,000 triangles: a smooth, curved surface is cut into very many of them at export quality, whatever the size. The limit is 400,000 now, and the message says how many triangles the views really take. The projection needs about 0.6 s per 250,000 triangles, so the old limit was far too careful. Reported by @MartinShake on GitHub.
## 1.80.0

- **The guide says how far a pattern spiral goes (#412):** The pattern's radius change was described as making a flat spiral, but an angle above 360° counts as a full circle and there are at most 100 copies, so it winds once around at most. The guide now says so in every language. Pointed out by @geoff315 on GitHub.
- **Danish:** The whole interface and the 16 chapters of the guide are in Danish (Dansk), with the language list and the guide's language switch. It is picked automatically for a browser set to Danish. It is not marked complete, so sentences added later show in English until their Danish follows. Made by @Henrikth12 with AI help and read through by a Danish speaker; wrong words are fixed with a small pull request (#411).
- **Undo steps survive a reload when the last step was made on a face workplane (#390):** A design whose last step was made with the workplane on a face lost its whole undo history when it was loaded again, for instance after an update with Save and reload: the design came back complete, but Undo, Redo and the history view were greyed out. Loading compared the stored last step to the base plate instead of to the workplane it was made on, found a difference and threw the history away. It compares to the step's own workplane now. Reported by @gquiring on GitHub.
- **Framing square: the height arrow stands where the guide lines meet:** The green arrow for the height stood at a corner of the body of its own, apart from the two guide lines along the floor. It now stands at the point where they meet. Suggested by DrJones in the forum.
- **The README says what a network copy can and cannot do offline (#409):** Installing layerling as an app and using it offline need an `https://` address (or `localhost`), and the offline files (`sw.js`) are only in the static export, not in the Docker image or `npm run start`. Both are now written under Docker and Static Hosting, and so is `LAYERLING_ALLOWED_DEV_ORIGINS`, which lets the development server be opened by a name such as `myhost.local`. Reported on GitHub.
- **French names of four shapes corrected (#406):** The shapes were called "Printemps" (the season) for Spring, "Équipement" for Gear, "Grenier" (an attic) for Loft and "Tube plié" for Bent Tube. They are now "Ressort", "Engrenage", "Lissage" and "Tube courbé", as is "Engrenage à chevrons" for the herringbone gear, and the French guide uses the same words. Suggested by @jeromefc50.
- **A backup restored twice no longer doubles your designs (#407):** Restoring a backup ZIP added every design as a new one, so restoring it into the same browser again left duplicates behind. A design that is already there (same name and same creation time) is now skipped, and the notice says how many were. The tooltip of "Back up all" also names the button that restores a backup, "Open a design or import geometry". Reported by @danyvanimpe-lab on GitHub.
- **Push a flat face out or cut a pocket in (#404):** A thicker wall, a raised pad or a pocket on a finished part took a workplane on the face, a traced sketch, an extrusion and a group, and on an imported STL there was no other way. A new tool, **Face**, in the Modify group next to the hole pattern, does it in one step: click a flat face, go out or in, and set the depth, an inset from the face's edge and a corner radius that rounds the inner corners too. A pocket goes no deeper than the body behind the face. What it adds is an ordinary sketch body grouped with the body, so Edit group, Edit sketch and Undo work as usual. On the top or the bottom of a plain sketch body it keeps that body's own outline, set in exactly by the CAD kernel where it can, and the new part keeps its inset when it is resized; on any other face, and on every face of an imported mesh, it follows the face's triangles. Also as the MCP action `layerling_push_face`. Contributed by @mlugo-apx.

## 1.79.0

- **Hollowing a rounded box with rounded top and bottom edges says why it fails (#401):** A rounded box whose top and bottom edges are rounded as well can only be hollowed with a wall clearly thinner than that rounding; with a thicker wall the panel only said that the walls cannot be this thick. It now adds the reason and what to do (a thinner wall, or the rounding set to 0 first) and points to the Bug report button in the footer, and the guide says so. Reported by @gquiring on GitHub.
- **Fillet and chamfer on a body cut by a group of holes (#394):** Two holes grouped first, that group made a hole and then grouped with a plate, ended in "The mesh is open or non-manifold" as soon as an edge was treated, although the cut was perfectly closed. The edge tool took the hole group as one mesh of two loose pieces, which the kernel could not join into one body. It now opens a group of nothing but holes (or of nothing but solids) into its parts, so every hole is the exact shape it was and its rim is a true curve, not facets. A mesh of several loose pieces is also joined into one body now, wherever it still turns up. Reported on GitHub.
- **Back and Close in the section view only on a tablet (#402):** The two buttons came for a tablet, which has no Esc key, but they showed on every device, where the X and Esc do the same and "Confirm" confirmed nothing. They are shown on a touch device only now, and "Confirm" is called "Close". The guide describes the panel's closing as rondlh wrote it. Reported by @rondlh on GitHub.
- **Sweep a sketch along a path (#400):** A third way from sketch to body, next to Extrude and Revolve: **Sweep sketch** pulls a cross-section along a path - a cable channel that turns a corner, a conduit, a guard round a belt, a ring round a hexagon. Draw the cross-section as a closed outline (a second outline inside it makes it hollow) and the path as a construction line, one chain of lines or curves, open or closed. The section is seen looking along the path from the end it is drawn near and centred on it, a corner of straight pieces is a mitre, and the body is exact, so Fillet, Chamfer and the STEP export work on it. Edit sketch goes back to section and path. The MCP bridge builds one from numbers with `layerling_sweep_sketch` (a rectangle or circle, solid or hollow, along a list of points). Asked for by dholling1 on GitHub; the hexagon ring is what #377 asked for.
- **Gemini CLI and Antigravity in the AI bridge setup:** The README and the AI chapter of the guide now say how to use the MCP server and the skill with Gemini CLI and Google Antigravity, with a config example (`docs/mcp/gemini-config.example.json`) and a copy of the skill that Antigravity finds in the workspace. A test keeps the three copies of the skill identical. By @corefile (#399).
- **The framing square can hide behind bodies, and its guide lines run along the floor:** The square's arms and ticks were drawn on the screen over everything, which looks confusing from many angles, and the guide lines of the green numbers ran up to the middle of the body in the air. Now the arms, ticks, arrows and guide lines are lines in the room: a body in front of them hides them, and the numbers on the arms fade there. A small eye below the handle switches that, so the square can also stay in view over everything. The guide lines run along the floor to the point below or beside the body; the height has its own arrow. The switch of each green number now sits in the frame of the number. Asked for by DrJones in the forum.
## 1.78.0

- **A locked object can be renamed and hidden in the properties again (#393):** The list of objects and the keys did it, but the properties window silently refused, because every change there also carried a new height, which a locked body does not accept. A change that is only a name, hiding or locking now goes through as it is. Contributed by @gogades.
- **A key ring hole on the icon tag:** The name tag had a hole for a key ring, with a tab round it on the bottom layer, but the icon tag did not (only because it was made later). The icon tag's card now has the same: **Key ring hole**, its diameter and its side. The hole goes through every layer, the tab sits on the bottom one beyond the icon, and the layers stay lined up. The AI bridge's `layerling_layer_icon` takes `keyring` as `layerling_layer_text` does. Asked for by @kwjaarsveld-star (#391).
- **Double Countersink (#392):** A new shape in the library, after the Countersink: a hole chamfered on both faces, for a plate that should look neat front and back. A cone at each end opens to the head diameter, the shaft sits between them; set the head diameter, the shaft diameter, the cone angle (90° to start with) and the total length. If the hole is too short for two full cones they meet in the middle at that angle and the shaft stays wider than set (head 10, shaft 6, 90°, length 2 make two cones from 10 down to 8 mm). It is an exact body, so the edge tools and the STEP export work on it, and the MCP bridge builds it as `doubleCountersink`. As a pattern hole it is not available yet. Asked for by AAWAV67 on GitHub.
- **Sketch: keys 1 to 4 and arrow keys on a handle (#347):** In the sketch and the revolve sketch the keys 1, 2, 3 and 4 pick Line, Bezier Curve, Smooth Curve and Add or Remove Points, as in Tinkercad; while a line is being drawn the digits stay its length. A curve handle that you click lights up, and the arrow keys then move it by one grid step (Shift: a larger step), a smooth point's other handle with it, so a curve can be set precisely; a run of presses is one step in the history. Asked for by @hlmodtech.
- **A browser that runs out of memory says so:** On a very large design, an export or a bug report could stop with the bare "Array buffer allocation failed", from the browser itself. It now says, in the person's language, that the browser has run out of memory for this step, and what to do: reduce the triangles of imported meshes with Simplify mesh, close other tabs and try again. Found with the screenshot of @shanni1783 (#383).
- **The zoom speed slider goes up to 14:** The wheel was still too slow for some at 10, with a Mac mouse that sends small steps. The slider now runs from 1 to 14. The first ten steps are exactly as they were; each step above them goes on with the same factor, so 14 zooms about 3.8 times as fast as 10. The zoom buttons and the + and - keys stop at the rate of 10, so they do not jump. Reported by @t9vh6j9rt4-collab (#331).
- **A plain notice on the start page that designs live in the browser:** Designs are kept in the browser's storage, and a browser can fail or cause problems after an update. A bold notice under the start tiles now says that layerling takes no responsibility for designs kept there, and that it is worth backing them up from time to time with **Back up all**, or saving them as files; the notice has the button for it. It cannot be closed. The guide says the same in the first chapter.
- **The guide names the language list:** Two sentences still spoke of the EN and DE buttons, or of "German or English", from before the language list. The guide's page on settings and chapter 15 now say that the language is chosen from the list at the top right, in English, German, Dutch, French, Czech, Russian and Slovenian.

## 1.77.0

- **The tube and the ring have an inner diameter:** Both had only the wall thickness, so a bushing for a shaft of a known size meant working out the wall by hand. The settings now have an **Inner diameter** beside the thickness: it is the width minus twice the wall, and typing it sets the wall. Set the width first; if you change it afterwards, the wall stays and the inner diameter changes with it. Asked for by @PotomacSharpening (#384).
- **Stretch letters to the frame (#382):** A straight text was always fitted into its frame with the proportions of its letters kept, so a frame pulled wider or deeper than the word needs stayed larger than the letters, and a capital could not be made lower the way it can in Tinkercad. A new switch, **Stretch letters to the frame**, fills the whole frame in both directions; the exact outlines for the edge tool and the fill modes follow, and the letter size of a stretched text is its height. The MCP action `layerling_update_object` takes `stretch` for it. Asked for by @Adams3DCrafts on GitHub.

- **A browser without WebAssembly SIMD gets a message that says so:** The CAD kernel is built with WebAssembly SIMD. In a browser or on a computer without it, the kernel stopped while it was compiled, and the edge tool answered "The CAD kernel ran out of room and was restarted", which sent people looking for a memory problem. A tiny test module now checks for SIMD before the 22 MB kernel is fetched, and the message says that the kernel needs WebAssembly SIMD and a current Chrome, Edge, Firefox or Safari. Found with the console log of @unico988 (#350).
- **A mouse-wheel notch zooms alike in every browser:** The wheel zooms by how far the browser says it has turned, and that number differs: Chrome on Windows sends 100 for a notch, Safari on a Mac with a plain mouse sent 12, so there a notch zoomed an eighth as much, even with Zoom speed at 10. A small step that arrives alone now counts like a full notch. A trackpad, a smooth wheel and a pinch send many small steps in a row and stay as they were. Reported by @t9vh6j9rt4-collab (#331).
- **Hungarian:** The whole interface and the 16 chapters of the guide are in Hungarian (Magyar), informal like the German, with the language list and the guide's language switch. It is picked automatically for a browser set to Hungarian. It is not marked complete, so sentences added later show in English until their Hungarian follows. Made by @plazmabokor with an AI assistant; a Hungarian reader is welcome to go through it, wrong words are fixed with a small pull request.

## 1.76.1

- **A handle dragged past the opposite side keeps growing there:** The side and corner handles of a shape or a selection, and the arrow on top, stopped at the smallest size when you pulled them over the other side, so you had to let go and pick up the opposite handle. Now the shape keeps growing on the other side of that edge, as in Tinkercad; a selection is mirrored with it. A body made of a mesh still stops at the smallest height. From @andrej-cds' list of iPad changes (#170), and asked for by @rondlh (#329).
- **Easier on a tablet:** A finger that starts on empty space turns the view, and a tap there clears the selection, so the turn switch is not needed for it; a finger on a shape or a handle works as before, and the selection box stays with the mouse and Shift. The turn switch also shows on an iPad that does not report a coarse pointer. The section view has "Confirm" and "Back" buttons, because a tablet has no Enter or Esc. From @andrej-cds' list (#170).
- **A picture plate no longer slows down a drag:** The material signature of a shape held the whole image data URL, several MB, and was rebuilt on every move. It now carries the length and four short samples. Found by @andrej-cds (#170).
- **The lift arrow stays near tall bodies:** Its distance grew with the height of the body, so it stood far away over a tall one. It is capped at 12 mm now; smaller bodies look as before. Suggested by @andrej-cds (#170).
- **An OBJ with a colour at every point opens (#351):** A model from a scan or an AI tool such as Tripo can carry its own colour at every point. layerling made one body per colour, and a file of 980,000 points became 125,000 bodies, which crashed the editor. A file with more than 64 vertex colours now comes in as one body in a single colour, with a note that the colour gradient is not kept; files with a few colours stay one body per colour. Reported by shanni1783 on GitHub.

## 1.76.0

- **The edge under the pointer is easy to see in the edge tools:** In Fillet and Chamfer the light blue line of the hovered edge had almost no contrast where it ran in front of the pale floor, so the near bottom edges were hard to pick, and worse when the shape was lifted off the plane. The hovered edge now has a dark outline around it and reads clearly on light and dark ground. Reported by @rondlh (#376).
- **A run of arrow presses is one step in the history:** Every press of an arrow key moving the selection (also Ctrl with Up and Down, and in the sketch) was a step of its own, so holding a key or tapping it a few times filled the history with tiny steps. Now a press that follows the last one within a second and a half, on the same selection and with nothing else done in between, replaces that step instead of adding one, so Undo takes back the whole run. A run that ends where it started leaves no step. (#370)
- **Czech:** The whole interface, the 16 chapters of the guide, the release notes and the guide's own pages are in Czech, with the language list and the guide's language switch. It is picked automatically for a browser set to Czech. The PDF of a drawing writes the base letter where its standard typeface lacks a Czech or Polish accent (the SVG keeps the original). Made by @mispacek with an AI assistant (pull request #379); a second Czech reader is still welcome, wrong words are fixed with a small pull request.
- **Slovenian:** The whole interface, the 16 chapters of the guide and the guide's own pages are in Slovenian, with the language list and the guide's language switch. It is picked automatically for a browser set to Slovenian. Made by @andrej-cds with an AI assistant (discussion #170); a second Slovenian reader is welcome, wrong words are fixed with a small pull request.
- **A custom snap grid used whole carries its name alone:** In the snap menu a custom grid you named "10 mm" read "1 × 10 mm". Used whole it now reads "10 mm"; the halves and quarters still read "½ × 10 mm" and "¼ × 10 mm". Reported by @rondlh (#329).
- **Measuring a circle in the section view:** The cut through a round part, a cylinder or a hole, is a polygon, so a click could only land on one of its corners and a distance across it was never quite the diameter. Round outlines are now recognised: a small cross marks the centre, the centre snaps, a first point on the circle makes the second one land exactly across the centre, and the number then reads Ø (the diameter); from the centre to the circle it reads R (the radius). Ellipses from slanted cuts are not recognised. The MCP action `layerling_measure_section` snaps the same way and returns `circle` ("diameter", "radius" or null). (#375)
- **A preview of the intersection, and shapes that share nothing stay:** A new button beside Boolean Intersection, "Show common part", turns the selected shapes see-through and draws what they share in green. Select and move each shape on its own and the green part follows (it is calculated again a moment after every change; above 40,000 triangles the preview says it is too big). Boolean Intersection applies it, Esc closes the preview. And when the selected shapes share nothing, Intersection no longer removes them all: they stay and a message says so. Asked for by @prmod3d (#372).
## 1.75.0

- **French:** The whole interface and the 16 chapters of the guide are in French, with the language list and the guide's language switch. It is picked automatically for a browser set to French. Made by @ZelTroN-2k3 with an AI assistant (pull request #368); a French reader is still welcome to go through it, wrong words are fixed with a small pull request.
- **Arrow keys move by 0.01 mm when the snap grid is off:** With the grid off an arrow key moved the selection by 0.1 mm. It now moves it by 0.01 mm, and Shift with an arrow by five times that. With a grid on, the arrows still follow the grid; for 0.1 mm steps choose the 0.1 mm grid. Suggested by @HopGabor62 (#370).
- **The keys stand beside the names in a shape's settings, and Multicolor has one:** Solid (S), Hole (H), Transparent (T) and Edit group (E) now show their keys in the settings card, and Multicolor, which had none, gets **Shift+M**: it switches the selected groups between each part in its own colour and one colour for the group, and it is in the shortcuts list. Shift+M used to turn on the mirror mode like M; M alone still does. Suggested by @prmod3d (#367).
- **A custom snap grid can be offered whole only:** Each custom snap grid in the workspace settings has a switch "Also half and quarter". With it off, the snap menu offers just the size you typed, not its half and quarter as well (so "10 mm" no longer adds a second 5.0 mm). It stays on for the grids you already have. Suggested by @rondlh (#329).
- **A text with a line round its letters can be cut out again:** With a block font the mesh of such a text could have small gaps along letters that touch, which the 3D kernel refused ("not manifold"), so grouping it as a hole with a plate stopped with "Could not cut this imported mesh cleanly". The 2D kernel now extrudes the text itself, and its mesh is closed. Reported by @Mycraftymama (#352).
- **Extrude and Revolve stand side by side:** In the Sketch tab the two ways to build a body from a sketch were in a drop-down under "Sketch to 3D". They are now two buttons next to each other in the Create group, each with its hint as tooltip and the question mark beside them, so switching between the modes takes one click less. Suggested by @prmod3d (#366).
- **The sketch can show the parts seen through:** The sketch showed a body only as its outline at the height of the sketch plane. A new button with layers in the view buttons on the left, "Show the parts seen through", draws all edges of the bodies on the sketch plane, thin and faint, whatever height they lie at, so you can trace round existing work; on an upright workplane set on a front face it is the front view of the part. Every edge that turns by more than 30 degrees, the outline of a body as seen from the plane and every open edge is drawn; a mesh with a great many is thinned to 30,000 pieces. The choice is kept in the browser. Asked for by @meecenz (#354).
- **Video list:** "10 things I love about Layerling and 3 things that Tinkercad does better" by TECHNOBENTO is now first in the list on the start page and in the guide.
- **Center view reaches parts beyond the plate:** A part lying beyond the edge of the plate could not be brought to the middle of the screen, because the view's pivot could not leave the plate. Center view now goes as far out as the selected part; the house button takes it back. Reported by @Mycraftymama (#352).
- **The three start boxes share one ground:** On the start page the box "Create new 3D design" had a cream ground, the two beside it white, and in the dark theme it was tinted orange. All three now have the same cream ground (in the dark theme the same dark one), and the icons of the two plain boxes no longer sit on a square of their own; the orange border and the solid orange icon still mark the first one.
- **The icon tag takes PNG and JPG pictures:** The icon tag window's **Browse...** now also takes a PNG or JPG file. The picture is traced into an outline, and the preview shows the outline; under it, **Traced from** chooses whether the **Dark part** or the **Light part** of the picture becomes the icon, at first the part that is not the background, the background being the part that takes up more of the picture's edge. Transparent pixels never belong to the icon. An SVG that holds nothing but one picture, as Inkscape saves an imported bitmap, is traced the same way instead of being refused; an SVG that only links to a picture outside it now says so in plain words. **Another file...** on the icon tag card takes pictures as well. The tracer is layerling's own: the outline runs between the pixels, specks are left out and the line is straightened to at most 1200 pieces, so the CAD kernel builds the layers quickly. Suggested by @plazmabokor in #359.
- **PNG and JPG pictures in the import:** The import window (and the start page's import) takes PNG and JPG files. A picture is traced into an outline, as for the icon tag, and comes in 50 mm wide with the picture's proportions, ready to be resized, in the editor as a sketch body like an SVG: the part of the picture that is not its background, the background being the part that takes up more of the picture's edge. An SVG that holds nothing but one picture is traced the same way instead of being refused, and the design keeps the traced outline as its SVG. An SVG that holds a picture next to a drawing says so. Suggested by @plazmabokor in #359.

## 1.74.0

- **Snap grid: the dragged face lands on the grid:** Dragging the height handle snapped the change of the height by whole grid steps, so a body 0.5 mm high on a 1 mm grid went to 1.5 mm. Now the face you drag (the top, or the bottom for the lower handle) lands on the grid of the workplane, so it goes to 1.0 mm, and the lift arrow puts the bottom of the body on the grid the same way. A tilted selection keeps the old step. Reported by @rondlh (#329).
- **A double click on a slider sets it back to its default:** The small arrow next to a changed value in a shape's settings was the only way back. Now a double click on the slider does the same, there and for seven sliders of the workspace settings (shadow strength and softness, the light's direction and height, shade contrast, overhang angle, zoom speed). The sliders of the small tool windows (edge tool, simplify, split) have no default to go back to and stay as they are. Suggested by @AD3DStuff (#361).
- **The guide shows how to set an exact angle between two faces:** There is no tool for it, but Align faces, then the rotation pivot on the edge where the faces meet and the angle typed in the Rotation card does it. The chapter on selecting and arranging now says so, in the section on laying flat on a face. Asked in #360.
- **A new name in the STL export asks for a new file:** In Chrome and Edge the first STL export asks where to save, and every later one writes into that file again. The file name field at the top of the export window then did nothing: a model and its liner exported one after the other with two names ended up in the same file. A name typed there that is neither the one the window started with nor the linked file's own now asks where to save, and the line under the window says so ("Asks where to save ..."). The guide says in the STL row that overlapping bodies are joined into one mesh in an STL, and that 3MF keeps them as parts. Reported by mail.
- **Framing square: every number counts to the edge or the middle on its own:** Beside each green number of the framing square sits a small switch, so a standing cylinder can be placed by its middle in the two arms and by its bottom in height, and a lying one by its middle across and an end along. The button at the handle still switches all three at once and shows the middle only when all three count to it. Typing a width, depth or height with the square follows the same switch. Suggested by DrJones in the forum.
- **The height dimension stands outside the body:** The height of a selected body was drawn through its middle, where its number sat on the shape. It now runs along the vertical edge that stands furthest right on the screen, with the number outside. Reported by DrJones in the forum.
- **A cut that stays open says so:** Cutting a part that has a gap-ridden surface - a STEP part whose mesh has a few cracks, say - gave a body that was open inside, because the clean cut refuses such a mesh and the fallback takes the material away without closing the cut face. The notice after grouping now says that the cut face is open, how many open edges the surface has, and that importing the part as STL or 3MF may give a closed mesh. Reported by @dsptech11-prog (#346).
- **Align and Snap to Grid stand under Arrange:** The two tools sat in the Modify group of the ribbon beside Chamfer, Fillet and Hollow, but they place a shape, they do not change it. They now head the Arrange group, next to Drop to workplane, Lay flat, Mate faces and Center on workplane, and take its colour; the command search lists them there as well. Nothing else moved. Suggested by @AkiraNorthstar (#357).
- **The object list stays open:** It started closed every time a design was opened or the page was loaded. Open or closed now stays as you left it, for every design, in this browser. Suggested by @grmonsey (#356).
- **A revolve can be built from a line with a width:** In a revolve sketch the Stroke button is back. Draw the wall of a vase or a cup as one open line to the left of the axis, tick the stroke and set the width: the revolve turns that band into a thin wall, as in Tinkercad. Closed outlines work too (a frame); what reaches across the axis is cut off at it. The body keeps the drawn line, so Edit sketch goes on from there. Suggested by @hlmodtech (#347).
- **The lift arrow keeps its distance:** On a thin body the arrow that lifts the selection sat only 2 mm above the middle handle, a few pixels on the screen, and covered it. It now stays at least 30 pixels away, whatever the zoom and the view. Reported by @Mycraftymama (#353).
- **Russian:** The whole interface, the 16 chapters of the guide and the guide's own pages are in Russian, with the language list and the guide's language switch. It is picked automatically for a browser set to Russian. Made by @CjCrash with an AI assistant (pull request #124); a second Russian reader is still welcome, wrong words are fixed with a small pull request. Along the way the guide builder learned two things any language profits from: a language without pictures of its own points at the English pictures instead of carrying a copy of them, and a heading in Cyrillic gets an id of its own instead of an empty one.
## 1.73.0

- **The corner ruler no longer draws a fan of lines:** With the camera close above the ruler, looking along one of its arms, the part of the arm that lay behind the camera was drawn mirrored on the screen: a fan of fine lines that crossed the picture. Lines, ticks and numbers of the ruler (and the arrows and guide lines of its distances) are now cut off where they pass the camera, so only what is in front of it is drawn. Reported in the forum.
- **Video list:** The second entry, a single video by HL ModTech, is now their whole playlist "Layerling Tutorials", so new videos in it appear without a release.
- **A detailed SVG imports again (#340):** An SVG with one very detailed outline, such as a traced drawing, stopped with "Maximum call stack size exceeded" and did not come in. Building the body handed all its triangles over in one call, which has a size limit that thousands of points overrun; they are now added one at a time. Reported by @shanni1783.
- **Touch screens: a finger reaches further:** On a touch screen, a press on the sketch plate or on a line that lands beside a point, a handle or another line goes to that part, so small points and thin lines can be grabbed with a fingertip. The tape measure (points, lines and reference points), the section measure and the edges in the chamfer and fillet tool catch the pointer from a larger distance when it is a finger. A mouse and a stylus keep the reach they had. The guide now recommends a stylus on a tablet. Suggested by @cyryllo (#337).
- **The tape measure snaps to reference points:** Mark the centre of a circle or the middle of a line as a reference point (the crosshair button under a measurement, or the right-click menu of a body), then click that point with the tape measure to measure from it. Suggested by @RobbieKnobbie (#334).
- **A way back from the server folder:** Inside "Designs on the server" the only way back to the designs of the browser was the logo. A button **Back to designs in this browser** stands at the left of the row with New design, New folder and Refresh. Suggested by @foofoodog (#333).
## 1.72.3

- **Reference points: delete them all, and cards that behave:** Marking the centre, corners or edge middles of a body leaves several points, and deleting them one by one was cumbersome. **Delete all** in a point card, and "Delete all reference points" in the right-click menu, take them all away in one step (it can be undone). The cards no longer move as a group: each is dragged away on its own, and the card you touched last stands in front, so a card can no longer end up behind another. A point whose card was never moved keeps the place the shared offset had before. Reported by @rondlh (#329).
- **A wrapped shape is not wrapped a second time:** "Wrap around a cylinder" could be repeated on a shape that was wrapped already, and the second wrapping gave a mesh of an unexpected size and shape. The card is not offered for a wrapped shape any more, and the AI tool answers that the shape is wrapped already; undo the wrapping, or wrap the flat original again. Reported by @rondlh (#329).
- **The edge tool names chamfer and fillet in your language in its messages:** "Select one solid to chamfer" and "Preparing fillet edges" got the word as a raw English identifier in every language, so a German message read "Wähle einen Körper für chamfer". They take the name of the tool as the interface spells it now. Pointed out by @cyryllo in the Polish translation (#307).
- **Zooming into the sky no longer tips the view down near the back edge:** The camera that now goes on forward when the pointer is on the background (1.72.2) kept the pivot inside the plate by cutting each coordinate to the plate in turn. Near the back edge that turned the line of sight: the camera looked steeply down. The distance is now cut short along the line of sight, so the view keeps its direction. Reported in the forum.
- **Shared folder: what the README had left out (#301):** With Docker, the shared folder showed on the start page but nothing could be saved in it, because the container runs as the user 999 and a mounted folder is usually owned by somebody else. The README and `compose.yml` now say so (`chown 999:999`, or run the container as yourself with `user:`). With `npm run start` they now say that `npm run build` comes first and that the folder must be writable by the user who starts it. Reported by @foofoodog.
- **Turn a selection in the sketch by any angle:** The sketch could turn only a whole closed outline, 45 degrees at a time with R, so a single line, a curve, several outlines together or an angle in between could not be turned at all. A round handle now stands above the frame round a selection: dragging it turns everything selected - points, lines and curve handles - about the middle of the selection, with the angle shown over the handle, and with Shift in steps of 15 degrees. R still turns a closed outline by 45 degrees; the guide now says so in the text, not only in the table of keys. Asked for by @lukyanenkomax (#330), and earlier in the forum and by @strelniece (#260).
## 1.72.2

- **Sliders in whole millimetres also in Firefox:** A length slider snaps to the grid while it is dragged, but in Firefox the value jumped to a crooked one in the neighbourhood when the mouse button was let go. Firefox sends one last value after the pointer is up, and by then the slider no longer counted as dragged. It now counts as dragged for a moment longer, so the last value is snapped too. Reported in the forum.
- **Zooming into the sky no longer runs into a wall:** With the pointer above the plate, on the background, the mouse wheel zoomed towards the old pivot with a share of the distance at each notch, so the steps got smaller and the camera stopped at its closest distance although the way ahead was free. When nothing under the pointer can be zoomed towards, the pivot is now kept 50 mm ahead of the camera, within the box the pivot may be in, so the camera goes on. Reported in the forum.
- **Alt+Enter resizes round the middle:** A size typed into one of the numbers beside a shape made the shape grow from the edge opposite the number, as when a handle is dragged, so two shapes that had been aligned on a common centre fell out of line after a resize and needed aligning again. With **Alt+Enter** instead of Enter the shape now grows round its middle, for width and length, in the single number and in the pair that opens on a click at a corner. It is the key that makes a line grow to both sides in the sketch. The height always grows upward from the underside. The size fields in the properties keep the middle without a key, and Alt while dragging a corner handle does so as before. Suggested by @micro4rgb-maker (#327).
- **A cylinder can become an ellipse:** A cylinder is round on purpose - one diameter, handles that change both sides together - so that nothing turns it into an oval by accident. Anyone who wants an oval after all had to build an Ellipse and move everything over. A cylinder now has a button **Make it an ellipse**: the shape becomes an Ellipse, the same body, and its length and width can be set one by one. It is not offered for a cylinder with a chamfer, a fillet or a wall, or a turned, imported or grouped one, which would lose its exact body. The MCP tool `layerling_update_object` takes `makeEllipse` for the same. Asked about by @kwjaarsveld-star (#322).
- **Align puts a teardrop hole on the middle of its round part:** "Centre" took the middle of the box round a teardrop, which has its tip on top, so the round part sat about a millimetre below the middle of the box (0.98 mm for 9.5 mm). A teardrop hole aligned with a tapped hole therefore did not lie on its circle. Now the middle of a teardrop is the middle of its round part, up and down as well. With that a threaded hole on its side gets a teardrop of its own that opens its top: a teardrop of the thread core and the tapped hole, aligned in the middle and grouped. The guide describes it in the chapter on threads. Asked about by @robstern (#268).
## 1.72.1

- **A sketch outline stretched by its frame lands on whole values:** Pulling a handle of the frame round a selected outline - a rectangle of 20, say - gave sizes like 24.83 although the snap grid was on. The handle stands off the edge it moves by a gap that follows the zoom; the pointer was snapped and the gap taken off afterwards, so the edge came out crooked. Now the edge itself lands on the grid. Reported in the forum.
- **A reference point at the middle of a measurement:** Under the number of every tape measurement there is now a small crosshair button that puts a reference point in its middle: after a click on a round edge - the rim of a hole, even on an imported mesh - the centre of that circle, and for a diameter or any straight measurement the point halfway along it. A shape you drag snaps to it with its centre, so a new hole or magnet pocket can be placed exactly on an existing hole without typing numbers. Before, reference points could only be put on the centre, corners and edge middles of a body. Suggested in #323.
- **The guide printout is easier to find:** The link to the whole guide for printing is now a button with a printer icon at the top of the chapter list and on the overview page.
## 1.72.0

- **The whole guide on one page, to print:** Every chapter of the guide could be printed on its own, but not the guide as a whole. A new page in each language - the link **Whole guide, to print** under the chapter list - shows all chapters one after the other with a table of contents, each chapter starting on a new page, the pictures included, in light colours whatever theme you use. Press Ctrl+P (or the Print button) to print it or to save it as a PDF; to print only a part, choose its page numbers in the print dialog. The page is not stored for offline use and not offered to search engines.
- **Import/Export window toggle:** The keyboard shortcuts for import and export toggle the window on or off, like other windows that have a shortcut (eg. fillet, chamfer, hollow). By @gogades (#319).
- **Dutch: a round of corrections:** A native-speaker read-through of the Dutch interface and guide fixed wrong or half-translated sentences (the number of printers, the name of the bug report button, "solid" left in English, a decimal comma) and settled on one word each for the intersection ("Doorsnijding", so "Doorsnede" stays the section view) and the box shape ("Blok"). By @brauwers1981.
- **A material estimate that is near the real number:** The Material box in the export window counted every body as solid, which for a bulky part is easily three times what a slicer uses with walls and 15 % infill. It now has an **Infill** (15 % to begin with) and a **Walls** choice (2) and works out weight and filament from the surface and the volume: a skin on every surface - the walls on the sides, a thicker skin on top and bottom - and the infill's share of what lies inside, so a thin part stays solid. The volume stays the body's own, and the note under the numbers says what was counted and what the part would weigh solid. At 100 % infill it counts solid, as before. It is still a rough guide - the slicer's settings decide - and the MCP tool `layerling_estimate_print` gives the same estimate next to the solid numbers (`infillPercent`, `walls`). Suggested by @gogades (#320).
- **Align puts a hole in the middle of a triangle, a pentagon or a star:** "Centre" took the middle of the box round a shape. For a triangle, a pentagon, a five-pointed star and the like that is not the middle of the shape, so a hole centred on one sat beside the middle: about 5 mm for a triangle with a 20 mm circumradius, 1.9 mm for a pentagon or a five-pointed star. Centre now means the shape's own middle for the regular polygons (also the pyramids over them) and for the star; a hexagon, a square, a cylinder and every other shape stay as they were, and left, right, front, back, top and bottom still go by the box. Reported by @robstern (#268).
- **Designs on your own server, without moving them one by one:** When shared server storage is on, the start page has two new controls. **Put all on the server** moves every design of this browser into the server folder in one go, and the tick **New designs on the server** makes each new design start there, so it is saved to the server from the first change on. Until now a design stayed in the browser unless you remembered to move it, and a lost device or cleared browser storage took it along. A design whose name is already taken on the server stays where it is, and the message says how many. The tick is kept in this browser. An operator can set it for everyone: `LAYERLING_NEW_DESIGNS_ON_SERVER` in the environment of a Node or Docker copy - `true` starts new designs on the server for people who have not chosen (they can untick it), `always` holds for everyone and locks the tick. Suggested in #316.

## 1.71.0

- **layerling speaks Dutch:** The interface, the whole user guide (16 chapters) and the README are available in Dutch (Nederlands). The language is chosen from the browser - a visitor whose browser asks for Dutch lands in it - and from the language list. It was translated by @rmpel (#312), with the help of an AI, and it is open to corrections from anyone who reads it: a pull request with the fixed word is all it takes. How to add further languages is in `docs/TRANSLATING.md`.
- **A thin scrollbar on the settings panel:** The panel on the right hid its scrollbar, so on a laptop's touchpad nothing showed that it goes on below, and there was no bar to take hold of. It shows a thin bar now, in its own colours in the light, dark and graphite themes; a short panel shows none. By @plazmabokor (#315).
- **The design's name sits right in the corner:** The name written on the plate no longer leaves room for the axis arrows but starts at the front left corner as if they were not there, with the same small gap from both edges, so it takes less of the plate and gets in the way of bodies less often.

## 1.70.0

- **The tape measure: a point on a round edge, and the diameter across a circle:** A click on an edge, a bore for example, measured the whole edge - the length of the circle - and was done; there was no way to put a point on it. The tape measure's menu has a new switch, **Measure whole edges**: with it off, a click sets a point on the edge, and the second one goes anywhere. **Alt** turns the switch round for one click. On a circle the point across from the first one snaps in when the pointer comes near, and points on a round edge sit on the true circle, not on a side of the polygon it is drawn as, so the diameter comes out exact (30.00 for a 30 mm bore, where a click on the polygon gave 29.96). Suggested in #308.
- **21 more printers, 221 in all, the large ones among them:** Goofoo (GP1, TurboM, Velo+, Velo+ Tool), the big machines Elegoo OrangeStorm Giga (810 x 805 x 1010), Creality CR-6 Max, Wanhao D9-400 and D9-500, Volumic EXO42, EXO65 and SH65 Performance, RatRig V-Core 3 and 4 in 400 and 500 (and the two IDEX), Vivedino Troodon 2.0 and Xplorer, Tronxy X5SA 400. Plates and heights are the ones from OrcaSlicer. Left out on purpose: kit, dual-extruder-mode and firmware variants, and the industrial makers.
- **Drawings for the workshop:** The export window has a new format, **2D**: front, top and right-side views at 1:1 on an ISO sheet (first-angle projection, A4 up to A0 or larger), with the overall size of every view, hidden edges and a table of the round bores that run parallel to a view axis, as vector PDF or SVG - to print or to take to the lathe and mill. The words on the sheet follow the interface language, and an AI can make the drawing too (`layerling_export_drawing`). Built by @WC3D (#309, #89), based on his drawing work for Cadverix-3D.
- **The Docker image is on Docker Hub too:** `henmedia/layerling` - the same image as at `ghcr.io/henmedia/layerling`, for amd64 and arm64, published with every release under its version number and as `latest`. A NAS finds it in its registry search (Synology Container Manager, for example) and can update it there. The first tag is 1.69.0. Suggested in #310.
- **Editing a sketch no longer moves the body:** When you opened the sketch of a body, pulled a point or a curve handle outward and finished, the body was centred on its new outline but kept its old middle, so everything you had not touched slid sideways - by half of what the outline grew, or by the bulge of a curve, which made the sketch look shifted when you opened it again. The body now moves with its outline, so what you left alone stays where it was, and the sketch lies exactly over the body when you open it, curves included. Reported by @AkiraNorthstar in #291.
- **Geometry kernel 5.7.1:** The CAD kernel (occt-wasm) moves from 5.7.0 to the patch release 5.7.1; all kernel tests pass unchanged.

## 1.69.0

- **Hollowing a block with a pocket:** A block with a hole that does not go all the way through - a box grouped with a raised hole - could not be hollowed with its top open (the default): the kernel's thick solid does not take a face with a pocket in it, and the message said the body could not be hollowed with any wall. Where it fails, the body is now grown over the open face, hollowed from its inward offset, and the cavity cut out of the body as it was - the way a closed body is hollowed. The result is exact: for a 20 x 10 x 20 block with a 10 x 10 pocket 5 deep and a 1.5 mm wall it has the volume worked out by hand. The same goes for the bottom, both ends and a hole right through. Reported in the forum as a wish for mould making (#302).
- **Ten more printers, 200 in all:** Creality Ender-3 V4, Ender-5S, CR-10 V3, CR-M4, CR-M4 SE and K2 SE, Elegoo Neptune 2D, Anycubic 4Max Pro 2, Sovol SV01 Pro and Flashforge Guider 3 Ultra, with the plate and height from OrcaSlicer. The list is looked at against OrcaSlicer's once a week now, so new printers and corrected plates do not go unnoticed.
- **The printers are in alphabetical order:** In the workspace settings, under Workplane, the list of printers followed the order of the slicer's profiles: Bambu Lab had its A1 mini before its A1, with the P1P and X1 in between and the H2 models last. The makers and the models of each maker are sorted now, numbers counted as numbers (CR-6 SE before CR-10, Kobra 2 before Kobra 3). The list of profiles itself is unchanged (#305).
- **Text from LightBurn comes in whole, nearly:** An SVG with text made in LightBurn lost half of its letters on import. Their outlines carry points along the straight edges, the extrusion put a triangle of no area at each of them, and a letter with such a triangle was left out as no solid. Points that lie on the line between their neighbours are now dropped when the outline is read, before the extrusion. In the file of the report 18 of 20 letters come in now, where 10 were left out. The remaining two have an outline that runs along itself and still need joining in the drawing program, as the message after the import says (#304).
- **A thread profile of your own: flank angle, depth and core diameter:** A new **Custom (trapezoid)** under the thread's profile: a tooth with a flat crest and a flat root of the same width, set by the **flank angle** (the angle between the two flanks, 10 to 90 degrees, 60 is the V thread) and the **thread depth**, or the **core diameter**, which is the diameter minus twice the depth - changing one changes the other. At 90 degrees or less no flank is flatter than 45 degrees, so the thread prints without supports; machine screw profiles are standardised but not made for 3D printing. The depth is limited by the angle, the pitch and the diameter, follows a change of the pitch and of the scale, and the play and chamfers work as with the other profiles. Through the MCP bridge: `threadProfile` `custom` with `threadFlankAngle` and `threadDepth`. The own profile is built in the mesh and in the exact body, in a nut, a tapped hole, a rod and a screw. Suggested in the forum.
- **A thread chamfer of any width above zero counts:** A chamfer of 0.001 mm made it vanish, because only more than 0.001 mm counted. A tiny one is a cone that opens the mouth of a tapped hole or nut to exactly the outer diameter and cuts the crests at the rim - good for a hole whose thread starts a few millimetres behind the opening, with a plain bore of that diameter in front. Now any width above 0 builds it, in the mesh and in the exact body. 0 still means no chamfer. Suggested in the forum.
- **Zooming near the top edge no longer jumps out:** With the pointer close to the top of the view, in a view seen from the side, the mouse wheel zoomed far out instead of in. The wheel zooms towards a point under the pointer, and a ray that runs almost level met the plate thousands of millimetres away; the camera was then pulled back onto the plate and the picture with it. Such a point is now left alone, and the zoom goes on around the old pivot. Reported in the forum.
- **Sliders snap to the grid:** Dragged with the mouse, the slider of a length - the height of a box, say - now snaps to the snap step, so it comes out in whole millimetres instead of steps of 0.81. Not with the grid off, not on short sliders, and a typed value and the arrow keys on the slider stay exact. Suggested in the forum.
## 1.68.1

- **Tab goes from value to value in the properties panel:** After a number field, Tab stopped on its slider and on the small reset arrow before it reached the next field: three presses per value. The slider and the reset arrow are out of the Tab order now, so Tab steps straight from one number field to the next. Both still work with the mouse (#300).
- **The tape measure no longer lags on a dense imported mesh:** With the tape measure on, every mouse move went through all the edges of every body, and joining the line pieces into edges took longer the more there were: on a design with a Raspberry Pi model one move took about four seconds. The edges are now kept while the body stays where it is, the pieces are joined through a lookup instead of a search, and the points are only built where the pointer is. The same design takes about 65 ms per move now, and the first move after switching the tool on about a second (#299).
- **A new video in the list:** "The PERFECT FREE CAD Program Between Tinkercad and Fusion 360!" by Print N Repeat, at the top of the video list in the app, the guide and the README.
- **Locked objects show it and hold still:** A selected locked object now greys out its resize, lift and rotate handles and shows a padlock beside the Z arrow; hovering a handle still shows the sizes. Every Combine, Modify and Arrange tool and Delete are greyed out, the right-click menu leaves out what would change it, and every key or command that would change it says "Object locked" - before, some reported success ("Moved 1 shape", "Selection to hole") without doing anything, and others failed without a word. One locked object now holds the whole selection: moving, turning, snapping, dropping, centring, scaling, colouring or dragging a mixed selection no longer changes only the unlocked parts. Locked objects can no longer be deleted or cut, and Hide in the toolbar and Ctrl+H now hide them, as the outliner and the properties panel already did. Locking while a tool is open closes the tool. Align keeps working with a locked part in the selection, which stays in place as the anchor. Nine different lock messages became the one "Object locked".

## 1.68.0

- **Arabic and Hebrew read right to left, Arabic letters join up:** A text in Arabic came out mirrored, letter by letter from the left, and each letter stood alone instead of joining its neighbours. Text in Arabic, Persian, Urdu or Hebrew is now laid out right to left - in a mixed line only the right-to-left words turn round, digits stay - and Arabic letters take their initial, medial and final forms, lam-alef its ligature. The forms come from the font: from its character map or from its shaping rules when it is added. This needs a font of your own with these letters, such as Arial, Noto Naskh Arabic or Amiri; the built-in typefaces have none. Reported by @iq0099 (#294).

## 1.67.6

- **Arrow keys take fine steps when the snap grid is off:** With the grid switched off, the arrow keys still moved a shape by a whole millimetre (5 with Shift), although dragging with the mouse was free. They now move by 0.1 mm (0.5 with Shift), in the 3D view and in the sketch (#293).
- **One snap grid for the sketch and the 3D view:** The sketch kept a grid of its own. A grid picked there did not reach the 3D editor and was gone when you came back from it. Now both use the same grid, and it is saved with the design (#291).

## 1.67.5

- **Slanted walls for stamps, a tip in the guide:** The sketches chapter has a new section, "Slanted walls, for stamps": layerling has no draft angle for an extrusion, but for plain outlines a body with Taper and a hole with its own Taper give slanted walls outside and inside, step by step. Suggested by @HolmanCotton.
- **The Script typeface draws Cyrillic, Greek and Polish letters:** The Script typeface lacked the Cyrillic and Greek alphabets and 37 letters of Latin Extended-A, among them the Polish ą and ę, and drew a question mark for each. It now borrows them from Sans, as the other typefaces already did, and a test checks the Russian, Ukrainian, Greek and Polish letters in every typeface.
- **Search in the German guide finds umlauts typed as two letters:** "aushoehlen" finds "aushöhlen", "ueber" finds "über", as the command search in the editor already did. English guides take a query as typed.
- **Raw English error messages get a sentence around them in every language,** not only in German, as soon as there are more languages; a message that is one of the language's own sentences is left alone.
- **The import window's picture in the guide** shows the new SVG box.
- **Thread fields say their direction (#256):** On a nut or tapped hole the two play fields are now called **Clearance (XY)** and **Play along the axis (Z)**, German **Spiel (XY)** and **Spiel in Achsrichtung (Z)**, so it shows at a glance that the first opens the thread across the diameter and the second along the axis, as in Tinkercad's thread tools. Suggested by @hlmodtech.
- **The flat view no longer cuts off what is near the camera:** In the flat (orthographic) view, the front part of a scene could vanish: after zooming close in on something and switching to the flat view, then zooming out or turning it, everything nearer than the camera was cut off, the plate included; going back to the normal view and again to the flat one brought it back for a while. The flat view's depth range now reaches behind its camera as far as in front, so nothing in the picture is cut off, and the edge preview and the helpers for moving, aligning and mirroring follow it. By @gogades (PR #287).
- **Writing a note:** Moving the cursor back into the text of a note and typing or deleting there threw the cursor to the end after every key, because the text came back to the field the long way round, through the editor and the view, and the field showed the old text in between. The field now keeps what you type itself while you write, so the cursor stays where you put it. Writing has keys now too: **Enter** keeps the text and closes the field, **Esc** puts the text back to how it was when you clicked into it, and **Shift+Enter** starts a new line (before, Enter did that, and there was no way back short of undo).
