- **The note button names its key:** The tooltip of the note button in the toolbar named no key, although **N** places a note; it now reads "Note (N)", like the tool buttons.
- **Zoom keys in the sketch:** The 3D view zooms with **+** and **−**, the sketch only with the wheel and its buttons. Now **+** and **−** zoom the sketch too, by the same step as in the 3D view; with Ctrl or Cmd they stay the browser's page zoom. The sketch's zoom buttons say so in their tooltip, the shortcuts window lists the keys under the sketch, and the guide mentions them for the sketch and for the 3D view, where they were missing too.
- **Every button with a key says so:** Some buttons named no key in their tooltip although they have one. They now do, like the tool buttons: Import (Ctrl+I) and Export (Ctrl+E) in the toolbar; Home (F), Zoom in (+) and Zoom out (−) beside the view cube, where Home and the zoom buttons had no tooltip at all; Zoom in (+) and Zoom out (−) in the sketch; Undo (Ctrl+Z) and Redo (Ctrl+Y) in the sketch; and the lock (Ctrl+L) and hide (Ctrl+H) icons in the properties panel, which had no tooltip either.

## 1.67.4

- **SVG files of one page line up (#258):** Every imported SVG was centred on its own drawing, so the layers of one document exported as separate files - an orange fill, its black outline, a white part and its outline - drifted apart when the pieces had different extents; Tinkercad keeps each file's place on the page. The import window has a new box, **SVG: keep the place on the drawing page**: with it, each drawing sits where it was drawn on its page (the viewBox, or a width and height in pixels), and files of one page meet again, even for the four files of the report, whose pairs now lie on top of each other. It is off by default, so a single drawing still comes in centred, and it remembers its setting. A drawing that states no page is centred as usual, and the import says so. Through MCP, `layerling_import_file` takes `keepPagePosition`. Reported by @stainlessdmc12.
- **Corner and line tools on a mixed sketch selection (#283):** A frame round a shape, or a click inside it, selects its corners and its lines together, and then the sketch offered neither **Round corner** nor **Chamfer corner**, and **Curve line** only ever worked on one line. Now the bar shows the corner tools for the selected corners whatever lines come with them, and **Curve line** and **Straight line** act on all the selected lines at once, in one undo step. A line selection alone works the same, so a whole outline can be curved in one click. Suggested by @lukyanenkomax.
- **Hollowing an imported drawing, and an honest message when it fails:** An imported drawing of a house, traced from a picture, could not be hollowed at any wall thickness, and the message blamed the wall ("The walls cannot be this thick", also at 0.2 mm). Two things were behind it: the mesh had come out of the conversion with a flat sliver of a face, as long as a finger and thinner than a hair, which the CAD kernel cannot hollow around, and rounded inner corners fail on an outline of very many short straight pieces. Slivers like that are now taken out before hollowing, and when the rounded inside cannot be built the hollow body gets sharp inner corners instead. And when a body still cannot be hollowed, a very thin wall is tried: only if that works was the wall too thick; otherwise the message says the body cannot be hollowed at all and names a likely cause, such as a cutout that touches the outer edge of an imported shape. Found in a forum report.

## 1.67.3

- **The guide follows Graphite (#278):** With the Graphite theme chosen, the user guide still showed the brown dark theme, because its pages only knew light and dark. They now have the grey palette too, worked out from the dark one the way the program does it, with the orange accent kept, and a test keeps the two in step. Reported by @AkiraNorthstar.
- **Polygon and star in the sketch shapes (#280):** The sketch shapes menu has two new shapes, a **Polygon** and a **Star**, and two number fields at the bottom of the menu: the number of sides or points (3 to 64, 5 at first) and, for the star, how deep its valleys reach in (10 to 90 percent of the outer radius, 40 at first). The values apply to the next shape you add, by click or by dragging it onto the sketch, and afterwards the shape is made of ordinary corner points like every other sketch shape, so you can move, round or chamfer them. The guide's line about the sketch shapes said to drag a frame, which was long gone; it now says what the menu does. Suggested by @lukyanenkomax.
- **Search in the user guide (#279):** Every guide page has a search box in its header. Type a word or two and it lists the sections that contain all of them, chapter and heading first and a snippet with the matching words marked; a click, or Enter for the first, jumps straight to the section. It ignores capital letters and accents, takes "ss" and "ß" for each other whichever is typed, finds the start of a word ("fas" finds "fase"), ranks a word in a heading above one in the text, and works in every language the guide is built in, including a language's chapters that still show English text. The guide build writes a small index per language (about 175 KB English, 200 KB German), which the page fetches only when the box is used. Suggested by @danyvanimpe-lab.
## 1.67.2

- **A default theme for your server (#278):** `LAYERLING_DEFAULT_THEME` (`system`, `light`, `dark` or `graphite`) sets the colour theme people get on their first visit, for Docker and `npm run start`, set like `LAYERLING_SHARED_PROJECTS_DIR`. Browsers that already have a theme keep it, anyone can still change it, and without the setting the theme follows the system. A copy served as plain files has no server to read it, so it does not apply there. The page asks a small route, `/api/server-defaults`, once on a first visit.
- **Herringbone gear picture (#277):** The herringbone gear's button in the inspector showed a black square, because the picture for the new gear type had not been added. It is rendered from the real geometry now, and a test fails if a gear type is added without one.
- **Ready for more languages (#141):** The language choice is a list that opens, like the colour theme's, with each language named in its own tongue, instead of two buttons with flags, so any number of languages fit. What belongs to a language - its name, how it writes numbers, its quotation marks, its community and README, the words of its guide pages - now sits in one table (`languages.ts`) instead of in a dozen places that only knew German and English. A language that is not built in is fetched only when somebody picks it, so a further language adds nothing to the page everybody loads. Sentences a language has not translated yet show in English, and so do its guide chapters, which are then built from the English ones with a note at the top, and its what's-new texts. `npm run i18n:status` shows where each language stands, and tests hold every language to the rules (no sentence without an English original, matching placeholders). `docs/TRANSLATING.md` says how to add one.
- **Workplane on the cut of the section view (#154):** The section view only clipped the model, so there was no face on the cut for the workplane tool to land on, and a sketch could not start exactly there. A new button in the section panel, **Workplane here**, puts the workplane on the cut: on the plane of the section, facing out of the body that stays. The sketch then starts there and shows the outline of the cut, which you draw along and snap to. The MCP action `layerling_set_workplane` has `onSection: true` for it. Asked for by jrs94yj on GitHub.
## 1.67.1

- **Wrap around a cylinder: the diameter in tenths of a millimetre (#274):** The diameter of the cylinder wall moved and read in whole millimetres, so a cup of 63.5 mm could not be set. The slider now steps by 0.1 mm and the field shows and takes the decimals. Asked for by Bitt-Cloud on GitHub.
- **Loft: smooth transition (#271):** A transition between two outlines always ran in straight lines from bottom to top, like a cone, but a hose adapter that flares from a narrow to a wide pipe, as drawn by hose-adapter generators, runs in an S-curve. A new slider, **Smooth transition**, blends between the two: 0 is the straight transition as before, 100 lets the sides leave both ends straight up and change their size in between. It works with the wall (open at both ends), the twist, the tilt and the offset, and stays an exact body, built through sections along the same curve as the display, so the edge tool and the STEP export work on it. The MCP bridge takes `loftSmooth` too. Asked for by whanner11-cpu on GitHub.
## 1.67.0

- **SVG import says what it left out (#258):** An SVG shape that does not build into a solid, such as an outline crossing itself or touching itself at a point, was dropped without a word, so a drawing came in with a gap nobody could explain. The import message now says how many shapes of the file were left out and what to try. The guide has it under adding geometry. Follows from the closing sliver found by stainlessdmc12.
- **SVG import: a letter closing a hair beside its start (#258):** A traced logo (letters F, T and L from Affinity) came in without its T. The program writes every number with three decimals, so the T's outline ended 0.001 beside where it began, and the closing edge between the two was a sliver the extrusion could not triangulate. The check for a solid threw the whole letter out without a word, and a file in which every shape ended like that was refused as having "no filled contours". A last point within a hundred-thousandth of the drawing's size of the first is now taken as the first. Reported by stainlessdmc12 on GitHub.
- **Guide: hollow bodies that end in a point (#263):** Hollow needs a flat face to leave open, and a closed cavity fails at a sharp tip too, as on a cone or a revolved triangle. The guide now says so under Hollowing and shows the way that works: draw the wall into the profile before revolving. Asked about by danyvanimpe-lab on GitHub.
- **Herringbone gear (#265):** A new gear type, the herringbone gear: two helical halves turning opposite ways, like an arrow. It centres itself on its shaft and has no axial thrust, which suits 3D printing, where a helical gear pushes sideways. It takes the helix angle and the quality of the helical gear, the angle applying to each half, and the teeth turn exactly at the middle ring. It stays a mesh for the edge tool and the STEP export. The gear list, the workspace defaults and the MCP bridge (`gearType: "herringbone"`) know it, and the command search finds it as "herringbone" and "Pfeilrad". Asked for on GitHub.
- **Threads: play along the axis (#256):** The clearance of a nut or tapped hole works on the diameter and opens the flanks of a V thread to only about 0.58 of its value, so a vertical gap between the flanks took a wide diameter and brought a sideways slop; printing a pair worked at 0.5 mm but rattled. A new value, **Play along the axis**, makes every tooth of the part thinner along the axis and leaves the diameter alone, so the two can be set apart: 0.2 mm in the diameter and 0.2 mm along the axis give a gap of 0.32 mm between the flanks with only 0.1 mm of air to each side. It is 0 for every saved thread, goes up to a fifth of the pitch, exists for nut and tapped hole, and reaches the display, the edge tool and the STEP export (a Whitworth thread with play goes there as straight pieces instead of the true arcs). The guide has a new section on threads for 3D printing, with the consequences and tips. Workspace defaults and the MCP bridge (`threadAxialClearance`) take it too. Asked for by hlmodtech on GitHub.
- **Sketch: shapes pushed over each other (#239):** Two closed shapes pushed over each other showed a hole where they overlap, while the body became their union, and dropping them made a shared point where their lines cross, which welded the shapes together so they could not be pulled apart again and left stray edges behind. Now the sketch shows what the body will be: a hole only where an outline lies wholly inside another. And moving shapes or points makes no shared points where lines cross; drawing a line across others still does. Reported by DrJones in the forum.
- **Hole pattern: the wall between the holes (#239):** The hole pattern showed only the spacing centre to centre, but what a print needs to know is the wall left between two holes. A new slider, **Wall between the holes**, shows it and sets it: it is the least distance between a hole and its neighbours, for every hole shape and both arrangements, and the spacing follows it and the other way round. Asked for by luk-saw on GitHub.
- **Rounded box: quality 1 is a true chamfer with rounded top and bottom edges too (#239):** The rounding of a rounded box with its top and bottom edges rounded was built from two faces meeting at each edge, so it always had twice the pieces of the quality: quality 1 gave two, no chamfer. Quality 1 now gives one flat 45 degree piece on all twelve edges and a triangle on each corner, as it does for the corners alone. The edge tool takes such a box as a mesh, as the exact round would not match what the view shows. Higher qualities are unchanged. Found by luk-saw on GitHub.
- **T toggles transparency (#276):** The T key makes the selected bodies see-through, and opaque again on the next press. If any selected body is already see-through, all become opaque, otherwise all become see-through, so a mixed selection goes opaque first. Holes and locked bodies stay as they are. Asked for by foofoodog on GitHub.
- **Sketch editor: Shift+right drag pans, the snap grid control sits over the plate:** Like in the 3D editor, the view of the sketch pans with Shift plus the right mouse button, next to the middle button and Ctrl/Cmd plus right. The snap grid control now floats over the sketch plate instead of having a strip of its own. By gogades (PR #269).
- **Torus: sides and steps (#272):** The torus drew its ring and its tube with fixed numbers of corners (144 and 36), so a big one, 200 mm across, showed facets and there was no way to change it. It has two sliders now, **Ring sides** around the ring and **Tube steps** around the tube. Left alone they follow the size, never coarser than before, so a large torus comes out smooth; held, they work like the side count of a cylinder: a torus of few sides is drawn faceted, in the view, in the export and for the edge tool. The workspace defaults for the torus and the MCP bridge (`sides`, `steps`) take them as well. Reported by micro4rgb-maker on GitHub.
## 1.66.7

- **Put a design on the server from its menu:** The menu of a design card in this browser has a new entry, **Put on the server**, for the shared folder you have open, as dragging the card onto the server tile does. Asked for by ucito in the forum, who moved a whole backup over.
- **Server designs: renaming and deleting (#265):** A design in the server folder is shown under its file name, but renaming it in the editor only changed the name inside the design, so the overview kept the old one. Renaming now renames the file on the server too, in both the Node and the PHP store (a new `renameFileTo`), and the working copy follows. And a design or folder deleted on the server took only the file with it: the working copy in the browser was let go of its binding and turned up again among the browser designs, without a thumbnail. It is removed with the file now. A design exported to the server under another name carries that name inside as well, so it no longer opens under the old one. Reported by ucito on GitHub.
## 1.66.6

- **Mirror in the right-click menu:** Right-click a selection and choose **Mirror**, as well as pressing M or using the ribbon: the three arrows for left-right, front-back and top-bottom appear. Asked for in a discussion by someone who could not find the tool.
- **See-through bodies no longer fade away behind the plate's middle:** A body shown see-through that stood in the back half of the plate, seen from the camera, could turn into a faint ghost, depending on the camera's height and while it was moved. The plate is see-through too, and the view draws such surfaces by the distance of their centres; behind the plate's centre the body came first and the plate was laid over it. The plate is now always drawn first, and the section plane always after the bodies, so neither changes with the view. Reported by DrJones in the forum.
- **Chamfer, bottom end shows up (#269):** 1.66.2 gave every end of a rod, nut or tapped hole a chamfer of its own, but the field for the bottom end never appeared in the thread's settings, because the list of fields shown for a thread did not know it. It is there now, as **Chamfer, bottom end**. A screw still has its one chamfer at the tip. Reported by DrJones and PePa in the forum.
- **Torus symbol:** the picture of the torus in the shapes menu showed SketchForge's flat ring with a square profile. It is now rendered from the real geometry, a donut with a round profile. Reported by DrJones in the forum.

- **Chamfer and fillet show the edge that does not fit:** When a selection fails with "The selected edges cannot be filleted (chamfered) together at this size", the edge you clicked that broke a selection that worked is now drawn in red and twice as wide, so you know which one to take out. It stays the one shown while you click more edges on top. Without an earlier selection that worked - the first click, Select all, or a size you raised - the worker tries each selected edge of the failing body on its own and marks those that fail alone, or all of them when only their combination fails. That search stops after 1.5 s and then marks all of them: on a dense mesh one edge alone takes about half a second, and a refusal must not hold up the next value while the size is dragged. On a short screen the panel scrolls down to the message, which sat under its buttons before. A chamfer at an angle other than 45° now gets the same message instead of the kernel's raw error.

- **Only visible edges:** The chamfer and fillet panel has a new switch, **Only visible edges**, off by default. Until now every edge was drawn through the body, like an X-ray, which made a busy part hard to read. With the switch on, the edges behind the body from the current view are hidden and cannot be clicked; turning the view brings them back. An edge marked red always shows.

- **The edge under the pointer is drawn wider:** In the chamfer and fillet tool, the edge a click would pick is now drawn twice as wide as the others, besides its lighter colour.

## 1.66.5

- **A size typed for several bodies changes all of them (#259):** With two or more bodies selected, a number typed into the width, depth or height label (or the pair of a corner) only changed the first body, and a typed height above the plate only the last one. The label shows the size of the whole selection, so the number now sets that: every body is scaled with it and moved with it, as when the handle is dragged. Reported on GitHub.
- **Rotate handles in perspective:** the three curved arrows are now pieces of the circle their axis turns on, drawn in space like the arrows in Tinkercad: they sit on the selection in perspective and move with the view. Each carries the colour of its axis (X red, Y green, Z blue, as in the axis arrows on the plate), has a light edge so it reads on the grid, and gets heavier on hover.
- **The rotation circle snaps to the ticks it shows:** the circle drew a tick every 22.5 degrees but only snapped every 45, so 22.5 needed Shift. Now it snaps to every tick; Shift adds finer ticks every 11.25 degrees. Outside the circle nothing changes (1 degree, Shift 45). The `R` key still turns by 45.
- **A 45 degree chamfer is no overhang (#269):** *Show overhangs* marks faces that lean out further than the set angle. A face exactly on the angle, a 45 degree chamfer at the default, was marked or not marked at random, because the float noise of its mesh decided. The limit now lies a hair above the angle (about 0.4 degrees), so a face on the angle is left alone and anything steeper is still marked. Reported by DrJones in the forum.
- **STL into your own folder:** In Chrome and Edge, the first STL export of a design now opens the system's save dialog, so you choose the folder and the name. Later STL exports of the design write into that same file without asking, and a message says so; **Save as…** in the export window picks another file. The dialog remembers its own folder, apart from the one for designs. Firefox and Safari still download the file.

## 1.66.4

- **Selecting a body above the horizon (#269):** Looking at the plate flat from the side, a body could only be selected by clicking its lowest part, below the horizon line. Above it the click ray never reaches the workplane, and the code that starts a drag gave up before it had selected the body. A click on the body now selects it wherever it is; there is just nothing to drag from when the ray misses the workplane. Reported by DrJones in the forum.

## 1.66.3

- **Holes have the size you typed (#254):** A hole cut 0.1 mm wider than its set size (a 7.9 mm hole came out at 8.0 mm, a 6.0 mm one at 6.1 mm), because the cutter was padded by 0.05 mm on every side to keep thin skins away. The padding now stays only at the two ends of the hole, where it keeps a hole that ends flush with a face from leaving a skin; width and depth are as typed, to within a thousandth of a millimetre, which also keeps a hole that touches a wall along a line from taking a slab out of it. Press fits work with the size you enter. Reported by Mondo-chumstyle.

## 1.66.2

- **Thread chamfers (#269):** A nut or tapped hole now has its chamfer cone run on, at 45 degrees, down to the thread core: before, it ended on a ledge at the outer diameter, below which the thread stood at full height. The chamfer still means the room the mouth opens beyond the outer diameter, so existing mouths keep their size. Every end of a rod, nut or tapped hole also has a chamfer of its own now (**Chamfer, top end** and **Chamfer, bottom end**; a screw keeps its one at the tip), and `threadChamferBottom` is there for an AI client. Switching a thread between **Threaded rod** and **Tapped hole** keeps its length instead of going back to the standard height. Reported by DrJones in the forum.

- **Round or chamfer several sketch corners at once (#269):** Select the corners with Shift or a frame, choose **Round corner** or **Chamfer corner** and give one radius or distance for all of them. Corners that share a side take at most 45 % of it each, so a rectangle comes out alike all round. Reported by DrJones in the forum.

- **More room in a new nut or tapped hole:** The clearance of a new nut or tapped hole now starts at 0.4 mm instead of 0.2 mm. The gap between the flanks of a V thread is only about half of the clearance across the diameter, so 0.2 mm left 0.05 mm and a printed screw and nut did not turn (seen in a video by HL ModTech). Designs you already made keep their value, and so do the defaults you set in the workspace settings. The two clearance fields of the thread now carry a hint: 0.3 tight, 0.4 usual, 0.5 loose, and print a test pair first.

- **Mismatched program files are noticed:** The page and its two CAD workers (edge tool, sketch bodies) are separate files that load at different times. Each worker now says which build it is from the moment it starts, and the page compares that with its own. If they differ - after an update, or because the browser handed out a stored copy of an older file - the same card as for an update appears ("Part of this page is from another version") and offers **Save and reload**. The build id is set per static export (`LAYERLING_BUILD_ID` in scripts/build-static-export.mjs) and is the version number everywhere else. Suggested by Stefan after #248.

## 1.66.1

- **Notice of an update while the page is open:** On the published program (layerling.com, any web server with the static export) an open page now notices that a new version was installed in the meantime. Every export carries a `version.json` with a build id; the page asks for it every five minutes while visible, when the tab comes to the front, when the connection returns, and at once when a program file or worker it needs fails to load. On a change a card at the bottom right offers **Save and reload** (the open design is written to the browser first and reopens as it was) or **Later** (15 minutes), and lists the new highlights. Until now an old page kept running on its own files and could meet parts of the new version that did not fit (#88).

- **Chamfer and fillet after Split:** On a body that was cut with the Split tool, the edges of the new cut face could not be rounded or chamfered, whatever the size ("cannot be filleted together at this size"). The cut leaves the flat sides of the body as many tiny triangles that are flat only to a millionth of a millimetre, and the CAD kernel then kept one side in several pieces and failed at the seams between them. The edge tool now gathers the triangles of a flat face back into one face and builds the corners exactly, so the edges of a cut corner can be treated like any other. Found by @lukyanenkomax in #248.

## 1.66.0

- **Save into your own folder:** In Chrome and Edge, saving a design as LYL now opens the system's save dialog, so you choose the folder and the name and can replace an existing file. After that **Ctrl+S** saves the design into the same file again, and a design opened from a `.lyl` file saves back into it. Firefox and Safari do not let a page write into files; there the file is still downloaded. Suggested by @boatingman44 in #251.

- **Delete history:** The History view has a new button at the bottom left, **Delete history**. After a question it removes every earlier step for good; the design keeps the state it has now, Undo has nothing to go back to and a file saved afterwards carries no history. It is alone and in a different colour so it is not pressed by accident. For an AI client there is `layerling_clear_history` (it needs `confirm: true`). Suggested by @prmod3d in #246.

- **Newer CAD kernel:** The CAD kernel (occt-wasm) is now 5.7.0 instead of 5.4.0 and the 2D geometry library (manifold-3d) 3.5.4: fewer redundant steps in boolean operations, a fix for general transforms that applied a placement twice, and the joint type of 2D offsets now passed on correctly. All tests pass and the shapes behave as before; nothing changes in how you work.

- **Quick guide, welcome text and README brought up to date:** The quick guide gained entries for Hollow, text with name tags and icon tags, construction lines and the B and Shift+B keys; it now mentions Undo an edge feature and Edit, the toolbar size and the grid-line switch under Settings, the language buttons at the top right, and the None stop and the file name when exporting a LYL. The welcome text and the README say that the AI connection needs a copy you run yourself and that the editor can open itself, and the guide chapter on measuring says that the tape measure and the framing square stay while you sketch.

## 1.65.0

- **A design file without its undo steps:** The slider "Saved action history" in the LYL export stopped at 30 actions. It has a last stop, **None**, now: the file then holds only the current state - for sharing a design on Printables or Makerworld without its whole history. The export is a copy under the file name you enter, so the design in your browser keeps its history. Suggested by @prmod3d in #246.

- **MCP: the AI opens the editor itself:** Until now an editor tab had to be open before an AI client could do anything. When none is, the first tool that needs one now starts your default browser with an editor and waits until it has connected (up to 45 s); the new tool `layerling_open_editor` does the same on purpose, for another tab. It works for a layerling on your own computer; a copy on a NAS or server cannot start your browser, and `LAYERLING_MCP_OPEN_EDITOR=0` for the MCP server switches it off. Suggested by @brauwers1981 in #250.

- **The corner ruler and the tape measure stay when you sketch:** A corner ruler or a tape measure that you had set was gone after a sketch - the 3D view is taken down while you draw and built again afterwards, and the rulers went with it. They now stay where they were until you reload the page. As before they are not saved with the design and not in the history, and a different design starts without them. Reported in the forum by yardi.

- **Chamfer and fillet on the wider layers of a name tag:** The rim and the plate of a name tag (and any text with a **Wider** fill) were built for the edge tool from hundreds of short straight pieces, the polygon outline the display uses. The tool then refused them or gave a body that was not valid - a fillet on a plate came out with more volume than before. Now the kernel moves the letters' own outlines out itself, so a wall is one plane, one arc or one curve, and the top edges of the rim and the plate take a fillet or chamfer of 0.2 to 0.3 mm in most words (the plate of "Name" has 81 edges to round, not 660). Slivers the kernel leaves at sharp inside corners are taken out first. Where an outline is still too fine for the kernel - a very small counter, a very narrow stroke - the usual message stays; a key ring tab, bevelled corners and a stroke along the outline keep the polygon outline. A treated body is also stored without its display mesh now, which halves its size and lets a second edge treatment on it start again. Reported by @plazmabokor in #215 and #225.

- **MCP: `layerling_layer_icon` for the icon tag:** An AI client can now make what the Icon tag in the shape library makes. `svg` (the text of an SVG file) puts a new icon tag on the plate, `id` turns an extruded sketch body into one or builds an icon tag again; `width` is the icon's width, `layers` the layers as for `layerling_layer_text` (without letter edge and key ring hole), and `count` lays copies out on the plate. The guide (chapter 14) and both skills list it.

## 1.64.0

- **Undo says when it brings a workplane back:** Setting a workplane on a face or putting it back on the base plate is a step of its own in the history, and most tools in Modify change nothing when they are only opened. So an undo after opening one took back that older step and a workplane seemed to appear out of nowhere. The message after Undo and Redo now says when the workplane changed, e.g. "Undo - the workplane is back on a face". Reported by @prmod3d in #246.

- **The Hollow tool explains its "Inner edges" switch:** Rounded and Sharp only differ where the inner walls move apart, at a step or around an opening, so the cavity of a plain box looked the same with both and seemed to ignore the switch. A note under the switch now says so and how to get a rounded inside (round the outside edges first, then hollow), and the guide says the same. Reported by @slammerfox in #245.

- **Ctrl+Z works while a slider has the focus:** After moving a slider in a properties window (taper, size, angle ...) the slider kept the focus, and Ctrl+Z only said "Finish the current drag or transform before undoing" until you clicked into the workplane. Now Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Y on a slider, checkbox, list or colour field leave it and undo or redo the last step straight away; in a text or number field they still undo the typing. Suggested by @kwjaarsveld-star in #243.

- **The icon tag, an SVG icon as a layered tag:** The shape library has an **Icon tag** entry after the name tag. It opens a small window: **Browse...** for an SVG file, a picture of it, the icon's **Width (mm)** and **OK**, and the icon stands in the middle of the plate in the name tag's layers - the icon on top, a 1.5 mm rim, a 3 mm plate without holes. An imported SVG or a drawn sketch body has **Make layers** at the top of its settings too. The **Icon tag** card has **Another SVG...**, which puts another file's icon on the tag and keeps its layers and width, the icon's **Width**, the layer rows of the name tag (colour, Wider by, Corners, Height, No holes, + and -) and a **Count** of 1 to 100 with **As many as fit**, which works it out from the plate of the chosen printer; the copies are laid out on the middle of the plate, 5 mm apart. Each layer is a sketch body in the sketch's own fill modes, so the bundle builds again on every change. **No holes** on a wider sketch line now also closes the pockets that form between two parts once they touch, as it does for a text. No key ring hole and no MCP action yet. Suggested by @plazmabokor in #225.

## 1.63.0

- **Toolbar size:** A new setting under Settings → Appearance, **Toolbar size**, makes the ribbon on top and the camera controls on the left **Small** (75 %), **Medium** (as before) or **Large** (125 %) - room for the workplane on a laptop, bigger targets on a large screen. The existing icons are scaled by the browser through one CSS value, `--toolbar-scale`, so no icon was redrawn; the tool row grows or shrinks with them and the workplane moves along. With large buttons the ribbon's groups wrap onto a second row whenever the window is too narrow, at any width. The size is kept in the browser (`layerling.toolbarSize`) for every design, and applied before the page is drawn so the bar does not jump. The shape menu of the 3D editor scales with the bar, zoomed as a whole, and shows 5, 4 or 3 columns (Medium had 3 before); the other menus and pop-out panels, and the sketch editor's shape menu, keep their size.

- **Rounded box with quality 1 gives chamfered corners:** The Quality of a rounded box can go down to **1** (it started at 4): the corner is then one straight cut instead of an arc - a chamfer on the four upright edges, and a single flat piece per corner when the top and bottom are rounded too. The MCP bridge takes 1 as well. Suggested in #239.

- **Grid lines can stay thin when zooming:** The fine and darker lines of the grid are drawn twice - as a one-pixel line and as a band on the plate that grows as you zoom in, as in Tinkercad (#143) - so zoomed in they get thick. A new switch under Settings → Workplane, **Grid lines grow when zooming**, turns the bands off: the lines then stay one pixel wide at every zoom, as in the sketch. It is on by default. Suggested in #240.

- **Shortcuts in the tooltips, and B for the fillet:** The tooltip of a tool in the ribbon now shows its keyboard shortcut - "Mirror (M)", "Group (Ctrl+G)" - where it has one, from the same list the command search (Ctrl+K) shows. The fillet and the chamfer had none: **B** opens the fillet and **Shift+B** the chamfer for the selected body, and pressing it again closes the tool. They are in the shortcut list and the guide. Suggested in #242.

## 1.62.3

- **Rounded corners of a sketch come out as true arcs:** A corner rounded in the sketch is stored as a curve, and the exact body built a free-form surface from it, which the viewer meshed unevenly - the wall showed patches of shading and a net of stray triangles. A curve that is a circular arc (every corner the fillet draws, and a circle drawn as curves) now goes to the kernel as an arc and becomes a plain cylinder wall: clean shading, fewer triangles, and an exact shape for the edge tool. Reported in the drucktipps3d forum.

- **Stroked and widened sketches are built exactly:** A sketch drawn as a line (inside, outside or centred) or filled "Wider" had its outline worked out as hundreds of short straight pieces - a rounded corner became a net of strips, the walls showed stripes and the edge tool could not work on the result. The kernel now moves the outlines itself: a line stays a plane, an arc a cylinder, a curve one smooth face, with a few dozen faces instead of hundreds, and fillet and chamfer work on the top edges. Where the kernel cannot do it - open lines, bevelled corners, a very pointed miter corner, a stroke wider than the outline - the old way builds the body as before.

- **Tidier hole pattern icon and letter edge row:** The hole pattern icon in "Adjust" has no dark fill any more and its hexagonal holes sit on one even grid, with the same distance to each other and to the plate edge. On the name tag card the "Letter edge" row gives its three buttons equal width and room.

## 1.62.2

- **Taper is back for a plain text:** 1.62.0 hid the Taper and Twist & Lean cards for every text, but they were meant to go only for name tags, where they only distort the letters. A plain text shows them again; a name tag, a stack of layers, still has none. Reported by @Hns-Rtgrs in #238.
- **The Hollow tool shows Revert and Edit too:** The list of what a body already has - **Revert edge treatment** with **Edit** next to each fillet, chamfer or hollowing - was only in the Fillet and Chamfer tool. Whoever opened Hollow to change a hollowing found no button and no way back. The Hollow window now shows the same list, and Edit on a hollowing opens it again with the old wall, openings and inner edges. Reported by @AkiraNorthstar in #237.
- **A rounded or chamfered edge on the letters of a name tag:** The name tag card has a new row, **Letter edge**: None, Round or Chamfer, with a size (0.4 mm to start with, at most as high as the layer), for the letters on the top layer - the finish of a printed tag, which until now meant opening the bundle and using the edge tool on the letters. It is built from the outline of the letters stepped inwards by the 2D kernel and joined level by level into one smooth skin, so it works for every font and word, where the edge tool's fillet failed on some names (it stops on "Name" at 0.3 mm, on "Oga" at 0.4 mm). Where letters are thinner than twice the edge the skin cannot be joined and slabs take its place, which always stand. The edge belongs to the text, so words, font and letter size can still be changed and a name list gives every tag its edge. It is a mesh in the file and the exports (`textEdge`, `{ kind, size }`, on the shape and as `edge` on a layer); the edge tool takes such a text as a mesh and stops above its triangle limit. The MCP bridge takes `layers[].edge` in `layerling_layer_text` and `letterEdge` in `layerling_update_object`. Suggested by @plazmabokor in #225.
- **A stretched bundle of turned parts cuts where it stands:** A bundle whose parts lie turned - bars on their side - and that was then made longer cut nothing, or in the wrong place, once it was grouped with a body: the stretch went to each part's width instead of the length that really runs along the bundle, so the bars grew fat instead of long. Grouping and Ungroup now stretch every part along its own axes. The picture in the editor was right all along. Reported by @danyvanimpe-lab in #234.
- **The bug report is a ZIP:** "Bug report" in the footer saved a `.lyl`, and GitHub does not accept that ending, so the file could not be attached to an issue or a discussion. It now saves a ZIP with the design as a `.lyl` and the text with the version, browser and last messages next to it. The start page opens the ZIP like a backup, or you unpack it and open the `.lyl`; the bug report form on GitHub points to it. Suggested after a report in #234 could not attach its file.

## 1.62.1

- **The key ring tab as a printed one:** The tab of a name tag's key ring hole is now 6.5 mm wide round a 3 mm hole - a 1.75 mm wall either side - and sticks out 6.75 mm, the hole's edge 2 mm beyond the plate; the hole is 3 mm by default (it was 1.85 mm in a 4.34 mm tab, 2.5 mm clear). The tab still grows with the hole. **Make layers** on a plain text now starts a name tag at 9 mm letters too, as the shape library's Name tag does. Measured on a printed tag by @plazmabokor in #215.
- **Shift with an arrow key follows the darker grid line:** Shift and an arrow key moved a selection by five grid steps, fixed. It now moves as many steps as the setting **Darker grid line every** says: 5 by default, so nothing changes, and 10 as in Tinkercad. It holds in the editor and in the sketch; in inches, where the setting is not offered, it stays 5. Asked for by @prmod3d in #222.
- **Align follows a workplane:** A shape turned at an angle, say 45°, was aligned by the box round it, not by its own edges, and a workplane set on one of its faces changed nothing. With a workplane set on a face, the align tool now measures and moves along that plane's axes - x along the face, y out of it, z across - and its points stand on them, as in Tinkercad, so a cylinder lines up with the true edge of a bar turned 45°. On the plate, or on a plane that only stands higher with its axes where the plate's are, everything is as before. `layerling_align_objects` follows the workplane too. Asked about by @prmod3d in #232.
- **The align handles no longer jump:** With two shapes far apart, resting the pointer on a handle of the align tool, such as the centre of the width, made the handle jump back and forth between two places and the shapes flicker. The handles stood on the box of the shapes as drawn, and while a handle shows its preview the shapes are drawn where they would go: the handle moved away from under the pointer, the preview ended, the handle came back, and so on. The handles now keep the box the shapes had before the preview, so they stay put, and a click aligns as before. Reported by @AkiraNorthstar in #235.

## 1.62.0

- **Construction lines in the sketch:** A line can now be a construction line: drawn dashed, never part of the outline, so it can run as far beyond the shape as you like without making the body bigger, and a revolve may have one right of the axis. The new **Construction line** button in the Draw group makes new lines - typed ones too - construction lines; a selected line has a button to make it one or a normal line again. Points snap to the ends of a construction line and to where it crosses another line (a blue ring shows it), so a corner can go on a middle or at a fixed distance from an edge without calculating. Where a construction line crosses the outline it does not cut it apart. Saved in `.lyl` as an optional `construction` on the line. Constraints were asked for too and are not planned: they need a solver, which layerling does not have. Asked for by @OE3JGW in #229.
- **Change a fillet, chamfer or hollowing later:** The list in the edge tool, **Revert edge treatment**, has an **Edit** button next to every treatment you make from now on. It takes the treatment back like Revert, and opens the tool again at once with the same edges and the old value - change the value, apply it, and a 3 mm fillet is a 5 mm one without picking the edges again; for a hollowing the Hollow tool opens with its wall and openings. Newer treatments on top go with it, as with Revert, and older treatments and single parts of a group offer only Revert. The edges picked are stored with the treatment as `edgeIds`. A variable table was asked for as well and is not planned: layerling is not a parametric program. Asked for by @OE3JGW in #227.
- **Hole pattern for a face:** A new tool, **Hole pattern**, perforates a flat face in one go - for vents, speaker grilles, baskets and enclosures. Select a body and click **Hole pattern** in the **Modify** group of the toolbar, next to Pattern (or right-click the body, or find it with Ctrl+K), click a flat face - the whole flat face around the click counts, a curved one gets a hint instead - and set the hole shape (round, square, rectangle, hexagon or slot), its size, the spacing centre to centre, the arrangement (grid, or staggered, which shifts every second row by half a spacing and packs the rows so each hole has neighbours one spacing away), the margin to the face's edge and the depth (through the whole body by default, or a set depth). The holes show as ghosts on the face while the numbers change; **Create** adds them as ordinary hole shapes grouped with the body, nothing baked in: ungrouping frees them again, and the whole thing is one history step. Only holes that lie wholly inside the face minus the margin are made, and the margin also holds around openings in the face; up to 400 holes at once. The window moves by its title bar like the other tool windows and has a question mark that opens the new guide section. The calculation is a plain function (`holePattern.ts`) with unit tests for rectangular and L-shaped faces. The MCP bridge gets the same action, `perforate_face` (tool `layerling_perforate_face`: the body with `targetFace`, or `point` and `normal`, plus `form`, `size`, `height`, `spacing`, `arrangement`, `margin` and `depth`). Asked for by @danyvanimpe-lab in #223.
- **The Honeycomb shape is gone:** The hole pattern tool does what the Honeycomb was used for, so it is no longer in the shape menu, the guide or the MCP tools (`layerling_create_shape` refuses it and points to `layerling_perforate_face`). A honeycomb in an existing design becomes a box with hexagonal holes - the same cell size, wall and frame, grouped - when the design is opened, with a note, and Undo can bring the old one back (it is not converted a second time). Designs saved earlier, in the browser or as files, open as before; a honeycomb that was itself a hole, and one inside a group, stay as they were and still draw. Asked for by @danyvanimpe-lab in #223.
- **A simpler name tag panel, with a key ring hole:** The Layers card was hard to find and to see through. The shape library now has a **Name tag** entry after Text: one click places "Name" as white letters on a red rim and a dark plate, and a plain text has **Make layers** at the top of its settings. A name tag opens with a **Name tag** card: text, font (fonts of one's own too) and a **letter size** - the height of the capitals seen from above, for all layers at once and kept through new words and fonts - then one row per layer with its colour, how much wider it is, its **corners** (round, bevel or sharp), its height and **No holes**, **+** and **–** adding and removing layers at the bottom (two to six). A **key ring hole**, off by default, puts a straight tab with a round end on the bottom layer, its hole 2.5 mm clear of the plate and through every layer it touches, on the left, right or top. **No holes** now also closes the pockets that form between two letters once their wider outlines touch. The name list says how many tags it makes ("Make 12 name tags"). The fill of a plain text is one list as in Tinkercad - **Filled**, **Outline**, **Outer line**, **Inner line** - with the line width and corners under it, and a plain text has the same **letter size** above **Height**, which now says it is the thickness. `layerling_layer_text` takes `keyring` and a `join` per layer. Suggested and built by @plazmabokor in #215. **Taper** and **Twist & Lean** no longer show for a text or a name tag, where they only distorted the letters.
- **The size fields follow the way a turned body lies:** Lay a roof on its side and its "Height" field still meant the roof's own height, which then ran sideways - to make it 34 mm tall you had to type 34 into "Length" and the 70 mm into "Height". A body turned by right angles (laid flat, stood on its end, turned a quarter) now names its fields by the direction each measure runs in: **Height** is what goes up, **Width** runs along X, **Length** along Y, in the usual order, and each still writes the same measure of the body. Typing a height into a body lying down changes the vertical measure and its underside stays on the plate. A body tilted at some other angle keeps its own measures, with a note under the fields. The handles and the boxes drawn at them were not at fault and are unchanged. Reported by @slammerfox in #230.
- **A revolve sketch shows the bodies on the workplane you set:** With a workplane on a face, the sketch for an extrusion showed the bodies as seen from that plane, but the sketch for a revolve still showed them from the base plate, as they were created - a body laid flat or a face chosen for a bottle or knob profile did not match what was on screen. The revolve sketch now shows the same view as the extrusion sketch, including the cut outline of a workplane inside a body. A body that lies tilted on the base plate - laid flat, or stood on an edge - was drawn in both sketches as it was made, with its width and depth as created; it now shows as it sits there. Reported by @slammerfox in #231.

## 1.61.1

- **STL files can be picked on an iPad:** In the file picker of an iPad or iPhone the STL, 3MF, STEP and `.lyl` files were greyed out and could not be selected, while ZIP files worked: iOS greys out every ending it does not know as a type, and the picker asked for exactly those. On these devices the pickers now show all files, and layerling checks what it is given, as it always did; elsewhere the list narrows the picker as before. Not tried on an iPad yet. Reported by @andrej-cds in #219.
- **The start page's import tile shows the editor's import picture:** "Open a design or import geometry" had a different symbol (a sheet with an arrow) than the import button in the editor (a cube with an arrow pointing at it). Both show the editor's picture now.
- **The settings window opens where you left it:** Moved by its title bar, the settings window was remembered, but opened shifted to the left and up by the distance it stands from the corner when centred - so far off the left edge on a wide window that part of it was out of reach, and dragging it back helped only until the next time. The same held for the guide and the shortcuts window. A moved window now opens exactly where it was dropped. Reported by @prmod3d in #219.
- **A design opens in the view you left it in:** The angle, zoom and position of the camera, and whether it was perspective or flat, are kept with the design in the browser and written again a moment after every move of the view, when the page is hidden or closed and when you go Home. Opening the design puts the camera back there; a new design opens in the standard view as before. The view is not part of the undo history and a move of the camera never saves the design. A saved `.lyl` file carries it as optional `editor.view`, so it opens in it elsewhere; older versions pass over it. Asked for by @prmod3d in #220.
- **`npm install` reports no vulnerabilities:** Next.js 15 ships an old PostCSS, and `npm audit` flagged it as one high and one moderate vulnerability, whose suggested fix `npm audit fix --force` would have moved the app to Next.js 16. `package.json` now pins PostCSS 8.5.28 through `overrides`; Next.js stays at 15.5.27, the audit finds nothing, and typecheck and static export run as before. Reported by @ARSoderberg in #221, who also tested the fix.

## 1.61.0

- **Reference image in sketch mode: turn, centre, calibrate, crop:** A photo laid under a sketch could only be moved and scaled. Its settings now have an **Align** card: a **Rotation** in degrees, with a round handle above the frame (Shift snaps to 15°), so a photo taken at a slant lines up with the revolve axis; **Centre on X = 0** and **Y = 0**, X = 0 being the axis of a revolve; **Calibrate** as in Fusion - click two points in the picture whose distance you know, type the real distance, and the picture scales to match with the first point staying put; and **Crop**, where the frame's handles cut the picture down instead of scaling it, the cut-away part staying stored and coming back on request. The picture keeps its pixels throughout. Asked for by @ucito in #216.
- **The dimension boxes keep the calculation too:** A calculation such as "(140+2)/2" typed into one of the boxes drawn round the selected body - width, depth, height and the height above the plate - was worked out but forgotten; only the fields of the Properties panel kept it (1.59.0). The boxes now keep it as well and open on the formula again, as long as it still gives the size, and the Properties panel shows the same formula for the same value, and the other way round. The corner pair of width and depth does the same, and the guide says where the formulas work. Asked about by @prmod3d in #180.
- **The selection's dashed guides no longer shine through bodies:** Select a body and a dashed footprint with a vertical centre line is drawn at its foot. They were drawn without a depth test, so they showed through the body itself and, depending on the view, over its neighbours - they read as floating centre lines. Bodies now hide them like everything else on the plate. Reported by @prmod3d in #219.
## 1.60.0

- **Fill modes for the Text shape, and "Wider":** A text can now be drawn as sketches and SVGs are since 1.47: **Stroke Outside**, **Inside** or **Centred** with a line width and corner style, and as a **Silhouette** without the counters of its letters. New for text, sketches and SVG alike is the fill mode **Wider**: the filled shape grown outwards by the line width all round, with sharp, round or bevelled corners - the piece that was missing for a filled base layer under a text or logo. A name tag for a multicolour print is three copies of one text: the letters, the same text 1.5 mm wider in a second colour, and 3 mm wider as a silhouette (no holes) carrying the key ring. The box of the text grows with an outside, centred or wider stroke, so the letters keep their size and the layers line up; the letters are exact bodies for the edge tool and STEP in every mode, and letters that run together become one piece. The MCP bridge takes `stroke` (with `align: "grow"`) and `silhouette` on a text as on a sketch body. Asked for by @plazmabokor in #215.
- **Layered text and name tags:** The properties of a text have a **Layers** card: **Split into layers** turns the text into a stack - white letters on top, the same text 1.5 mm wider in red under them, 3 mm wider as a dark plate without holes at the bottom - as a bundle that moves as one, every layer a text of its own with its own colour and height, the way a slicer wants them for a multicolour print. With the stack selected the card edits text, font, the number of layers (up to six) and, per layer, how much wider it is, its height, colour and whether it keeps the holes; every change builds the stack again, and new words run through all layers. The card also takes a **name list**, one name per line, up to a hundred, and makes a tag like the selected text or stack for each, in rows with a chosen gap. The MCP bridge does the same with `layerling_layer_text`. Asked for by @plazmabokor in #215.
- **The static export builds with Next.js 16:** `npm run export` stopped with "This build is using Turbopack, with a `webpack` config and no `turbopack` config" on a machine that had Next.js 16. Next.js 16 builds with Turbopack by default and refuses a `webpack` hook without a `turbopack` entry; `next.config.ts` now carries an empty one. Next.js 15, which `package.json` pins, ignores it for webpack builds. With that fixed, Next.js 16 built with Turbopack, and the export check then failed with "Could not find the generated CAD worker runtime": the CAD worker is bundled by webpack. The export script now builds with `--webpack` when Next.js is 16 or newer (15 does not know the flag). Reported by @ARSoderberg in #217 and #218, who found both fixes.
## 1.59.0

- **A number field remembers its calculation:** Typing "(140+2)/2" into a field left only 71 behind. The field now keeps the calculation with the body: click into it again and the formula is back for editing, as long as the value still equals it - a value changed by a handle, the slider or scaling shows the number again, and a plain number clears the formula. Value and formula change together, so undo takes both back, and the formulas are saved in the design. No variables, no tables. Asked for by @prmod3d in #180.
- **Pen tablets:** The barrel button of a graphics tablet's pen now counts as the right mouse button in the editor: a short press opens the body's menu, and a drag that ends elsewhere still only turns the view. Before, only a mouse opened the menu. Asked about by @whanner11-cpu in #213.
- **Grouping with grouped holes froze the page:** Grouping an imported mesh with a group of holes - three countersunk screws in one group, say - could block the browser for many minutes. The check that counts which triangles a cutter touches asked for the group's bounding box once per sampled point, and each time rebuilt the whole group from its children, thread geometry included. The box is now computed once per cutter. Reported by @MiniCoop8 in #214.

## 1.58.1

- **Ring gear and rack:** The gear has two more types. The **Ring gear** has its teeth pointing in - the outer wheel of a planetary set - and is set by teeth, module and backlash like an involute gear, plus the rim round the teeth; its outside is module × (teeth + 2.5) plus twice the rim, with round teeth module × (teeth + 1.7). A pinion of the same module runs in it at a centre distance of module × (ring teeth − pinion teeth) / 2, which the properties panel gives for the selected pair; ring teeth = sun teeth + 2 × planet teeth makes a planetary set. The **Rack** is the gear of endless radius, its involute flanks straight lines at the pressure angle: a bar as long as teeth × π × module, so racks of one module line up end to end, its depth free; a gear of the same module rolls on it with its centre teeth × module / 2 in front of the pitch line, a module behind the tooth tips. Both take round teeth too, both are exact bodies for the edge tool and STEP, and switching the type keeps the module. The MCP bridge takes `gearType: "internal"` with `gearRim`, and `"rack"`. Promised to @prmod3d and @luk-saw in #201.
## 1.58.0

- **Cancelling a sketch returns to the 3D editor:** Cancelling a new or an edited sketch (extrude and revolve) left the toolbar on the Sketch tab, now empty, until you clicked 3D editor yourself; finishing one switched back as expected. Cancelling now switches back too and closes the stroke panel. Reported by @prmod3d in #212.
- **Every grid line grows with the zoom:** Only the centre cross and the plate border grew as you zoomed in; the other grid lines stayed one pixel wide and faded into a light plate, most of all in the Tinkercad look. Every grid line now also lies on the plate as a narrow band - a twentieth of the grid step, twice that for the darker lines - so it grows as you zoom in, as in Tinkercad, while the pixel line keeps the grid visible from afar. Where lines cross, the colour is laid once, so no darker dots form. Reported by @prmod3d in #143.
- **A design from a newer layerling says so:** A design saved by a newer layerling may hold what an older one does not know - a new shape, a new setting, fonts of one's own. Where that kept it from opening, the reason was a bare technical one such as "unknown shape type"; now it names both versions and says that reloading the page brings the newest one. Where it opens, the notice says it was saved by a newer version and newer things may look different. This helps from this version on; older ones still give the plain reason.
- **Fonts of your own for text:** Under Font, "Your own fonts …" opens a window that takes a TrueType, OpenType or WOFF file, and in Chrome and Edge on a computer also a font installed there (the browser asks once for permission; Firefox, Safari and phones choose the file instead). The font is read once into the typeface format the built-in fonts use, so curved text, edge treatment and STEP work on it; outlines drawn the PostScript way round are turned, and strokes that overlap - script and variable fonts - are merged into one outline. It is kept in this browser. A design stores only the outlines of the letters its texts use, in every undo state - never the font file - so it opens with its lettering on any computer, and letters typed there without the font come from Sans. WOFF2 and font collections are refused with a reason. The guide explains the browsers and what passing a design on means for a font's licence. The MCP bridge has `layerling_add_font` and `layerling_list_fonts`, and `font` takes a font of one's own by id or name.
- **Round gear teeth:** Gears have a third tooth shape, Round: each tooth one arc over the tip and each gap one arc over the root, running smoothly into each other, like Tinkercad's Useful gear. Small printed gears with them are more forgiving and turn more easily than involute ones, and on a knob they make a smooth grip. They are set like involute teeth by number of teeth, module and backlash (no pressure angle). They stand lower - tip 0.6 module above the pitch circle, root 0.85 below - so the outside diameter is module × (teeth + 1.2); the tooth is half a pitch thick on the pitch circle less half the backlash, so round gears with the same module mesh at the usual centre distance. The exact body is made of true arcs, for the edge tool and STEP. The MCP bridge takes `gearProfile: "round"`. Suggested by @luk-saw and @prmod3d in #201.
- **Round knurling:** The knurl has a third pattern, Round: grooves and ridges along the axis as a smooth wave of arcs, the same shape as the round gear teeth, but with the number of grooves, the depth and the end chamfer free - a pleasant grip for a knob. Round grooves stay shallower than 0.45 of the pitch round the grip, or the ridges would bulge at their foot; a greater depth gives way. It is an exact body of true arcs, chamfered ends included. The MCP bridge takes `knurlPattern: "round"`. Suggested by @prmod3d in #201.

## 1.57.0

- **Tinkercad look, and darker grid lines your way:** Settings → Appearance has a "Tinkercad look" button that sets the work area as Tinkercad shows it - white ground, a pale blue millimetre grid with a darker line every centimetre, black edge lines, light from above with soft shadows - with the values @prmod3d measured; everything stays adjustable. Under Workplane the darker grid line now comes every 5 or every 10 grid steps and can have a colour of its own, which the centre cross and the plate border take too. The centre cross and the border are drawn as narrow bands on the plate, so they are stronger and grow as you zoom in. Suggested by @prmod3d in #143.
- **Typed colour values are kept:** In the colour fields of the settings a typed hex value only counted once the field lost focus, and a click outside closed the picker before that - the value was lost. Enter now takes it, and closing the picker keeps it. Reported by @prmod3d in #143.
- **The sketch's area under the grid has a colour setting, and the colours clearer names:** In sketch mode the sheet under the grid kept its yellowish colour whatever was set - "Sketch background" only coloured the window round it, and "Sketch like work area" could not reach it either. It has a setting of its own now, "Sketch: area under the grid", which "Sketch like work area" and the Tinkercad look set too. And the colour settings say which area they mean: "Window background" and "Workplane under the grid", likewise for the sketch. Reported by @prmod3d in #143.
- **Loft: twist and tilt:** The loft can now turn its section evenly about the vertical on the way up (up to 360° either way), so the sides wind round, and tilt its top end about both horizontal axes (up to 45°), the sections in between tilting by their share. The highest point stays at the set height, and a tilt too steep for the height gives way. It stays an exact body - built through sections a few degrees apart, like a twisted extrusion - so edges can be filleted and STEP keeps it. The MCP bridge takes `loftTwist`, `loftTiltX` and `loftTiltZ`. Suggested by @prmod3d in #205.
- **Point or comma in the section position:** The section view's position field, and the radius of a corner fillet or chamfer in a sketch, were number fields, which read the decimal mark by the browser's language - a German browser refused "12.5". They are text fields now, read like every other measure: point or comma, fractions and sums. Reported by @AkiraNorthstar in #210.
- **Chrome no longer takes the start page for German:** The hidden copy of the welcome guide that search engines and link previews read held its German half in plain text; marked not to be translated, it still counted when Chrome guessed the page's language, and Chrome set to always translate German translated the page on every load. The German half now sits in `<noscript>`, which programs without JavaScript read as text and a browser never draws. Reported by @gogades in #191.
- **Capsule with one smaller end:** The capsule can now be narrower at one end, a belt guard round two pulleys of different size: under Properties "Small End (Diameter)" sets the small end, and "Centre Distance" how far apart the two arcs' centres are. The large end keeps the capsule's width, the sides run as tangents to both arcs, and changing the small end keeps the centre distance. It stays an exact body, so edges can be filleted and STEP keeps it. The MCP bridge takes `slotEndRatio`. Suggested by @nz-pilgrim in #206.
- **An SVG comes in as a sketch, with Tinkercad's fill modes:** An imported SVG was a fixed mesh. It now comes in as a sketch body: its outlines become sketch lines and Bézier curves - quadratic curves and arcs turned into cubic ones - so the body is exact, edges can be filleted, STEP keeps the curves, and the points can be edited in sketch mode. Every extruded sketch body, an SVG among them, has its fill in the properties: Area, Stroke Outside, Stroke Inside or Stroke Centred, with the line width and the corners, and Silhouette (No Holes) leaves out every outline lying inside another. Silhouette with an outside stroke is a cookie cutter from one SVG. The body is built again where it stands; the Silhouette switch is in the sketch's Stroke panel too. An SVG with more than 2500 lines and curves, or one the CAD kernel builds differently from the drawing, still comes in as a mesh. The MCP bridge sets the fill with `stroke` and `silhouette` on `layerling_update_object`. Suggested by @AkiraNorthstar in #197.
- **SVG import and export read the right way round:** An imported SVG lay mirrored top to bottom on the plate - lettering read upside down and backwards from above - and the SVG export of the top view was mirrored the same way, so a design exported and imported again looked right. Both now read as the top view shows the plate, the drawing's top at the back. An SVG in a design saved before keeps lying as it did.

## 1.56.2

- **Fillet and chamfer: Apply no longer hangs after the size field:** Clicking into the size field and leaving it without a change - a click on its label is enough - threw the preview away as if the size had changed, but never computed a new one, so Apply kept spinning until the sharp-edge threshold was moved. Leaving a field unchanged now changes nothing. Reported by @trabtrading-design in #204.

## 1.56.1

- **Loose holes are cut on export:** A hole left ungrouped was dropped from an STL, 3MF, OBJ or SVG export, though the view shades the cut and the STEP export already made it, so a part had to be grouped before every export. Now every visible hole that goes along is taken out of the bodies it reaches, as grouping would cut it; with a selection, only the selected holes count. The material estimate, the section SVG and the MCP bridge's `layerling_export_section_svg` count them the same way. Reported by @mobiusfix in #203.

## 1.56.0

- **No moiré under a workplane on the plate:** A workplane put on the empty plate lies in the plate's own plane, and the two grids, slightly offset, made a flickering moiré. While a workplane lies flat on the plate, the plate now shows only its outline; a tilted or raised workplane keeps the plate's grid. Reported by @prmod3d in #178.
- **Corners and edges of the view cube:** A click on a corner of the view cube now looks at the model diagonally from that corner, and a click near an edge looks from that edge, between two sides; the spot lights up under the pointer. Dragging the cube and the keys 1-6 work as before. The MCP bridge's `layerling_capture_image` takes the same views as faces joined with "-", like `front-right-top`. Suggested by @hlmodtech in #202.
- **Gears with involute teeth:** New gears have involute teeth that mesh, set like a gear generator: number of teeth and module, which give the size (outside diameter module x (teeth + 2)), plus pressure angle (20° by default) and backlash for printing (0.2 mm per pair by default). Select two gears and the properties panel gives the centre distance they mesh at and how far apart they stand now, or says their modules differ. A helical gear's angle is then the true helix angle, so a pair meshes with the same angle, one positive and one negative; the bevel gear takes the same teeth, tapered, as an approximation. The exact body draws the flanks as smooth curves, so edges can be filleted. Older designs keep their straight teeth, now called "Simple", until the tooth shape is switched; the shape defaults in the settings take the tooth shape too. The MCP bridge takes `gearProfile`, `gearPressureAngle` and `gearBacklash`. Suggested by @prmod3d in #201.

## 1.55.2

- **The design menu stays in front in the list view:** On the start page's list view, a design's Options menu slipped behind the next row while the pointer was on its own card. Hovering lifts a card with a transform, which made it a stacking context of its own, so its menu only counted inside it; the card with an open menu now stands in front. Reported by @ucito in #200.
- **"New since your last visit" lists every version since then:** The card on the start page opened only the newest version and kept the others behind a button, so under its title "since your last visit" it read as if one update was all that came. It now lists every version since the last visit, newest first, and scrolls inside the card after a long absence; a button below adds the versions from before the last visit.

## 1.55.1

- **Smooth curves no longer loop at every point:** The handles of a smooth curve - drawn with Smooth curve or given by the Smooth button on a point - pointed the wrong way, so the curve made a loop at each point. They were laid out the way the path happened to be walked, which mostly started at the last point drawn, while a curve is drawn the way its segments are stored. Reported by @luk-saw in #199.
- **SVG import keeps holes again:** A hole inside an outline - a round cut-out drawn in Inkscape, for one - came in filled, as a body of its own. Inkscape closes an outline of arcs a fraction of a micrometre short of its start, and that sliver made the import believe the hole crossed the outline. Reported by @AkiraNorthstar in #197.
- **The Bézier curve shows what you draw:** While a point is dragged out with the Bézier tool, the stretch to it now bends live, exactly as it will once let go. Before, a straight line ran to the pointer - not even to the point being placed - so the bend only showed after letting go and felt the wrong way round. The guide now also says which way to drag. Asked by @hlmodtech in #196.

## 1.55.0

- **Double click opens groups, and shaky clicks no longer move parts:** A double click on a group or a bundle now opens it for editing, as a double click on a sketch body opens its sketch; two switches in the settings turn either off. And a part only starts to move once the pointer has travelled a few pixels, so a slightly shaky click or double click no longer nudges it off the grid. Suggested by @luk-saw and @prmod3d in #150.
- **Set down on faces, like Cruise in Tinkercad:** A new shape on the pointer now lies down on the face of the body under the pointer, sloped faces included, lined up with that face; over the empty plate it still lands on the workplane. And C picks up the selection: it follows the pointer over the faces of other bodies and a click sets it down there (Esc leaves it), its underside on the face, keeping its own turn about its up axis; several parts travel together. The MCP bridge gets the same as `layerling_place_on_face`. Suggested by @danyvanimpe-lab in #195.

## 1.54.0

- **New shape: Loft** ("Übergang" in German): joins one outline at the bottom to another at the top - a circle, an ellipse, a rectangle with rounded corners or a polygon at each end, the top moved sideways if you like. With a wall thickness it is a tube open at both ends, for hose adapters or a square fan onto a round duct. An exact body, so edges can be chamfered and filleted and STEP keeps it. Asked for by @Richard6360 and @mobiusfix in #188.
- **Lines by length and angle:** While drawing a line in a sketch, type a number and two fields open: the length, then "<" or Tab and the angle, as in AutoCAD ("50<30"). Enter sets the point and the next line can be typed at once; an empty angle follows the pointer. The angle now shows next to the length at the pointer, and Shift snaps a new line in 15° steps instead of only horizontally or vertically. Suggested by @AnanasAPod in #194.
- **Click through a narrow slot:** A body seen through a slot or hole narrower than about 3 mm could not be selected - every click picked the body around it. Its edge lines counted as hit within 1.15 mm of the pointer, so the rim always won, however far you zoomed in. Only surfaces pick a body now. Reported by @mobiusfix in #193.
- **The plate labels switch moved to Workplane:** "Plate labels" sat under Appearance in the settings; it now sits under Workplane, right below the printer whose name it shows.

## 1.53.0

- **Top edges for cookie cutters:** The edge panel has a new "Top edges" button that picks only the edges along the very top of a body - build an outline as a stroke, then chamfer its top edges for a cookie cutter. Through MCP, layerling_apply_edge_treatment takes `edgeIds: "top"`. Asked for by @hlmodtech in #154.
- **Plate labels can be switched off:** The design's name on the plate and the printer's name and build volume in its corner can be hidden with "Plate labels" in the settings, for anyone who finds them distracting. Asked for by @jamesjoyce2208 in #192.
- **Sketch strokes free their memory:** Working out a stroked sketch left its intermediate outlines in the geometry library's memory, and the preview works it out again on every edit, so a long session with a stroked sketch kept growing. They are now freed.
- **Align overlay stays light on big meshes:** The Align handles measured the drawn parts point by point on every frame; with a large imported mesh that slowed the view. The measurement is now kept until a part moves or changes.
- **The fill light starts exactly where it was:** With the light at its default direction, the fill light now stands precisely where it always stood, and turns along with the main light from there.
- **Esc closes Align faces and Scale by percent again:** The keyboard handler could still hold the state from before one of these tools opened, so Esc cleared the selection instead of closing the tool; Ctrl+A could likewise miss a body that had just been added.
- **A server project opened twice stays one copy:** Opening a design from the shared folder looked for its existing working copy in a list that could be out of date, and could then make a second one.
- **Touch and pen drags no longer break off:** Starting a drag with a pointer that was already gone made the browser throw and stopped the whole drag handler; now the drag carries on.
- **Security updates:** Next.js 15.5.27 and sharp 0.35.5, within their version ranges.
- **A question mark on every window:** Align faces, the "Sketch to 3D" menu, the visibility menu, the point and line bars in a sketch and the revolve preview now have one too; the shape menus, text, sketch bodies and imported meshes jump straight to their own section of the guide, and a reference point's card opens "Reference points" instead of "Notes".
- **Cleaner code:** About 1,000 lines of code that nothing used any more, unused imports and four unused texts are gone.
- **Docs caught up:** The README, the quick guide and the welcome text now mention the sketch stroke, the light and shadow settings, the draggable pivot, Multicolor in the export and the workplane on corners and edges; the quick guide no longer says that only a closed outline makes a body.

## 1.52.0

- **Align handles where the parts will go:** The handles and guide lines of the Align tool stood at the box round each part's own, turned box, while the parts moved to the box round what is actually drawn. For a turned star, cone or tipped part those differ, so the parts seemed to jump to a different edge than the one shown. The handles now stand where the parts line up. Prompted by @voltage3d's stream in #187.
- **Say when a chamfer runs on along smooth edges:** "Select tangent chains" looked as if switching it off would keep a chamfer or fillet to the edge that was picked, but the CAD kernel always carries it on along edges that continue smoothly. The switch is now called "A click also picks edges that continue smoothly", and the panel says along how many more edges the kernel will carry the treatment. Reported by @RobbieKnobbie in #161.
- **Multicolor groups keep their colours in 3MF and OBJ:** A group showing each part in its own colour was exported in a single colour, so the slicer could not map its parts onto filaments. Every face now carries the colour of the part it belongs to - in 3MF per triangle, in OBJ per point. Asked for by @prmod3d in #153.

## 1.51.0

- **No translation offer on an English page:** Chrome offered to translate layerling on every load and Next.js reported a hydration error, even with everything in English. The page holds a hidden copy of the welcome guide in English and German for search engines; Chrome took the German half for a page to translate and marked <html> while doing so. That hidden text is now kept out of translation, and a mark on <html> no longer causes an error. Reported by @gogades in #191.
- **Drag the pivot with the mouse:** The pivot marker can now be dragged. It slides over the surface of the bodies and snaps to corners, edge midpoints and edges, and where it is let go it is set as usual - kept with a single body, saved and undoable. Asked for by @prmod3d and @rjacobs1969 in #140.
- **Calmer edge lines:** With "Edge lines on all bodies" switched on, every crease from 25° got a near-black line, so a thread or a knurled grip turned into a dark mass and every body had a hard frame. The lines are now drawn at half strength, and a body with a texture of many small faces only keeps its real outline, such as the rims of its ends.
- **Axis letters stay behind bodies:** The X, Y and Z of the axis arrows showed through any body standing in front of them.
- **Stroke for sketches:** A sketch could only become a filled area. The new Stroke button in the sketch's Finish section builds the line itself with a width: a closed outline becomes a frame - its wall centred on the line, inside or outside it, so a 0.2 mm stroke outside a dovetail hole gives the print its tolerance - and an open line becomes a stripe with flat, square or round ends. Corners can be sharp, round or bevelled, the sketch shows the result while drawing, and it keeps the drawn line so line and stroke stay editable. Through MCP, layerling_create_shape takes a stroke for a sketch. Asked for by @jrs94yj and @luk-saw in #154.
- **Softer shadows, light from any direction, more contrast:** Even at full contrast and shadow strength the view stayed paler than Tinkercad, the shadows had hard edges, and the light always came from the front right at about 45°.
